For this reason, then, according to the divine Fathers, we make no diminution whatever of natural wills, nor indeed of operations, just as none of the natures themselves, in the case of the same and one God the Word incarnate; believing in orthodox fashion that the same is in all respects perfect God and man at once, from his both having naturally, and willing and operating, perfectly the divine things and the human, and from his having properly a divine and a human essence and volition and operation at once; lest, by a deficiency in either of the natural idioms, we should unreasonably teach as doctrine the diminution — or rather the perfect non-existence — of each of the natures from which and in which he is.

For that he had a human will according to nature, as also a divine one according to essence, the Word himself plainly shows through the refusal of death, befitting man and by way of economy, which he made for our sake, in which he said: Father, if it be possible, let the cup pass from me; Matth. xxvi, 39. that he might show the weakness of his own flesh, and that he was not known to those who saw him as flesh in phantasm, cheating the sense, but was truly and properly man, the natural volition bearing witness to this, to which the refusal by way of economy belonged.

And that again it had been deified throughout, converging upon the divine will itself, always moved and shaped from it and according to it, is plain from his having perfectly made the ratification of the paternal will alone, according to which he said as man: Not mine, but thy will be done; giving himself to us in this too as a type and example, for the setting-aside of our own will for the sake of the perfect fulfilment of the divine, even if we should see death hanging over us on this account.

Εἰ γὰρ μὴ ὡς ἄνθρωπος φύσει γενό-

81μενος, καὶ φυσικὸν ἤγουν ἀνθρώπινον θέλημα κεκτημένος, τοῦτο τε κατ᾿ οἰκονομίαν ὑποκλίνων, καὶ πρὸς ἕνωσιν τοῦ πατρικοῦ συνελαύνων θελήματος, ἔλεγεν αὐτῷ τῷ Πατρί· Μὴ τὸ ἐμὸν θέλημα γινέσθω, ἀλλὰ τὸ σόν· δῆλον ὡς φύσει Θεὸς τοῦτο ἔλεγε· κἀκ τούτου λοιπὸν ἑαυτὸν ἐδείκνυ, μὴ τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ ἴσον θέλημα τῷ Πατρὶ κεκτημένον, ἀλλ᾿ ἕτερον καὶ κατὰ φύσιν διάφορον· ὃ καὶ ὑποτάσσων, ᾕτει γενέσθαι μόνον τὸ πατρικόν. Καὶ εἰ ἄλλο παρὰ τὸ τοῦ Πατρὸς φυσικὸν ἐκέκτητο θέλημα, δῆλον ὡς καὶ τὴν οὐσίαν εἶχε παρηλλαγμένην. «Μιᾶς γὰρ οὐσίας, ἓν δὴ καὶ τὸ θέλημα,» κατὰ τὸν σοφώτατον Κύριλλον Tom. x, in Joan. Refertur in vi synod., act. 10.. Διαφόρου δὲ τοῦ φυσικοῦ θελήματος ὄντος, διάφορος πάντη τε καὶ πάντως ἡ φύσις. Δυοῖν οὖν θάτερον· ἢ γὰρ ὡς ἄνθρωπος εἶχε θέλημα φυσικὸν, καὶ δι᾿ ἡμᾶς οἰκονομικῶς παρητεῖτο θέλων τὸν θάνατον· καὶ πάλιν ὥρμα κατ᾿ αὐτοῦ διὰ τῆς πρὸς τὸ πατρικὸν θέλημα τελείας συννεύσεως· ἢ μὴ ἔχων ὡς ἄνθρωπος θέλημα φυσικὸν, ὡς Θεὸς φύσει τὰ τοῦ σώματος εἰς ἰδίαν οὐσίαν ἔπασχε πάθη, συστελλομένην φυσικῶς τὸν θάνατον, καὶ ἄλλο κατ᾿ οὐσίαν πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα θέλημα φυσικὸν κεκτημένην· ὃ καὶ μὴ γενέσθαι δι᾿ εὐχῆς ἐζήτει καὶ παρεκάλει. Καὶ τίς Θεὸς θάνατον κατὰ φύοιν σαρκὸς δεδιὼς, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο παρελθεῖν τὸ ποτήριον ἐξαιτούμενος, καὶ ἄλλο παρὰ τὸ πατρικὸν θέλημα φυσικὸν κεκτημένος; Ταύτην οὖν τῶν ἡμετέρων ψυχῶν ἀποσκευαζόμενοι τὴν ἀτοπίαν, τὴν εὐσεβῆ τῶν Πατέρων ὁμολογίαν κατέχωμεν· καὶ ὅταν λέγει· «Πάτερ, εἰ δυνατὸν, παρελθέτω τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο, καθώς φησιν ὁ μέγας Ἀθανάσιος ἐν τῷ περὶ Σαρκώσεως αὐτοῦ καὶ Τριάδος λόγῳ Refertur act. illa 10 sextæ synodi.. πλὴν μὴ τὸ ἐμὸν θέλημα γενέσθω, ἀλλὰ τὸ σόν· τὸ μὲν πνεῦμα πρόθυμον, ἡ δὲ σὰρξ ἀσθενής· νοοῦμεν ὡς δύο θελήματα ἐνταῦθα δείκνυσι· τὸ μὲν ἀνθρώπινον· ὅπερ ἐστὶ τῆς σαρκός· τὸ δὲ θεῖκόν. Τὸ γὰρ ἀνθρώπινον διὰ τὴν ἀσθένειαν τῆς σαρκὸς παραιτεῖται τὸ πάθος· τὸ δὲ θεῖκὸν αὐτοῦ πρόθυμον. Ταύτῃ καὶ ὁ μέγας Θεολόγος Γρηγόριος ἐν τῷ περὶ Υἱοῦ δευτέρῳ λόγῳ σαφῶς ἐκδιδάσκει, λέγων· «Τὸ γὰρ ἐκείνου θέλειν, οὐδὲν ὑπεναντίον, θεωθὲν ὅλον.» Ὥστε θέλειν εἶχεν ἀνθρώπινον, κατὰ τὸν θεῖον τοῦτον διδάσκαλον· οὐ μὴν Θεῷ καθοτιοῦν ὑπεναντίον· ὅτι μὴ γνωμικὸν τοῦτο καθάπαξ, ἀλλὰ φυσικὸν κυρίως ἐτύγχανεν, ὑπὸ τῆς αὐτοῦ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν θεότητος τυπούμενον ἀεὶ καὶ κινούμενον πρὸς τὴν τῆς οἶκονομίας ἐκπλήρωσιν· καὶ ὅλον δι᾿ ὅλου τῇ πρὸς τὸ πατρικὸν συννεύσει τε καὶ συμφυΐᾳ τεθεωμένον, καὶ θεῖον τῇ ἑνώσει κυρίως Marg. Ὅτι ἡ θέωσις τὴν ἕνωσιν σημαίνει. Deificationem, unionem significare., ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τῇ φύσει, καὶ γενόμενον ἀληθῶς καὶ λεγόμενον· μηδαμῶς τῷ θεωθῆναι, τοῦ κατὰ φύσιν ἐκστᾷν.

For if it was not as one who had become man by nature

81and as one possessing a natural, that is a human, will — bending it down by way of economy and driving it together into union with the paternal will — that he said to the Father himself, Not my will be done, but thine, it is plain that he said this as God by nature; and from this, then, he showed himself as not possessing the same will as the Father and an equal one, but another, and different according to nature; and, subjecting it, he asked that the paternal alone be done. And if he possessed a natural will other than the Father's, it is plain that he had also an essence varied from his. «For of one essence there is indeed also one will», according to the most wise Cyril. And the natural will being different, the nature is different in every way and altogether.

One of two things, then: either as man he had a natural will, and for our sake, by way of economy, refused death, willingly, and again rushed against it through his perfect convergence with the paternal will; or, not having as man a natural will, he suffered as God by nature the passions of the body in his own essence — an essence contracting naturally before death and possessing a natural will other, according to essence, than the Father's; which also he sought and besought in prayer should not be done. And what God fears death after the nature of flesh, and on this account begs that the cup pass, and possesses a natural will other than the paternal?

Ridding our souls, then, of this absurdity, let us hold fast the pious confession of the Fathers; and when he says, «Father, if it be possible, let this cup pass», as the great Athanasius says in his discourse On his Incarnation and on the Trinity, «nevertheless not my will be done, but thine; the spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak, we understand that he shows here two wills: the one human, which is of the flesh, the other divine. For the human, because of the weakness of the flesh, refuses the passion; but his divine will is willing.» In this way also the great Gregory the Theologian, in the second discourse On the Son, plainly teaches, saying: «For his willing is in nothing contrary, being wholly deified.» So he had a human willing, according to this divine teacher, yet in no way contrary to God; because this was not at all of inclination, but was properly natural, always shaped and moved by his Godhead according to essence towards the fulfilment of the economy; and wholly, throughout, deified by its convergence and growing-together with the paternal will, and properly divine by the union, but not by nature, both truly become so and called so; in no way, by being deified, departing from what is according to nature.

Τῷ γοῦν θεωθὲν ὅλον εἰπεῖν, τὴν τοῦ κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἀνθρωπίνου θελήματος πρὸς τὸ θεῖον αὐτοῦ καὶ πατρικὸν 84ἕνωσιν ὁ διδάσκαλος παραστήσας, πᾶσαν ἐναντίωσιν, καὶ τοὺς τἀναντία θέλοντας ἐκ τοῦ κατὰ Χριστὸν μυστηρίου τελείως ἀπήλασε· τῷ δὲ φάσκειν, «Τὸ γὰρ ἐκείνου θέλειν,» τήν τε τοῦ κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἀνθρωπίνου θελήματος ἔμφυτον κίνησιν, καὶ τὴν πρὸς τὸ θεῖον αὐτοῦ καὶ πατρικὸν οὐσιώδη καὶ φυσικὴν διαφορὰν ἐπιδεικνύμενος, τὴν σύγχυσιν μετὰ τῆς φαντασίας ὁλικῶς ἀπεσκεύασε. Ταύτην γὰρ καὶ τὴν ὁμότιμον αὐτῇ ἐπ᾿ ἀσεβείᾳ διαίρεσιν πολεμούσας εἰδότες τὸν τῆς οἰκονομίας λόγον, ὅ τε θεῖος οὗτος Πατὴρ Fr. ἀνήρ, emendatum marg. πατήρ., καὶ οἱ κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἅγιοι τῆς καθολικῆς Ἐκκλησίας διδάσκαλοι, μεγαλοφώνως τήν τε διαφορὰν καὶ τὴν ἕνωσιν καθ᾿ ἑκατέρας αὐτῶν ἐδογμάτισαν· τὴν μὲν, ἐν τῷ φυσικῷ λόγῳ πραγματικῶς καὶ τελείως συντηρουμένην, τὴν δὲ πάλιν, ἐν τῷ οἰκονομικῷ τρόπῳ παγίως καὶ ὑποστατικῶς σωζομένην, εἰς πίστωσιν τῶν ἐν τῷ ἑνὶ καὶ μόνῳ Χριστῷ τῷ Θεῷ καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν τὴν ἀδιάσπαστον οὐσιωδῶς ὄντων πραγμάτων καὶ πάντων τῶν προσόντων αὐτοῖς φυσικῶν. Πάντες γὰρ ὥσπερ ἄλλην καὶ ἄλλην, θείαν φημὶ καὶ ἀνθρωπίνην, καὶ διπλῆν φύσιν ἐπὶ τοῦ αὐτοῦ καὶ ἑνὸς ἐδογμάτισαν· οὕτως διαπρυσίως τοῖς πᾶσιν ἐκήρυξαν καὶ ἄλλο καὶ ἄλλο θέλημα, θεῖόν φημι καὶ ἀνθρώπινον, καὶ δύο θελήματα· καὶ ἄλλην καὶ ἄλλην ἐνέργειαν, θείαν τε καὶ ἀνθρωπίνην· καὶ διπλῆν αὖθις ἐνέργειαν, τουτέστι δύο. Οὕτω γὰρ καὶ τῶν οὐσιῶν τὴν ἐν ἀριθμῷ σημασίαν προδήλως ἐξεφώνησαν. Φαίνεται γὰρ ὁ αὐτὸς ὡς ἄνθρωπος, καὶ τὸ κατὰ φύσιν ὑπάρχων Θεὸς, θέλων οἰκονομικῶς παρελθεῖν τὸ ποτήριον· καὶ μέχρι τούτου τυπῶν τὸ ἡμέτερον, ὡς Κύριλλος ἡμᾶς ὁ σοφὸς ἐξεπαίδευσεν· ἵνα τῆς ἡμῶν φύσεως πᾶσαν θανάτου συστολὴν ἀπελάσῃ, καὶ πρὸς ὁρμὴν ἀνδρικῶς τὴν κατ᾿ αὐτοῦ, φημὶ τοῦ θανάτου, στομώσῃ τε καὶ διεγείρῃ. Φαίνεται δὲ πάλιν ὡς Θεὸς, καίπερ ἄνθρωπος ὢν κατ᾿ οὐσίαν, θέλων τὴν οἰκονομίαν πατρικῶς ἐκπληρῶσαι, καὶ τὴν πάντων ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν ἐργάσασθαι· δείκνυται δὲ καὶ ὡς ἄνθρωπος, φύσει τυγχάνων Θεὸς, ἀνθρωπίνως ἐνεργῶν, καὶ τὴν πεῖραν τῶν παθημάτων δι᾿ ἡμᾶς ἑκουσίως δεχόμενος· δείκνυται δὲ πάλιν ὡς Θεὸς, καὶ τὸ κατὰ φύσιν ἄνθρωπος ὢν, θεϊκῶς ἐνεργῶν, καὶ τὰς θεοσημείας φυσικῶς προβαλλόμενος· ἐξ ὧν ὅτι καὶ Θεὸς ὁμοῦ καὶ ἄνθρωπος ἦν ὁ αὐτὸς ἐγνωρίζετο, ταῖς ἐξ ὧν καὶ ἐν αἷς ὑπῆρχεν οὐσίαις ἰσάριθμα τὰ θελήματα, καὶ τὰς ἐνεργείας φυσικῶς κεκτημένος, εἰς βεβαίωσιν τῆς τῶν ἅπερ κυρίως ἦν τελείας ὑπάρξεως. Εἰ δέ τις πρὸς τὴν τούτων ἀθέτησιν ἀντιφατικῶς, τήν τε τοῦ θεοφάντορος Διονυσίου Epist. 4, ad Caium. Θεανδρικὴν ἐνέργειαν, καὶ τὴν τοῦ σοφωτάτου Κυρίλλου συγγενῆ καὶ δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεδειγμένην εἰς μέσον τῷ λόγῳ προἴσχεται, γινωσκέτω μηδεμίαν ἐντεῦθεν ἐρανισόμενος ἑαυτῷ κατὰ τῆς εὐσεβοῦς ὁμολογίας ἰσχύν.

By saying, then, «wholly deified», the teacher, having presented the union of his human will with his divine and paternal 84will, drove entirely out of the mystery of Christ all opposition, and those who will contrary things; and by saying «For his willing», showing both the implanted movement of his human will and its essential and natural difference from his divine and paternal will, he removed confusion together with phantasm entirely. For knowing that this, and the division equal to it in impiety, make war on the principle of the economy, both this divine Father and the holy teachers of the catholic Church who are like him taught as doctrine, with a loud voice, both the difference and the union against each of them: the one as preserved really and perfectly in the natural principle, the other again as kept firmly and hypostatically in the mode of the economy, for the confirmation of the realities that are essentially in the one and only Christ God by the indissoluble union, and of all the natural things that belong to them.

For all of them, just as they taught as doctrine one nature and another — divine, I mean, and human — and a twofold nature in the case of the same and one, so they proclaimed clearly to all one will and another — divine, I mean, and human — and two wills; and one operation and another, divine and human; and again a twofold operation, that is, two. For so too they plainly gave voice to the signification of the essences in number. For the same appears as man, though being according to nature God, willing by way of economy that the cup pass, and up to this point typifying what is ours, as the wise Cyril has taught us; that he might drive from our nature every contraction before death, and steel and rouse it manfully to impulse against it — against death, I mean. And he appears again as God, although being man according to essence, willing to fulfil the economy paternally and to work the salvation of us all; and he is shown also as man, being God by nature, operating humanly and voluntarily accepting the experience of the sufferings for our sake; and he is shown again as God, though being according to nature man, operating divinely and putting forth naturally the divine signs; from which it was known that the same was both God and man at once, possessing naturally wills and operations equal in number to the essences from which and in which he was, for the confirmation of the perfect existence of the things which he properly was.

But if anyone, to set these aside, puts forward in contradiction into the midst of the argument the theandric operation of Dionysius the revealer of God, and the kindred operation, shown forth through both, of the most wise Cyril, let him know that he will gather from this no strength for himself against the pious confession.

Ἡ μὲν γὰρ Θεανδρικὴ προσφόρῳ φωνῇ πρὸς τοῦ διδασκάλου φρασθεῖσα, διπλῆν τοῦ διπλοῦ τὴν φύσιν ἐνέργειαν 85περιφραστικῶς δηλονότισημαίνει. Θείαν γὰρ καὶ ἀνδρικὴν, ἤγουν ἀνθρωπίνην, διὰ τῆς κατὰ τὴν ἐκφορὰν συναφείας προδήλως ἐκήρυξεν· ἀλλ᾿ οὐχ ἁπλῶς· ὅθεν οὐδὲ δι᾿ ἀριθμοῦ ταύτην ὠνόμασεν Non enim dixit μίαν τινά, uti Cyrus mutavit, unam quamdam, sed καινήν τινα, novam., εἰ καὶ μοναδικῶς τὴν τῶν φυσικῶν ἐνεργειῶν ἕνωσιν ἐπισημαίνων, ταύτην ἐδίδαξεν· ἥτις οὐ λυμαίνεται τῇ φυσικῇ τούτων διαφορᾷ· καθάπερ οὐδ᾿ αὐτῇ τῇ τῶν οὐσιῶν, ἡ τούτων ἀδιάσπαστος ἕνωσις· ἐπειδὴ κατὰ τοὺς λέγοντας, Εἰ μίαν εἶχεν ἐνέργειαν, καὶ ταύτην Θεανδρικήν· εἰ μὲν φυσικὴν μίαν ὁ σαρκωθεὶς Λόγος ἐκέκτητο ταύτην, πρὸς δύο ἡ μία κατὰ φύσιν ἐνέργεια, πῶς μερισθήσεται; καὶ ποίῳ λόγῳ τὰς διαφόρους οὐσίας ἡ μία κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ συστήσει καὶ ἀφορίσει ἐνέργεια; πρός τε τὰ ὁμογενῆ τὴν κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ἀπαραλλαξίαν ἔχειν ἑκατέραν ποιοῦσα, καὶ πρὸς τὰ ἑτερογενῆ πάλιν τὴν κατ᾿ οὐσίαν διαφοράν. Καὶ παρῶ λέγειν, ὡς τῆς πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα καὶ ἡμᾶς φυσικῆς συγγενείας ἐκπέπτωκεν, ὡς μήτε παντελῶς ἐκείνου, μήτε ἡμῶν Θεανδρικὴ τὴν ἔμφυτον καὶ οὐσιώδη κεκτημένων ἐνέργειαν. Εἰ δέ γε πάλιν ὑποστατικὴν, πρὸς τῇ καινῇ δόξῃ· τίς γὰρ ὁ λέγων ὑποστατικὴν ὑπάρχειν ἐνέργειαν; Καὶ οὕτως ἀλλότριον τοῦ Πατρὸς κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν αὐτὸν ὁ τοιοῦτος ἀποφαίνει λόγος, εἴπερ ὑποστατικὴν, καὶ οὐ φυσικὴν ἔχει παρὰ τὸν Πατέρα ἐνέργειαν. Τοῖς γὰρ ὑποστατικοῖς ἰδιώμασι, τὴν πρὸς αὐτὸ κέκτηται διαφορὰν προδήλως ὁ Λόγος. Καὶ«ἡ συγγενὴς δὲ καὶ δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεδειγμένη μία» πάλιν «ἐνέργεια,» κατὰ τὸν ἀοίδιμον Κύριλλον, οὐκ ἐπ᾿ ἀναιρέσει τῆς τῶν κατὰ φύσιν ἐνεργειῶν οὐσιώδους διαφορᾶς· τῶν ἐξ ὧν καὶ ἐν αἷς ὑπάρχει ὁ εἷς καὶ μόνος Χριστὸς ὁ Θεός· ἀλλ᾿ ἐπὶ συστάσει τῆς ἄκρας τούτων ἑνώσεως εἴρηται τῷ διδασκάλῳ. Καὶ ἔοικέ πως δι᾿ ἄλλων φωνῶν, τὸν θεοφάντορα Διονύσιον ἐκμιμεῖσθαι. Ἵνα γὰρ δόξῃ μὴ γυμνὸν ὄντα τοῦτον Θεὸν, μήτε ψιλὸν αὖθις ἄνθρωπον· μήτε ἰδίᾳ Θεὸν ἀσωμάτως ἐνεργοῦντα, μήτε ἰδίᾳ ἄνθρωπον τὰ κατὰ γνώμην πράττοντα· ἀλλὰ Θεὸν σεσαρκωμένον καὶ τελείως δι᾿ ἡμᾶς ἐνανθρωπήσαντα, θεικῶς ἅμα καὶ ἀνθρωπικῶς τὸν αὐτὸν ἐνεργοῦντα, ἔφησεν, ὡς οὐ λόγῳ μόνον, καὶ τοῖς θεοπρεπέσιν ἐπιτάγμασιν, ἐνεργὸς εὑρίσκεται ὁΣωτὴρ ἐν τῷ τοὺς νεκροὺς διανιστᾷν καὶ θεραπεύειν πᾶσαν νόσον καὶ πᾶσαν μαλακίαν· ἀλλὰ συνεργάτιν ὥσπερ τινὰ πρὸς τοῦτο δὴ μάλιστα τὴν ἁγίαν αὐτοῦ παραλαμβάνειν ἠπείγετο σάρκα· τοῦτο ποιῶν μὲν ὡς Θεὸς τῷ παντουργῷ αὐτοῦ προστάγματι· ζωοποιῶν δὲ αὖ πάλιν καὶ τῇ ἁφῇ τῆς ἡνωμένης αὐτῷ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἁγίας σαρκὸς, ἵνα δείξῃ καὶ ταύτην ζωοποιεῖν δυναμένην διὰ τῆς οὐσιώδους αὐτοῦ ἐνεργείας, ἧς ἐστι κυρίως ἡ ἁφὴ, ἡ φωνὴ, καὶ ὅσα τοιαῦτα.

For the theandric, uttered by the teacher with a fitting word, signifies 85periphrastically, to be sure, the twofold operation of him who is twofold in nature. For he plainly proclaimed a divine and a manly — that is, a human — operation by the conjunction in the utterance; but not simply; whence he did not name it by number either, even if he taught it in the singular, marking the union of the natural operations; a union which does not harm their natural difference, just as the indissoluble union of the essences does not harm the difference of the essences itself; since, according to those who say, If he had one operation, and this theandric — if the incarnate Word possessed this as one natural operation, how will the one operation according to nature be partitioned into two? And by what principle will the one operation, in the same respect, constitute and mark off the different essences, making each have invariance according to essence towards things of the same genus, and again difference according to essence towards things of another genus? And I pass over saying that he has fallen from natural kindredness with the Father and with us, since neither he at all, nor we, possess the implanted and essential operation as theandric. But if again it is hypostatic — besides the novelty of the opinion (for who is it that says an operation is hypostatic?) — then too such an account declares him alien from the Father in operation, if he has an operation hypostatic and not natural, other than the Father's. For it is by the hypostatic idioms that the Word plainly possesses his difference from him.

And «the kindred and one operation shown forth through both», again, according to the celebrated Cyril, was said by the teacher not for the destruction of the essential difference of the operations according to nature — of the natures from which and in which the one and only Christ God is — but for the establishing of their utmost union. And he seems in some way, through other words, to imitate Dionysius the revealer of God. For, that he might seem to be not bare God, nor again mere man; neither God operating by himself bodilessly, nor man by himself doing the things according to inclination; but God incarnate and perfectly become man for our sake, the same operating divinely and humanly at once, he said that not by word alone and by commands befitting God is the Saviour found operative in raising the dead and healing every disease and every infirmity, but he hastened to take his holy flesh as a kind of co-worker for this especially: doing this, on the one hand, as God by his all-working command, and again giving life also by the touch of the holy flesh united to him according to hypostasis, to show that this too is able to give life through his essential operation, to which properly belong the touch, the voice, and all such things.

Εἶτα, ὡς ἔδειξε τὰς φυσικὰς ἐνεργείας αὐτοῦ, τοῦ ἐξ ἀμφοῖν συγκειμένου Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ, σωζομένας τελείως· τὴν τῆς θεότητος αὐτοῦ, διὰ τοῦ παντουργοῦ 88προστάγματος, τὴν δὲ τῆς ἀνθρωπότητος αὐτου, διὰ τῆς ἁφῆς· παρέστησε ταύτας ἡνωμένας διόλου τῇ πρὸς ἀλλήλας συμφυΐᾳ καὶ περιχωρήσει· ὡς μίαν διὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν αὐτοῦ τε τοῦ Λόγου καὶ τῆς παναγίας αὐτοῦ σαρκὸς δείκνυσθαι τὴν ἐνέργειαν· οὐ φυσικὴν, οὐχ ὑποστατικήν· οὐ γάρ τι τοιοῦτον εἴρηκεν ὁ διδάσκαλος· ἀλλὰ συγγενῆ τοῖς μέρεσι· δι᾿ ὧν, ὡς εἴρηται, κατά τε πρόσταγμα παντουργὸν, κατά τε ἁφὴν χειρὸς ἐπεδείκνυτο. Δέδεικται τοίνυν πρὸς αὐτῆς τῆς ἱερᾶς τοῦ σοφοῦ διδασκαλίας, ὡς καὶ τὴν διαφορὰν τῶν φυσικῶν ἐνεργειῶν, ὥσπερ οὖν καὐτῶν τῶν φύσεων ὧν καὶ εἰσὶ, διαγορεύει φυλαττομένην καὶ μετὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν· καὐτὴν τὴν ἕνωσιν αὖθις ὁρίζεται· τὴν μὲν, ἐν τῷ λέγειν, πρόσταγμα, παντουργὸν καὶ ἁφήν· τὴν δὲ, τῷ λέγειν, μίαν τε καὶ συγγενῆ· δι᾿ ὧν πᾶσαν συναἱρεσίν τε καὶ διαίρεσιν τοῦ κατὰ Χριστὸν μυστηρίου μεγαλοφυῶς ἀποπέμπεται· οἱ δὲ μὴ οὕτω λαμβάνοντες, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς μίαν Λόγου τε καὶ σαρκὸς τὴν συγγενῆ νοοῦντες ἐνέργειαν, τὴν Εὐτυχοῦς καὶ Ἀπολιναρίου συνουσίωσιν δογματίζουσι. Δεῖ τοιγαροῦν ἐν πᾶσι καὶ τὸν λόγον ἀλώβητον διασώζειν, καὶ τὸν τῆς οἰκονομίας τρόπον διατηρεῖν ἀπαράφθαρτον· ἵνα μηδεμίαν ἡ πονηρὰ ξυνωρὶς, ἡ διαίρεσις, φημὶ, καὶ ἡ σύγχυσις, κατὰ τῆς ἀληθείας σχοίη παρείσδυσιν. Κατὰ ταύτην οὖν, καὶ τὰς ἄλλας, εἴπερ εὑρεθεῖεν, μοναδικὰς τῶν ἁγίων Πατέρων φωνὰς, τὴν εὐσεβῆ διάνοιαν ἐκληπτέον· καὶ μηδαμῶς εἰς ἀντίφασιν ταύτας, πρὸς τὰς τῶν ἁγίων δυϊκὰς ἐκδεξώμεθα· γινώσκοντες, ὡς καὶ αὗται πρὸς τὴν διαφορὰν κατὰ τῆς συγχύσεως ἰσχυραὶ, κἀκεῖναι πρὸς τὴν ἕνωσιν κατὰ διαιρέσεως ἀσφαλεῖς· ἀλλ᾿ ἄμφω, καὶ ταύτας κἀκείνας, ψυχῇ τε καὶ στόματι περιχαρῶς ἀσπασώμεθα, καὶ ὀρθοδόξως ὁμολογήσωμεν· καὶ διὰ τῶν κατὰ προφορὰν δοκούντων πως ἐναντίων φωνῶν, τοὺς κατὰ διάνοιαν ἐναντίους ἐπίσης ἑαυτοῖς καὶ ἀλλήλοις, καὶ τῇ ἀληθείᾳ, σοφῶς τροπωσώμεθα· καὶ τῆς ἡμετέρας αὐλῆς, ἤγουν τῆς καθολικῆς τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ ἀποστολικῆς Ἐκκλησίας, ἀνδρικῶς ἐξελάσωμεν· καὶ μήθ᾿ ὅλως τὴν οἱανοῦν πάροδον κατὰ τῆς ὀρθοδόξου πίστεως, τοῖς ληστρικῶς ὅρια πατρικὰ μηχανωμένοις μεταίρειν, παράσχωμεν, τῷ διαῤῥίπτειν τι τῶν εὐσεβῶν ὅπλων τε καὶ δογμάτων, δι᾿ ὧν γίνεσθαι πέφυκεν ἡ ἐκείνων καταστροφὴ καὶ ἀναίρεσις.

Then, when he had shown that the natural operations of him — of Christ God composed of both — are preserved perfectly, that of his Godhead through the all-working 88command, and that of his manhood through the touch, he presented them as united throughout by their growing-together and interpenetration with each other; so that the operation of the Word himself and of his all-holy flesh is shown to be one through the union: not natural, not hypostatic — for the teacher said no such thing — but kindred to the parts; through which, as has been said, it was shown forth both by all-working command and by touch of hand.

It has been shown, then, from the sacred teaching of the wise man itself, that he declares the difference of the natural operations, as also of the natures themselves whose they are, to be kept even after the union, and again defines the union itself: the one in saying command, all-working and touch; the other in saying one and kindred; by which he magnificently sends away from the mystery of Christ all coalescence and division. But those who do not take it so, but understand the kindred operation as one of the Word and of the flesh, teach as doctrine the co-essentiation of Eutyches and Apollinarius. One must therefore in all things preserve the principle unharmed and keep the mode of the economy uncorrupted, so that the wicked pair — division, I mean, and confusion — may have no entry against the truth.

In accordance with this, then, the pious understanding must be taken of the other singular expressions of the holy Fathers too, if any should be found; and let us in no way receive them as in contradiction to the dual ones of the saints; knowing that these are strong for the difference against confusion, and those secure for the union against division; but let us embrace both, these and those, joyfully with soul and mouth, and confess them in orthodox fashion; and through the expressions that seem somehow contrary in utterance, let us wisely rout those who are contrary in understanding alike to themselves and to one another and to the truth, and manfully drive them out of our fold, that is, the catholic and apostolic Church of God; and let us not give at all any passage whatever against the orthodox faith to those who scheme, like robbers, to remove the paternal boundaries, Prov. xxii, 28. by throwing away any of the pious weapons and doctrines through which their overthrow and destruction is wont to come about.

Πλὴν κἀκεῖνο γινώσκειν χρεὼν, ὡς ἐπί τε φύσεων καὶ φυσικῶν ἐνεργειῶν, καὶ μοναδικὰς πρὸς ταῖς δυϊκαῖς φωναῖς τῶν ἁγίων Πατέρων εὑρίσκομεν· ὡς, «τὴν μίαν φύσιν τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγου σεσαρκωμένην Cyrill. Epist. 2 ad Successum.,» καὶ, «τὴν Θεανδρικὴν ἐνέργειαν,» καὶ, «τὴν συγγενῆ καὶ δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεδειγμένην·» ἐπὶ δὲ φυσικῶν θελημάτων, οὐδεμίαν μοναδικὴν, ὡς ἐμὲ γινώσκειν, τὸ παράπαν ἐκφώνησιν εὑρεῖν δυνατὸν, 89ἀλλὰ τὰς ἐν ἀντωνυμίαις καὶ ἀριθμῷ δυῖκὰς σημασιας.Πῶς οὖν καὶ τίνι λόγῳ, κἂν προσεξετάσαι δεχώμεθα εξετάσαιδεχώμαθα παντελῶς, ἓν ἢ δύο θελήματα χρὴ λέγειν ἐπὶ Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ, τῆς τῶν Πατέρων νομοθεσίας καὶ διδασκαλίας δύο φυσικὰ θελήματα παρακελευομένης ὁμολογεῖν ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῦ καὶ πρεσβεύειν, καθὰ καὶ φύσεις αὐτὰς, καὶ τὰς τούτων φυσικὰς ἐνεργείας, μέχρι τοῦ γινώσκειν τὴν διαφοράν; Τούτων μοι παρεκβατικῶς νῦν λελεγμένων πρὸς τὴν σὴν θεοτίμητον ἁγιστείαν, παρακαλῶ τὴν ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῖς, ἢ μὴ προσηκόντως ῥηθεῖσιν, ἢ μὴ νοηθεῖσι καλῶς, συγγνώμην ὁμοῦ καὶ διόρθωσίν μοι χαρίσασθαι, φιλαγάθῳ πατρικῆς εὐσπλαχνίας στοργῇ καὶ μιμήσει· καὶ Χριστῷ τῷ Θεῷ με παραθέσθαι, τῷ σὺν Πατρὶ καὶ Πνεύματι ἁγίῳ δοξαζομένῳ εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας τῶν αἰώνων. Ἀμήν.

αʹ. Εr. Ἄτοπον, φησὶ, τὸ ἓν θέλημα μερίζεσθαι. Τοῦτο γὰρ ἀναγκάζει ἡ φυσικὴ συγγένεια· καὶ τὸ μὲν ἥμισυ τῇ μιᾷ, τὸ δὲ λοιπὸν τῇ λοιπῇ φύσει δίδοσθαι· καὶ οὕτως ἀλλοτριοῦν τὸν Χριστὸν τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ ἡμῶν, ὡς οὐκ ἐχόντων σύνθετον θέλημα.

βʹ. Εr. Ὅτι τὴν διαφορὰν, καὶ τὴν ἕνωσιν οἱ Πατέρες κατὰ τῆς συγχύσεως, καὶ τῆς ὁμοτίμου αὐτῇ διαιρέσεως ἐδογμάτισαν.

γʹ. Εr. Ὁ Νεῖλος. Ἔστιν ὑποστατικὴ, ἀλλ᾿ ἐφ᾿ ἡμῶν ἡ παράλογος, δι᾿ ἣν ἄνθρωπος ὁ Θεὸς, ἵν᾿ ἐανασώσῃ τὸν ἄνθρωπον· ἀπαλλάξας ταύτης, ὅσοι φόβῳ θανάτου διὰ παντὸς τοῦ ζῇν ἔνοχοι ἦσαν δουλείας· ἤγουν ὅσοι φοβούμενοι τὴν ὀδύνην, τὴν ἡδονὴν περιεποιοῦντο, τὴν μητέρα τῆς ὀδύνης· τὴν τῆς φθορᾶς ἀρχηγόν· τὴν τοῦ θανάτου προαγωγὸν καὶ προμνήστριαν.

ἼΣΟΝ Ἐπιστολῆς γενομένης πρὸς τὸν ἁγιώτατον ἐπίσκοπον κύριον Νίκανδρον, παρὰ τοῦ ἐν ἁγίοις Μαξίμου, περὶ τῶν δύο ἐν Χριστῷ ἐνεργειῶν.

But this too one must know: that in the case of natures and natural operations we find singular expressions of the holy Fathers besides the dual ones, as «the one incarnate nature of God the Word», and «the theandric operation», and «the kindred one, shown forth through both»; but in the case of natural wills it is not 89possible, so far as I know, to find any singular utterance at all, but only the dual significations, in pronouns and in number. How then, and by what reason, should we even consent to examine at all whether one or two wills must be said in the case of Christ God, when the legislation and teaching of the Fathers bids us confess and uphold two natural wills in him, as also the natures themselves and their natural operations, so far as to recognize the difference?

These things having now been said by me by way of digression to thy God-honoured holiness, I beseech thee to grant me pardon and correction together for them, whether said unfittingly or not well understood, with the good-loving affection and imitation of fatherly compassion; and to commend me to Christ God, who with the Father and the Holy Spirit is glorified unto the ages of ages. Amen.

SCHOLIA

1. Explanation. It is absurd, he says, that the one will be partitioned. For the natural kindredness compels this, and that the half be given to the one nature and the rest to the other; and in this way it compels making Christ alien from God and from us, since neither has a composite will.

2. Explanation. That the Fathers taught as doctrine the difference and the union against confusion and the division equal to it.

3. Explanation. Nilus. There is a hypostatic operation, but in us: the one contrary to reason, on account of which God became man, that he might save man; freeing from it all those who through fear of death were all their lifetime subject to bondage; Hebr. ii, 15. that is, all who, fearing pain, procured for themselves pleasure, the mother of pain, the originator of corruption, the procuress and matchmaker of death.

Αὐτὸς, θεοτίμητε καὶ παμμακάριε Πάτερ, φερωνύμως ἔχων μετὰ τῆς ἀνδρείας, τὴν κατὰ πάντων ἀφανῶς, ἢ φανερῶς τὴν ἁγίαν τοῦ Θεοῦ καθολικὴν καὶ ἀποστολικὴν Ἐκκλησίαν πολεμούντων ἐχθρῶν ἱερωτάτην νίκην Scita allusio ad etymon nominis Νίκανδρος, fortis victor.· καὶ τὸν ὑπὲρ αὐτοῦ ἀειθαλῆ τοῖς χαρίσμασι, καὶ ταῖς ἐνεργείαις τοῦ Πνεύματος ἀμαράντινον στέφανον, ἤδη κατ᾿ ἐλπίδας λαβὼν παρ᾿ αὐτοῦ, καὶ ὡς ἐν χερσὶ κτησάμενος τοῦ κατὰ φύσιν ἐφετοῦ, διὰ τῆς πρὸς αὐτὸ τελείας συννεύσεως τὴν μακαρίαν ἀπόλαυσιν· κἀκ τούτου σαφῶς, εἰ καί τις 92ἄλλος, αὐτοῦ τοῦ Κυρίου κατατρυφῶν, πῶς ποτε, καὶ τίνι τρόπῳ ταύτην προσάπτειν, κἂν λόγῳ ψιλῷ, μὴ τί γε διὰ τιμίων σου καὶ μακαρίων προσάγειν καταδέχῃ γραμμάτων τῷ ἀδρανεῖ καὶ δειλῷ, καὶ μηδόλως εἰς παράταξιν Θεοῦ, καὶ τὸν αὐτοῦ καὶ ὑπὲρ αὐτοῦ, ἅγιον πόλεμον, τὰς τοῦ νοῦ πρακτικὰς δυνάμεις, ἢ τοὺς κατὰ ψυχὴν ὡσεί τινας δακτύλους χειρῶν, λογισμοὺς, μέχρι καὶ νῦν δεδιδαγμένῳ κινεῖν, καὶ ταύτῃ τροποῦσθαι γενναίως τοὺς κατέναντι Κυρίου παντοκράτορος ῥήμασί τε καὶ δόγμασιν ἐπαιρομένους· καὶ τῆς ἀγαθῆς γῆς ἐξελαύνειν, ἥτις ἐστὶν αὐτὸς ὁ Κύριος ἡμῶν καὶ Θεὸς, καὶ ἡ ταυτὸν ὀρθόδοξος πίστις· ἡ ὄντως παγία τῶν εὐσεβῶν δογμάτων ἕδρα καὶ σύστασις· καὶ τῶν ἀρετῶν ἡ ἀένναος καρπογονία καὶ φύσις.

OPUSCULUM 8 — TO NICANDER, ON THE TWO OPERATIONS

Copy of a letter written to the most holy bishop, the lord Nicander, by Maximus, who is among the saints, on the two operations in Christ.

Thou thyself, God-honoured and all-blessed Father, having, as thy name bears, together with manliness, the most sacred victory over all the enemies who secretly or openly make war on the holy catholic and apostolic Church of God; and having already received from him, in hope, the unfading crown for the fight on his behalf, ever-blooming with the gifts of grace and the operations of the Spirit; and having acquired, as in thy hands, through perfect convergence with it, the blessed enjoyment of what is by nature the object of aspiration; and from this plainly, if anyone 92else, delighting in the Lord himself: how ever, and in what manner, dost thou deign to attach this victory, even by a bare word — let alone to bring it through thy precious and blessed letters — to one feeble and cowardly, and not at all taught, even until now, to move for the battle-line of God, and for the holy war that is his and on his behalf, the practical powers of the mind, or the thoughts of the soul, which are like fingers of the hands, Psal. cxliii, 1. and in this way to rout nobly those who exalt themselves against the Lord Almighty with words and doctrines, and to drive them out of the good land, which is our Lord and God himself, and, what is the same, the orthodox faith: the truly firm seat and constitution of pious doctrines, and the ever-flowing fruit-bearing and nature of the virtues?

But this thou hast done in imitation of Christ; so that, having plainly shown his good-loving self-emptying towards me — who by inclination stand apart from him, and by intent dwell far from him in the habit of vice as in a far country, and by operation feed the passions of dishonour like swine Rom. i, 26. — and having shown, by the greatness of thy condescension, the power of the divine achievements that are in thee, thou mightest rouse me to aspiration for it, and steel me without change towards a sacred and blessed zeal, once I have thrown off, as far as I can, the burden of sluggishness and negligence that weighs on me very heavily, beyond all others.

Ἀλλὰ τοῦτο χριστομιμήτως πεποίηκας· ἵνα τὴν τούτου προδήλως εἰς ἐμὲ, τὸν γνωμικῶς αὐτοῦ διεστηκότα, καὶ τὴν μακρὰν ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ τῆς κακίας ὥσπερ τινὰ χώραν ἕξιν, ἐμπροθέτως οἰκοῦντα, καὶ τὰ πάθη τῆς ἀτιμίας καθάπερ χοίρους δι᾿ ἐνεργείας ἐκτρέφοντα, φιλάγαθον ἐπιδειξάμενος κένωσιν, καὶ τῶν ἐνόντων σοι θείων κατορθωμάτων τῷ μεγέθει τῆς συγκαταβάσεως ὑποδείξας τὴν δύναμιν, εἰς ἔφεσιν ταύτης ἐγείρης, καὶ πρὸς ἱερόν τινα καὶ μακάριον ζῆλον, ἀτρέπτως στομώσῃς, τῆς πάμπολύ με παρὰ πάντας βαρούσης νωθείας τε καὶ ἀμελείας τὸν φόρτον εἰς δύναμιν ἀποῤῥιψάμενον· καί σε τῆς περὶ ἐμὲ τὸν πτωχὸν σοφῆς οἰκονομίας καὶ προμηθείας χάριν ἀποθαυμάσας, μακάριε, τὴν ἐν πλείοσιν ἔτεσι καὶ ἀρεταῖς θειοτέραις διατήρησιν, σοῦ τοῦ καλοῦ καὶ ἀγαθοῦ ποιμένος τε καὶ πατρὸς, μετὰ δακρύων, καίτοι λίαν ὑπάρχων ἁμαρτωλὸς, χαρισθῆναί μοι καὶ πᾶσιν ἐντενῶς καθικέτευσα τὸν Κύριον ἡμῶν καὶ Θεόν· ἵνα ἔχοντές σε μάλιστα νῦν, ὅτε τῆς φύσει καὶ ὄντως ἀληθείας τὴν πρόῤῥησιν, ἡ κατὰ τὴν ἔκβασιν τῶν πραγμάτων ἀλήθεια σαφῶς ἐπιστώσατο· καὶ θλίψις, οἵα καθάπαξ οὐ γέγονεν ἀπὸ καταβολῆς κόσμου, οὐδ᾿ οὐ μὴ γένηται, πάντας πανταχῇ διὰ τὰς ἁμαρτίας κατέλαβε· τῇ τε πανσθενεῖ βακτηρίᾳ τῶν θεοπειθῶν προσευχῶν δυναμοῦντά τε καὶ ῥωννύοντα, καὶ τῇ πανιέρῳ σύριγγι τῆς θεοσόφου διδασκαλίας ὁδηγοῦντα καλῶς καὶ φωτίζοντα, καὶ εἰς τόπον χλόης τῆς ἀειζώου τῶν ἀρετῶν ἕξεως κατασκηνοῦντα διὰ τῆς πράξεως· καὶ ἐπὶ ὕδατος ἀναπαύσεως τῆς ἀειβλύστου τῶν ἱερῶν ἐν τοῖς δόγμασι χορηγίας ναμάτων ἐκτρέφοντα διὰ τῆς γνώσεως· καὶ εἰς τόπον ὀχυρὸν καὶ μάνδραν ὑψηλὴν τὴν καθολικὴν Ἐκκλησίαν, καὶ τὸν ταύτην δι᾿ αἵματος οἰκείου καὶ ζωοποιοῦ κατὰ θέλησιν ἁρμοσάμενον Κύριον ἀσφαλῶς διασώζοντα, τῆς τῶν ἐναντίων ἐλευθερωθῶμεν καταδρομῆς· οἱ προσφόρου καιροῦ τῆς τοῦ παντὸς δραξάμενοι ταραχῆς καὶ συγχύσεως, τὸ μέγα τῆς τῶν ἁπάντων σωστικῆς οἰκονομίας Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ ἀπόῤῥητον, ὡς πάντας αἰῶνας καὶ χρόνους, καὶ τοὺς ἐν αἰῶσι καὶ χρόνῳ φυσικῶς περιγράφον,συγχεῖν 93μηχανῶνται μυστήριον·οἷα τῆς ἐξ ἡμῶν νοερῶς τε καὶ λογικῶς ἐψυχωμένης, ὑπ᾿ αὐτοῦ κατ᾿ εὐδοκίαν Πατρὸς καὶ συνέργειαν ἁγίου Πνεύματος προσληφθείσης, καὶ αὐτῷ κατὰ μίαν καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν ὑπόστασιν ἑνωθείσης ἁγίας σαρκὸς, οὐκ ἐχούσης μετὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν τὰ προσόντα κατὰ φύσιν αὐτῇ, δίχα μόνης τῆς ἁμαρτίας· ὅτι μηδὲ τῆς φύσεως ἦν· μᾶλλον δὲ, αὐτοῦ τοῦ σαρκωθέντος Λόγου, καὶ δι᾿ ἡμᾶς ἐνανθρωπήσαντος, τὰ τὴν ἡμετέραν φύσιν οὐσιωδῶς χαρακτηρίζοντα καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς τελείως οὐ σώζοντα· ἀλλ᾿ ἢ ταῦτα πρὸς τὴν ἑαυτοῦ θείαν μετασκευάσαντος φύσιν· καὶ ταύτῃ, τὸ ἐλλιπὲς τῆς οἰκείας θεότητος δείξαντος, δεομένης παρενθήκης τινὸς πρὸς τὴν κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ἐντέλειαν· ἢ πάμπαν εἰς ἀνυπαρξίαν μεταχωρῆσαι ποιήσαντος, ὡς οὐ λίαν καλὰ, καὶ τῆς αὐτοῦ δημιουργίας τὸ ἔργον ἐξαφανίσαντος τῷ μὴ κατὰ λόγον τινὰ καὶ σοφίαν αὐτὰ ποιῆσαι, καὶ ἑνῶσαι πρὸς ἑαυτὸν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν· ἐπεὶ πῶς, τὸ λόγῳ καὶ σοφίᾳ γενόμενον, ἐν αὐτῷ τῷ λόγῳ καὶ τῇ σοφίᾳ καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν ἀδιασπάστως οὐ σώζεται; ἢ πῶς σωζόμενον καὶ μετὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν, οὐχ ὁμολογεῖται; καὶ δι᾿ ἥντινα αἰτίαν; ἵνα διὰ τῆς ὁμολογίας ὅτι γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος ὁ φύσει Θεὸς πιστευθῇ κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν; Ἢ τυχὸν οὐδὲ τὴν ἀρχὴν αὐτὰ τὰ φύσει προσόντα τῇ φύσει προσλαβὼν μετὰ τῆς φύσεως; Ἀλλὰ ἄψυχόν τινα καὶ ἄλογον, καὶ οὐδαμῶς ἐξ ἡμῶν, ἢ ἡμῖν ὑπάρχουσαν ὁμοούσιον σάρκα λαβών· ἢ οὐδὲ λαβὼν, ἀλλ᾿ ἄνωθεν καὶ ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς συνουσιωμένην αὐτῷ κατενέγκας, ἥνωσεν ἑαυτῷ· καὶ τὴν δι᾿ ἡμᾶς καὶ πρὸς ἡμᾶς οἰκονομίαν ἐφάντασεν, ἀνθρωπίνῳ σχήματι προσπελάσας ἡμῖν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐκ ἀνθρωπίνῃ φύσει προσομιλήσας, ἵνα τὸ γένος ἅπαν τοῦ παλαιοῦ παραπτώματος ἀνασώσηται, καὶ τοῦ δι᾿ αὐτὸ κατακρίματος ἐλευθερώσῃ.

And marvelling at thee, blessed one, for thy wise economy and forethought concerning me the poor man, I have earnestly besought our Lord and God with tears, though I am a great sinner, that the preservation of thee, the good and noble shepherd and father, for more years and in more divine virtues, be granted to me and to all; so that, having thee especially now, when the truth of the outcome of events has plainly confirmed the prediction of the Truth that is by nature and really truth, and a tribulation such as has never been from the foundation of the world, no, nor ever shall be, Matth. xxiv, 21. has overtaken all everywhere because of our sins — thee who strengthenest and fortifiest us with the all-powerful staff of prayers that persuade God, and guidest us well and enlightenest us with the all-holy pipe of God-wise teaching, and makest us dwell through practice in a place of green pasture, Psal. xxii, 2. the ever-living habit of the virtues, and nurturest us through knowledge by the water of rest, the ever-gushing supply of the sacred streams in the doctrines, and keepest us safe in a strong place and a lofty fold, the catholic Church, and the Lord who espoused her to himself by his own and life-giving blood, according to volition — we may be freed from the onslaught of the adversaries; who, seizing on the opportune moment of the turmoil and confusion of everything, scheme to confound the great and ineffable mystery of the economy of Christ God that saves all, as the mystery which naturally circumscribes all ages and times and those in ages and times; 93as though the holy flesh from us — ensouled intellectually and rationally, assumed by him according to the good pleasure of the Father and the co-operation of the Holy Spirit, and united to him according to one and the same hypostasis — did not have after the union the things that belong to it according to nature, sin alone excepted, since that was not of the nature; or rather, as though the incarnate Word himself, who became man for our sake, did not preserve perfectly, as in us, the things that characterize our nature essentially; but either changed them over into his own divine nature, and in this way showed the deficiency of his own Godhead, as needing some addition for completeness according to essence; or made them pass entirely into non-existence, as not very good, and obliterated the work of his own fashioning by not having made them according to some reason and wisdom, and united them to himself according to hypostasis.

For how is that which came to be by reason and wisdom not preserved indissolubly, by union, in the very Word and Wisdom? Or how, if it is preserved even after the union, is it not confessed? And for what cause? That through the confession it may be believed in truth that he who is God by nature became man? Or perhaps he did not even at the beginning assume, with the nature, the very things that belong by nature to the nature? But, taking some flesh soulless and irrational, and in no way from us or consubstantial with us — or not even taking it, but bringing it down from above, co-essentiated with him from the beginning — he united it to himself, and made a phantasm of the economy for us and towards us, approaching us in a human outward shape, but not consorting with us in human nature, that he might save the whole race from the old trespass and free it from the condemnation on its account.

Ταῦτα γὰρ, καὶ τούτων πλείω δοξάζουσιν, ὅσοι μὴ παντελείως ἐν Χριστῷ τῷ Θεῷ τὰ φυσικὰ, καὶ δίχα τῆς ἁμαρτίας, ὥσπερ καὶ τὰς φύσεις αὐτὰς σώζεσθαι 96κατὰ τοὺς ἁγίους Πατέρας ὀρθοδόξως ὁμολογοῦσι καὶ μετὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν. Οὐδὲ γὰρ τὴν θείαν φύσιν καὶ ἄκτιστον ἀνύπαρκτόν τε καὶ ἀθέλητον ἢ ἀνενέργητον καθορῶμεν· οὔτε μὴν τὴν κτιστὴν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς καὶ ἀνθρωπίνην οὐσίαν ἀνύπαρκτόν τε καὶ ἀθέλητον ἢ ἀνενἐργητον ἐπιγινώσκομεν. Καὶ εἰ μηδεμίαν τούτων ἔρημον ἐπιστάμεθα φυσικῆς ὑπάρξεως, θελήσεώς τε καὶ ἐνεργείας, ἐκ τούτων δὲ, θείας φύσεως καὶ ἀνθρωπίνης φύσεως λέγω, καὶ ἐν ταύταις, καὶ τούτων ὑπόστασίς ἐστι κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν ὁ εἷς καὶ μόνος Χριστὸς καὶ Υἱὸς, πῶς, εἴπερ φύσει κυρίως Θεὸς, καὶ φύσει κυρίως ἄνθρωπος ὁ αὐτὸς, οὐχὶ καὶ θεῖον θέλημα καὶ ἐνέργειαν φύσει κυρίως, καὶ ἀνθρώπινον θέλημα, καὶ ἐνέργειαν φύσει κυρίως εἶχε, μηδετέρου τούτων κατὰ φύσιν ὢν ἐλλιπὴς, δι᾿ ὧν ἐπαληθεύων τοῖς πράγμασι τὰ ὀνόματα, καὶ τούτοις αὖθις ἐπικυρῶν τὰ πράγματα, προδήλως ὁ αὐτὸς ἐγνωρίζετο, τά τε θεῖα καὶ ἀνθρώπινα φυσικῶς ἔχων καὶ θέλων καὶ ἐνεργῶν, κἀκ τούτου τὰ ἐξ ὧν, καὶ ἐν οἷς, καὶ ἅπερ ὑπῆρχε, πιστευόμενος; Ἐπεὶ, τοῦ φυσικοῦ θελήματος καὶ τῆς οὐσιώδους ἐνεργείας ἀναιρουμένων, τῆς τε θείας καὶ ἀνθρωπίνης οὐσίας, τοῦ ἐκ τούτων καὶ ταύτας ὄντος καὶ σώζοντος κατὰ μίαν ὑπόστασιν, πῶς Θεὸς ἢ ἄνθρωπος ἔσται; καὶ πῶς τοῦτο, ἢ ἐκεῖνο κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ὑπάρχων δειχθήσεται, μὴ σώζων ἑκατέρας φύσεως ἀνελλιπῶς τὴν ἰδιότητα, πάσης ἁμαρτίας χωρίς; Τὸ γὰρ τῶν κατὰ φύσιν ἐκστᾷν, κἂν τῆς ἔξω τῆς οὐσίας γεγένηται, μηδεμίαν ὕπαρξιν ἔχον· ὅτι μηδὲ κίνησιν φυσικήν. «Τὸ γὰρ μηδεμίαν κίνησιν ἔχον,» ᾗ φησιν ὁ θεοφάντωρ καὶ μέγας ἅγιος Διονύσιος De divinis nom., c. 8, s. 5. Pro κίνησιν cord. legit δύναμιν, vim, potentium., «οὔτε ἔστιν, οὔτε τί ἐστιν· οὔτε ἔστι τις αὐτοῦ παντελῶς θέσις.»

For these things, and more than these, are the opinions of all who do not confess in orthodox fashion that the natural things, sin apart, just as the natures themselves, are preserved altogether perfectly in Christ God, 96according to the holy Fathers, even after the union. For we do not behold the divine and uncreated nature as without existence, and without will or without operation; nor indeed do we recognize the created and human essence that is ours as without existence, and without will or without operation. And if we know that neither of these is bereft of natural existence, volition and operation, and from these — I mean divine nature and human nature — and in them, and of them, the one and only Christ and Son is in truth the hypostasis, how, if the same is properly God by nature and properly man by nature, did he not have also a divine will and operation properly by nature, and a human will and operation properly by nature, being deficient according to nature in neither of these; through which, verifying the names by the realities and again confirming the realities by these, the same was plainly known, having and willing and operating naturally the divine things and the human, and from this believed to be the things from which, and in which, and which he was?

Since, if the natural will and the essential operation of both the divine and the human essence are destroyed, how will he who is from these and is these, and preserves them in one hypostasis, be God or man? And how will he be shown to be this or that according to essence, if he does not preserve without deficiency the property of each nature, apart from all sin? For what has departed from the things according to nature has come to be outside the essence too, having no existence, since it has no natural movement either. «For what has no movement», as says the great and holy Dionysius, revealer of God, «neither is, nor is anything; nor is there any positing of it at all.»

Διά τοι τοῦτο τὰς Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ φύσεις ὁμολογοῦντας ἡμᾶς, τὴν θείαν φημὶ καὶ τὴν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς ἀνθρωπίνην, πρὸς βεβαίαν τῆς τε τούτων ὑπάρξεως πίστωσιν, καὶ τῆς ἡμῶν περὶ αὐτῶν ὁμολογίας ἀλήθειαν, καὶ τὰ φυσικὰ τούτων ἰσαρίθμως δεῖ πάντως πρεσβεύειν θελήματα, καὶ τὰς οὐσιώδεις τοσαύτας ἐνεργείας ὁμολογεῖν· καὶ μηδὲν αὐτῶν προφάσει δῆθεν τῆς καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν θείας ἑνώσεως ἀπομειοῦν ἢ ἐξαρνεῖσθαι τὸ σύνολον· γινώσκοντας, ὡς ἡ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσις οὐδὲν παραβλάπτει τῶν φυσικῶν, καθάπερ οὐδὲ τὰς φύσεις αὐτάς· εἰ καὶ ταύτας εἰς ἓν ἄγει δι᾿ ἑαυτῆς κατὰ τὴν μίαν ὑπόστασιν· τοῦ δύο, τῷ φυσικῷ λόγῳ ὑπάρχειν οὐδαμῶς ἐξιστῶσα· ὥσπερ οὖν καὐτὰ δὴ λέγω τὰ φυσικὰ ἰδιώματα, τῇ πρὸς ἄλληλα συμφυΐᾳ διόλου πάλιν ἑνίζουσα, καὶ μηδέτερον τοῦ ἑτέρου χωρὶς, ἢ τῆς θατέρου πρὸς θάτερον κοινωνίας ἐκτὸς, κατὰ τὸν τῶν Ῥωμαίων πάπαν ἀοίδιμον Λέοντα θεωρεῖσθαι Epist. ad Flavian. aliis ac locis. καθοτιοῦν συγχωροῦσα, τὴν τούτων οὐσιώδη καὶ φυσικὴν οὐκ ἁμαυροῖ παντελῶς, ἀλλὰ τελείως φυλάττει διαφοράν· 97τῷ γὰρ φυλάττειν φυλάττεται, καὶ τῷ συντηρεῖν συντηρεῖται.Μέχρι γὰρ τότε σαφῶς ἕνωσις πραγμάτων ἐστὶν, ἕως ἂν ἡ τούτων σώζηται φυσικὴ διαφορά· ἐπεὶ ταύτης παυσαμένης, παύεται πάντως κἀκείνη, τῇ συγχύσει τελείως ἀφανισθεῖσα. Ταύτης τοιγαροῦν, λέγω δὲ τῆς ἑνώσεως, οὐ λυμαινομένης τοῖς πράγμασιν, οὐδὲ τῇ τούτων διαφορᾷ, ἀλλὰ μόνον τῇ κλήσει προσφυῶς ἑνοποιούσης τὰ κατ᾿ αὐτὴν, εἰσὶ πάντοτε πάντῃ τε. καὶ πάντως ἀμειώτως ἐν αὐτῇ καὶ τελείως σωζόμενα, τὰ κατὰ φύσιν διάφορα, τὴν οἱανοῦν παρατροπὴν οὐ παθόντα καὶ σύγχυσιν· οὐκ ἐν οὐσίαις αὐταῖς, οὐ θελήμασιν, οὐκ ἐνεργείαις, οὐκ ἄλλῳ τινὶ φυσικῷ. Πῶς οὖν ἐπικάλυμμα τῆς ἰδίας, οὐκ οἶδ᾿ ὅπως εὐφήμως εἰπεῖν, καλοφροσύνης, ταύτην τινὲς προφασίζονται· καὶ τὸν σαρκωθένταΛόγον ἀθέλητον φύσει, καὶ ἀνενέργητον ἐν τῷ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς, ἤγουν τῷ ἀνθρωπίνῳ, φασί· ἅτε κυρίως ἀψύχου καὶ ἀλόγου τῆς ἐξ ἡμῶν προσληφθείσης οὐσίας ἐν αὐτῷ τυγχανούσης. Τὸ γὰρ ἀνενέργητον, ἀκίνητόν τε καὶ ἄψυχον προδήλως ἐστί· καὶ τὸ μηδεμίαν λογικὴν θέλησιν ἔχον, ἄλογον σαφῶς καὶ ἀνόητον. Καὶ εἰ τούτων χωρὶς κατὰ φύσιν ὑπῆρχεν ὁ σαρκωθεὶς Λόγος τὸ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς, πῶς ὅτι καὶ ἄνθρωπος γέγονε πιστευθήσεται; πῶς δὲ μᾶλλον οὐχὶ τραπεὶς τὴν θείαν φύσιν, καὶ σαρκὸς πάθεσιν ἀκουσίως ὑποπεσὼν δειχθήσεται κατ᾿ αὐτήν; Τῇ γὰρ τῶν φυσικῶν ἀναιρέσει, τὴν τῶν τοιούτων καταδρομὴν, Ἄρειός τε καὶ Ἀπολινάριος κατ᾿ αὐτοῦ πεποιήκασιν. Ὁ μὲν γὰρ, ἄψυχον, ὁ δὲ, ἄλογον, ὅπερ ἀν ενέργητόν ἐστι καὶ ἀθέλητον, αὐτὸν δογματίσαντες, κτίσμα τοῦτον καὶ ποίημα καθ᾿ ἑαυτὸν ἐβλασφήμησαν. Ἑκάτερος γὰρ παθητὸν φύσει θεότητος, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ φύσει σαρκὸς εἶπον τὸν Μονογενῆ· κἂν Ἀπολινάριος ἔτι κατ᾿ ἀσέβειαν δαψιλευόμενος, καὶ τελείαν τὴν εἰς σάρκα μεταβολὴν κατ᾿ ἔκπτωσιν καὶ τροπὴν τῆς αὐτοῦ θεότητος ἀπεφήνατο.

For this reason, then, we who confess the natures of Christ God — the divine, I mean, and the human that is ours — must by all means, for the firm confirmation of their existence and the truth of our confession concerning them, also uphold their natural wills, equal in number, and confess as many essential operations; and not, under the pretext forsooth of the divine union according to hypostasis, diminish any of them or deny them altogether; knowing that the union according to hypostasis damages none of the natural things, just as it does not the natures themselves; even if it brings these into one through itself according to the one hypostasis, in no way removing them from being two by the natural principle; just as, then, uniting again throughout the natural idioms themselves, I mean, by their growing-together with one another, and in no way allowing either to be considered without the other or outside the communion of the one with the other, according to Leo, the celebrated pope of the Romans, it does not obscure at all, but perfectly keeps, their essential and natural difference; 97for by keeping it is kept, and by preserving it is preserved. For so long is there plainly a union of realities as their natural difference is preserved; since, when this ceases, that too ceases altogether, wholly obliterated by confusion.

Since this, then — I mean the union — does not harm the realities nor their difference, but only unifies, fittingly, in the appellation, the things it concerns, the things that are different according to nature are always and altogether preserved in it without diminution and perfectly, suffering no deviation whatever, or confusion: not in the essences themselves, not in wills, not in operations, not in anything else natural. How then do some put this forward as a covering for their own — I do not know how to say it with fair words — fine thinking, and say that the incarnate Word is without will by nature, and without operation in what is ours, that is, in the human, as though the essence assumed from us were properly soulless and irrational in him? For what is without operation is plainly unmoved and soulless; and what has no rational volition is plainly irrational and mindless. And if the incarnate Word was by nature without these in what is ours, how will it be believed that he also became man? And how, rather, will he not be shown to have been changed in the divine nature, and to have fallen involuntarily under the passions of flesh according to it? For by the destruction of the natural things Arius and Apollinarius made their onslaught of such things against him. For the one teaching as doctrine that he was soulless, the other that he was irrational — which is without operation and without will — they blasphemed him as a creature and a thing made in himself. For each of them said that the Only-begotten was passible by the nature of Godhead, but not by the nature of flesh; even if Apollinarius, being still more lavish in impiety, also declared the perfect conversion into flesh, by the falling-away and change of his Godhead.

Χρὴ τοιγαροῦν εὐσεβοῦντας ἡμᾶς, τὴν τοιαύτην ὁμολογίαν διώσασθαι, καὶ τὴν τῶν ἁγίων Πατέρων κρατύνειν διδασκαλίαν· καὶ δύο φυσικὰ θελήματα πάντη τε. νΟτΑΕ.

100κατ᾿ αὐτοὺς, καὶ οὐσιώδεις ἐνεργείας, ἐπὶ τοῦ αὐτοῦ καὶ ἑνὸς ὁμολογεῖν. Εἰ γὰρ ἓν ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῦ θέλημα καὶ μίαν ἐνέργειαν εἴποιμεν, ἢ τοῦτον οὐκ ὄντα φύσει καὶ ἄνθρωπον, ἀλλὰ μόνον Θεόν· ἢ φύσει μόνον ἄνθρωπον, καὶ οὐ Θεόν· ἢ οὔτε ἄνθρωπον οὔτε Θεὸν σαφῶς δογματίσομεν. Οἷον, εἰ μὲν φυσικὸν τὸ θέλημα τυχὸν εἴποιμεν, ἢ θεῖον πάντως, ἢ ἀνθρώπινον τοῦτό ἐστι· καὶ εἰ θεῖον, τὰ θεῖα κατ᾿ αὐτὸ θελήσει φυσικῶς ὁ Λόγος· Θεὸν φύσει μόνον καὶ συνθελητὴν τῷ ἰδίῳ γεννήτορι δεικνὺς ἑαυτὸν, καὶ οὐκ ἄνθρωπον· οὐ γὰρ πέφυκε διὰ τοῦ φύσει θείου, τὸ ἀνθρώπινον φύσει χαρακτηρίζεσθαι· ὥσπερ οὐδὲ διὰ τοῦ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ἀνθρωπίνου, τὸ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν θεῖον γνωρίζεσθαι. Τοῦτο δὲ, καὶ εἰ ἀνθρώπινον φῶμεν τὸ θέλημα φύσει, κατὰ τὸ ἀκόλουθον συναχθήσεται. Εἰ δὲ ὑποστατικὸν, τῆς αὐτοῦ μόνης ὑποστάσεως ἔσται χαρακτηριστικόν· καὶ οὔποτ᾿ ἂν κατ᾿ αὐτὸ κοινωνήσῃ Πατρὶ καὶ ἡμῖν· τῶν γὰρ ἄκρων ὑποστάσει διαστέλλεται καὶ χωρίζεται. Τὰ αὐτὰ δὲ πάλιν καὶ περὶ ἐνεργειῶν ἔστιν εἰπεῖν. Ἀλλ᾿ οὐ διὰ τοῦτο, μὴ γένοιτο, τὰς τῶν θεοφόρων Πατέρων περὶ αὐτῶν, φημὶ τῶν ἐνεργειῶν, μοναδικὰς ἐξαρνούμεθα, τήν τε τοῦ λεχθέντος θεοφάντορος ἁγίου Διονυσίου Epist. 4, ad Caium. «Θεανδρικὴν ἐνέργειαν,» καὶ τὴν Κυρίλλου τοῦ σοφοῦ «μίαν τε καὶ συγγενῆ δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεδειγμένην ἐνέργειαν.» Αὗται μὲν γὰρ διὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν, καὶ τὴν πρὸς ἀλλήλας διόλου τῶν φυσικῶν ἐνεργειῶν συμφυΐαν, εὐσεβῶς αὐτοῖς ἐκηρύχθησαν· ὥσπερ καὶ αἱ δυϊκαὶ, διὰ τὴν οὐσιώδη καὶ φυσικὴν τούτων διαφοράν. Η γὰρ Θεανδρικὴ τῆς θείας ὁμοῦ καὶ ἀνδρικῆς ἐνεργείας ὑπάρχει περίληψις. Κατάλληλον γὰρ ὁ διδάσκαλος ἐπινοήσας φωνὴν, ἑκατέραν, ὡς ἔφην, τῇ προφορᾷ συλλαμβάνουσαν, καὶ ταύτην μοναδικῶς ἐκφωνήσας, τὴν διπλῆν, τοῦ διπλοῦ τὴν φύσιν, ἐνέργειαν περιφραστικῶς παρεδήλωσεν. Ἐπειδὴ γὰρ πρότερον ἔφησεν, «Οὐ κατὰ Θεὸν τὰ θεῖα δράσας, ὅτι μὴ γυμνὸς ὑπῆρχε Θεός· ἀλλὰ δράσας μὲν φύσει τὰ θεῖα· Θεὸς γὰρ ἦν κατὰ φύσιν· διὰ σαρκὸς δὲ, τῆς αὐτῷ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἡνωμένης· οὐ τὰ ἀνθρώπινα κατὰ ἄνθρωπον, ὅτι μὴ ἄνθρωπος ὑπῆρχε ψιλός· ἀλλὰ δράσας μὲν φυσικῶς τὰ ἀνθρώπινα· φύσει γὰρ ἄνθρωπος ἦν· κατ᾿ ἐξουσίαν δὲ θεικὴν,» ἀλλ᾿ οὐκ ἀνάγκῃ τινὶ καὶ βίᾳ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς φυσικῇ πρὸς τὸ πράττειν ἀγόμενος, συνέπλεξε τήν τε θείαν ἅμα καὶ ἀνδρικὴν τοῦ αὐτοῦ κατὰ φύσιν ἐνέργειαν· ὡς μηδετέραν τῆς ἑτέρας οὖσαν χωρὶς, ἀλλ᾿ ἐν ἀλλήλαις τε καὶ δι᾿ ἀλλήλων συμφυῶς γνωριζομένας, διὰ τὴν ἄλυτον ἕνωσιν· καὶ μοναδικῇ φωνῇ συλλαβὼν ἄμφω, περιφραστικῶς ἀνεκήρυξεν.

So then we who are pious must thrust away such a confession, and strengthen the teaching of the holy Fathers; and two natural wills in every way and

100… according to them, and essential operations, in the same and one [Christ]. For if we were to say one will and one operation in him, we shall plainly be teaching as doctrine either that he is not by nature also man, but God only; or by nature man only, and not God; or neither man nor God. For example, if we should say that the will is natural, then it is altogether either divine or human; and if divine, the Word will will the divine things naturally according to it, showing himself God only by nature, and co-willer with his own begetter, and not man; for it is not in nature for the human by nature to be characterized through what is divine by nature, just as neither is the divine according to essence made known through what is human according to essence. And this same will be concluded in consequence if we say that the will is human by nature. But if [we say it is] hypostatic, it will be characteristic of his hypostasis alone; and according to it he would never have communion with the Father and with us; for by hypostasis he is set apart and separated from the extremes. And the same again may be said concerning operations.

But not on this account, God forbid, do we deny the singular [expressions] of the God-bearing Fathers concerning them, I mean the operations: both the «theandric operation» of the aforesaid revealer of God, saint Dionysius, and that of Cyril the wise, the «one and kindred operation shown through both». For these were piously proclaimed by them on account of the union, and the growing-together of the natural operations with each other throughout; just as the dual ones also, on account of their essential and natural difference. For the theandric is the comprehension of the divine and at the same time the manly operation. For the teacher, having devised a fitting expression, which comprehends each in its utterance, as I said, and having uttered it singularly, indicated periphrastically the double operation of him who is double in nature. For since he had said before, «Not as God doing the divine things, because he was not bare God; but doing the divine things by nature, for he was God according to nature, yet through flesh, united to him according to hypostasis; not the human things as man, because he was not mere man; but doing the human things naturally, for he was by nature man, yet with divine authority», and not led to act by some necessity and natural force as we are, he wove together the divine and at the same time manly operation of the same according to nature; so that neither is without the other, but they are known in each other and through each other as grown together, on account of the indissoluble union; and comprehending both in a singular expression, he proclaimed them periphrastically.

Ἵνα τῷ μὲν δρᾶσαι τά τε θεῖα καὶ ἀνθρώπινα εἰπεῖν· εἰ καὶ μὴ κατὰ Θεὸν, μήτε μὴν κατὰ ἄνθρωπον· ὅτι μὴ γυμνὸς, ὡς ἔφην, Θεὸς, μήτε ψιλὸς ἄνθρωπος ἦν· ὅμως δὲ κατ᾿ ἀλήθειαν δρᾶσαι, τὴν τῶν φυσικῶν ἐνεργειῶν οὐσιώδη πιστώσηται 101διαφοράν· τῷ δὲ συλλαβεῖν ταύτας, καὶ τῇ προσφόρῳ φωνῇ συνοπτικῶς ἐξειπεῖν διὰ τῆς Θεανδρικῆς ἐνεργείας, τὴν τούτων ἀδιάσπαστον ἕνωσιν ἐκδιδάξῃ. Καὶ ἡ Κυρίλλου δὲ τοῦ σοφοῦ «μία τε καὶ συγγενὴς δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεδειγμένη ἐνέργεια,» τῆς τοῦ μεγάλου τούτου Πατρὸς μεγίστης ἔχεται διανοίας. Τὴν γὰρ τῶν φυσικῶν ἐνεργειῶν καὐτὸς συμφυϊαν καὶ ἕνωσιν δι᾿ αὐτῆς ἐκπαιδεύει. Θέλων γὰρ δεῖξαι καὶ ὁ πανεύφημος οὗτος ἀνὴρ, ὡς ἤ τε σὰρξ Θεὸς, καὶ ἡ κατ᾿ αὐτὴν οὐσιώδης ἐνέργεια, θεία τῇ πρὸς τῇ φύσει Θεὸν Λόγον ἑνώσει γεγένηται Fr. Ὅτι καὶ ἡ σὰρξ Θεός, τῇ πρὸς τὸν Λόγον ἑνώσει· καὶ τὰ φυσικὰ αὐτῆς θεῖα, τῇ πρὸς τὸν αὐτὸν ἑνώσει καὶ συμφυΐᾳ γεγένηται. Etiam carnem Deum unione cum Verbo; ejusque naturalia divina, unione cum ipso ac cohæsione facta esse., καὶ τὰ ὑπὲρ ἄνθρωπον ἐνήργει, μὴ ἐκστᾶσα τούτου, οὗπερ ἦν κατὰ φύσιν· ὥσπερ οὖν καὶ αὐτὸς ὁ Λόγος τῇ ταύτης προσλήψει δι᾿ ἡμᾶς ἑκουσίως γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος, τὰ κατὰ ἄνθρωπον ἐνεργῶν· καίπερ ὑπάρχων φύσει Θεὸς, διαγορεύει τοῦτον, μὴ παντουργῷ προστάγματι μόνον ἰᾶσθαι καὶ ζωοποιεῖν, ὡς μὴ Θεὸν ὄντα γυμνόν· ἀλλὰ συνεργάτιν ὥσπερ τινὰ, πρὸς τοῦτο δὴ μάλιστα, τὴν ἁγίαν αὐτοῦ σάρκα λαμβάνειν ἐπείγεσθαι· καὶ ζωοποιεῖν καὶ θεραπεύειν δι᾿ αὐτῆς καὶ τῆς κατ᾿ αὐτὴν ἁφῆς καὶ φωνῆς· ἵνα ταύτην δεικνύῃ ζωοποιεῖν δυναμένην, διὰ τὸ πρὸς αὐτὴν ἐν τῇ ἑνώσει γενέσθαι· καὶ αὐτοῦ τοῦ φύσει ζωογονοῦντος τὰ σύμπαντα, καὶ οὐχ ἕτερον σάρκα κυρίως ὑπάρχειν. Διὸ καὶ μίαν αὐτοῦ καὶ αὐτῆς, φημὶ τῆς ἁγίας σαρκὸς καὶ συγγενῆ δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεικνύει ἐνέργειαν· μίαν μὲν, τῇ ἑνώσει· συγγενῆ δὲ πάλιν, τοῖς μέρεσιν· ὡς τὴν αὐτὴν οὖσαν, ἕν τε παντουργῷ προστάγματι, καὶ ἁφῇ τῆς ἁγίας σαρκός· δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν γὰρ, ὡς Θεὸς, ὁ Λόγος ταύτην σαφῶς ἐπεδείκνυτο· δεικνὺς τῷ καὶ δι᾿ αὐτῆς ζωοποιεῖν, ὡς καὶ αὐτὴ γέγονεν δι᾿ ὅλου ζωοποιός· καθάπερ καὶ πῦρ διὰ σιδήρου καῖον, ἑνώσει τῇ πρὸς αὐτὸν καυστικὸν ἀποφαίνει τὸν σίδηρον· ὡς εἶναι λοιπὸν διὰ τοῦτο μὴ τοῦ πυρὸς κατὰ φύσιν μόνον τὴν καῦσιν, ἀλλὰ καὐτοῦ τοῦ σιδήρου διὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν· καὶ τὴν ἐν τοῖς θαύμασι θείαν τοῦ Λόγου πάλιν ἐνέργειαν, μὴ τοῦ αὐτοῦ μόνον διὰ τὴν φύσιν ὑπάρχειν, ἀλλὰ καὐτῆς τῆς ἁγίας σαρκὸς, διὰ τὴν πρὸς αὐτὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν· συνεργάτιν γὰρ ἑαυτοῦ ταύτην ἐν τοῖς θείοις παρελάμβανεν, ᾗ φησιν ὁ διδάσκαλος, ὥσπερ καὶ ψυχὴ τὸ ἴδιον σῶμα, πρὸς τὴν τῶν οἰκείων ἔργων ἐκπλήρωσιν.

So that by saying that he did both the divine and the human things (though not as God, nor yet as man, because he was neither, as I said, bare God nor mere man, but yet did them in truth) he might confirm 101the essential difference of the natural operations; and by comprehending them, and uttering them synoptically in the apt expression, by way of the theandric operation, he might teach their indissoluble union.

And the «one and kindred operation shown through both» of Cyril the wise also holds to the very great understanding of this great Father. For he too teaches through it the growing-together and union of the natural operations. For this all-praised man too, wishing to show that both the flesh has become God and the essential operation in it divine, by the union with the Word who is God by nature, and that it operated the things above man without departing from that which it was according to nature, just as the Word himself also, by the assumption of it, willingly became man for us, operating the things of man, although being God by nature, declares that he not only heals and gives life by an all-working command, as not being bare God, but hastens to take his holy flesh as a kind of fellow-worker, for this above all; and gives life and heals through it and through its touch and voice; that he might show it able to give life, because he had become one with it in the union, and that it is properly the flesh of him who by nature gives life to all things, and not of another. Wherefore he shows one and kindred operation of him and of it, I mean the holy flesh, through both: one, by the union; and kindred, again, by the parts; as being the same both in the all-working command and in the touch of the holy flesh. For through both the Word, as God, clearly showed this operation; showing, by giving life through it too, that it too had become wholly life-giving; just as fire burning through iron, by union with it, renders the iron burning, so that thereafter, on this account, the burning is not of the fire alone according to nature, but of the iron itself too on account of the union; and the divine operation of the Word in the miracles, again, is not his alone on account of the nature, but of the holy flesh itself as well, on account of the union with it according to hypostasis. For he took it as his own fellow-worker in the divine things, as the teacher says, just as the soul too takes its own body, for the fulfilment of its own works.

Αὕτη γοῦν ἡ μία καὶ συγγενὴς δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν ἐπιδεδειγμένη ἐνέργεια, τῶν κατὰ φύσιν ἐνεργειῶν δηλοῦσα τὴν ἕνωσιν· τὸ ἑτερογενὲς τούτων καὶ δυϊκὸν, καθάπερ καὶ 104ἡ Θεανδρικὴ ἐνέργεια, δι᾿ ἑαυτῆς κυρίως σημαίνει· τῷ πρὸς ἄμφω τὰ μέρη, τῶν ἐξ ὧν καὶ ἐν οἷς ὁ εἷς καὶ μόνος ἐγνωρίζετο σεσαρκωμένος Λόγος, ἀναφέρεσθαι· καὶ ἀμφοτέρων ὑπάρχειν, ὡς δι᾿ ἀμφοῖν, τοῦ τε παντουργοῦ προστάγματος, καὶ τῆς ἁφῆς τῆς ἁγίας σαρκὸς ἐπιδεδειγμένη. Οὐ δεῖ δὲ τῷ συγγενεῖ ξενίζεσθαι παντελῶς· ἐπὶ τε γὰρ ὁμοουσίων καὶ ἑτεροουσίων λέλεκται τοῖς ἁγίοις Πατράσι τὸ συγγενές· καὶ μάρτυς ὁ Θεολόγος καὶ μέγας Γρηγόριος, περὶ ψυχῆς λέγων οὕτως ἐν τῷ εἰς Καισάριον τὸν αὐτοῦ ἀδελφὸν Ἐπιταφίῳ λόγῳ, Μικρὸν δὲ ὕστερον καὶ τὸ συγγενὲς σαρκίον ἀπολαβοῦσα, ᾧ τὰ ἐκεῖθεν συνεφιλοσόφησεν. Ἐνταῦθα διὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τὴν φύσιν συγγενῆ τῇ ψυχῇ τὴν σάρκα κέκληκεν ὁ διδάσκαλος. Καὶ πάλιν, Ἐν τῷ Εἰς τὰ φῶτα, περὶ τοῦ μονογενοῦς Υἱοῦ διδάσκων, φησί· Καὶ ὁ ὑπὸ τοῦ συγγενοῦς μαρτυρούμενος Πνεύματος. Νῦν τὸ συγγενὲς διὰ τὴν φύσιν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τὴν ἕνωσιν εἴρηκεν. Αὐτὸς δὲ Κύριλλος ὁ σοφὸς κἀπὶ τῇ οὐσίᾳ καὶ ὑποστάσει διῃρημένων τὸ συγγενὲς ἀπαρατηρήτως λαμβάνει· καὶ τάδε λέγει τὸν εὐαγγελιστὴν Ἰωάννην ὑπομνηματίζων In Joan. iii, 5., περὶ τῆς κατὰ τὸ θεῖον βάπτισμα θεραπείας, «Συγγενῶς πως ἐχούσης πρὸς ἄμφω τῶν δεδηλωμένων. Πνεύματι μὲν γὰρ ἁγιάζει τοῦ ἀνθρώπου τὸ πνεῦμα· ὕδατι δὲ αὖ, τὸ σῶμα.» Τίς δὲ, καὶ ποία συγγένεια Πνεύματος ἁγίου καὶ ὕδατος κατά τε οὐσίαν ἢ ὑπόστασιν, πρὸς τὸ ἡμέτερον πνεῦμα καὶ σῶμα; εἰ καὶ τὰ μάλιστα κατὰ χάριν ἡμῖν καὶ τοῖς ἐξ ὧν συγκείμεθα μέρεσιν αὕτη προσγίνεται, διὰ τὴν ἱερὰν ἀναγέννησιν. Ἀλλὰ καὐτὸς αὖθις ὁ μοναδικὸς ἀριθμὸς, ὡς πᾶσιν εὕδηλον, καθ᾿ ἕκαστον τῶν ἐν πτώσει γενῶν ἓν τε ὁμοουσίοις καὶ ἑτεροουσίοις, πρὸς τῶν ἁγίων παραλαμβάνεται, τὸ καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν, ἢ δηλονότι σκοπούντων, ὡς τὸ, «Εἷς ἐξ ἀμφοῖν, καὶ δι᾿ ἑνὸς ἀμφότερα.» Καὶ τὸ, «Μία ὑπόστασις καὶ μία φύσις τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγου σεσαρκωμένη Athanas. de Incarn., et sæpe Cyrill. Greg. Orat. 36, p. 582..» Καὶ τὸ, «Εἰ γὰρ καὶ τὸ συναμφότερον ἓν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τῇ φύσει, τῇ δὲ συνόδῳ.» Καὶ πάλιν ἐπὶ τῶν ὁμοουσίων, ὡς τὸ, «Καὶ ἦν, καὶ ἦν, καὶ ἦν, ἀλλ᾿ ἓν ἦν. Φῶς, καὶ φῶς, καὶ φῶς, ἀλλ᾿ ἓν φῶς.» Καὶ τὸ, «Εἷς Θεὸς, καὶ μία φύσις ἡ ἀνωτάτη,» φησὶν ὁ Θεολόγος Γρηγόριος.

This one and kindred operation shown through both, then, while showing the union of the operations according to nature, signifies properly through itself their being of other genus and their duality, just as 104the theandric operation also does: by being referred to both the parts, from which and in which the one and only Word incarnate was known; and by being of both, as shown through both, the all-working command and the touch of the holy flesh.

One must not be wholly taken aback at the word kindred; for the holy Fathers have used kindred both of things consubstantial and of things of other essence. And witness is the Theologian and great Gregory, speaking of the soul thus in the Funeral Oration on Caesarius his brother: «And a little later, taking back also the kindred flesh, with which it had philosophized the things there». Here the teacher has called the flesh kindred to the soul on account of the union, and not of the nature. And again, in the oration On the Lights, teaching about the only-begotten Son, he says: «And he who is witnessed by the kindred Spirit». Now he has said kindred on account of the nature, and not of the union. And Cyril the wise himself takes kindred without discrimination also of things divided in essence and hypostasis; and says this, commenting on John the evangelist, about the healing by divine baptism: «[The healing] being somehow kindred toward both of the things indicated. For by the Spirit he sanctifies the spirit of man; and by water, in turn, the body.» But what kindredness, and of what kind, is there of the Holy Spirit and of water, according to essence or hypostasis, to our spirit and body? Even though this comes above all by grace to us and to the parts of which we are composed, through the sacred regeneration.

But the singular number itself, again, as is plain to all, in each of the genders in its cases, both in things consubstantial and in things of other essence, is employed by the saints, looking, that is, to what is one by union or [by nature]; as in «One from both, and through one both»; and «One hypostasis and one nature of God the Word incarnate»; and «For even if the both-together is one, yet not by nature, but by the concourse». And again in the case of things consubstantial, as in «And he was, and he was, and he was, but one was. Light, and light, and light, but one light»; and «One God, and one nature, the highest», says Gregory the Theologian.

Οὐ δεῖ τοιγαροῦν τὰς μοναδικὰς τῶν ἁγίων φωνὰς ἢ συγγενεῖς διὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν εἰρημένας, ἐπ᾿ ἀνατροπῇ τῶν δυϊκῶν ἢ ἑτερογενῶν λελεγμένων αὐτοῖς, διὰ τὴν διαφορὰν ἐκδέχεσθαι παντελῶς· ἢ πάλιν κατ᾿ 105ἐκείνων ταύτας. Ὁ γὰρ οὕτως ἐκλαμβάνων, καὶ μὴ ἀμφοτέρας ἐπίσης ἀσπαζόμενος, καὶ προσηκόντως, τὰς μὲν, τῇ ἑνώσει, τὰς δὲ, τῇ φυσικῇ διαφορᾷ προσαρμόζων αρμόζων· ἢ διαιρέσει πάντως, ἢ συγχύσει κατὰ τὸ εἰκὸς περιπίπτει· μᾶλλον δὲ φάναι κυρίως, ἑαυτὸν περιβάλλει, καὶ τῆς τῶν ἁγίων Πατέρων διωθεῖται κοινωνίας ἀληθοῦς καὶ ὁμολογίας. Εἰ γὰρ καὶ πρὸς τὸν Λόγον ἓν ἡ σὰρξ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν γέγονε, καὶ τῇ ἑνώσει τὴν ἐνέργειαν αὐτοῦ κατεπλούτησεν· ἀλλ᾿ οὐδαμῶς τοῦ κτιστὴν κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ὑπάρχειν διέπεσεν, οὔτε τῶν φυσικῶν καὶ ἀδιαβλήτων παθῶν τὴν οἱανοῦν πέπονθεν ἐκδρομήν. Καὶ εἰ τοῦτο κυρίως ἐστὶν, ὥσπερ οὖν καί ἐστι, διάφορον τήν τε φύσιν αὐτὴν, καὶ τὴν φυσικὴν πρὸς τὸν Λόγον ἐκέκτητο προδήλως ἐνέργειαν. Ἣν αὐτὸς ἔχων ὁ σαρκωθεὶς Λόγος ὡς ἄνθρωπος· αὐτοῦ γὰρ ἦν κατὰ φύσιν. Αἵματος γὰρ καὶ σαρκὸς παραπλησίως ἡμῖν κεκοινώνηκε, δύο φυσικὰς εἶχεν ὁ αὐτὸς ἐνεργείας ὥσπερ οὖν καὶ φύσεις· τὴν θείαν καὶ Πατρικὴν, καὶ τὴν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς ἀνθρωπίνην· δι᾿ ὧν ὅτι Θεὸς ἦν ὁ αὐτὸς καὶ ἄνθρωπος ἐπιστεύετο. Ὧν γὰρ εἶχε τὰς φυσικὰς ἐνεργείας, τούτων δηλαδὴ καὶ τὰς φύσεις. Ὡς γὰρ Θεὸς, διά τε φωνῆς καὶ ἁφῆς τῆς ἁγίας σαρκὸς, ἰώμενός τε καὶ ζωοποιῶν ἐδείκνυτο κατὰ φύσιν· οὕτω πάλιν καὶ ἄνθρωπος ὢν ὁ αὐτὸς οὐσιωδῶς ἐγνωρίζετο, διὰ τῆς αὐτῆς ἁφῆς καὶ χειρὸς, λεντίῳ διαζωννύμενος, καὶ νίπτων τοὺς πόδας τῶν μαθητῶν· καὶ κλῶν τοὺς ἄρτους, καὶ τούτοις διαδιδούς· καὶ τὸ ψωμίον εἰς τὸ τρυβλίον ἐμβάπτων· καὶ πάλιν διὰ τῆς φωνῆς τὴν προσοῦσαν λύπην καταμηνύων, Περίλυπός ἐστιν ἡ ψυχή μου ἕως θανάτου. Καὶ, Νῦν ἡ ψυχή μου τετάρακται. Καὶ, Οὕτως οὐκἰσχύσατε μίαν ὥραν γρηγορῆσαι μετ᾿ ἐμοῦ. Καὶ ὅσα τοιαῦτα, μηδαρ ὧ́ς τὸ φύσει πεφυκὸς ἑκατέρας φύσεως, τῶν ἐξ ὧν καὶ ἐν αἷς συνέστηκεν, ἀθετῶν· ἀλλὰ φυσικαῖς ἐνεργείαις τὰς φύσεις πιστούμενος. Ἵνα δὲ καὶ διὰ Κυρίλλου τοῦ σοφοῦ δειχθῇ, ὡς οὐ φύσει μίαν, ὥσπερ οὐδὲ φύσιν, ἡ σὰρξ εἶχε πρὸς τὸν Λόγον ἐνέργειαν, εἰ καὶ συγγενὴς γέγονεν πρὸς αὐτὸν, τῷ θεωθῆναι διὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν, ἀκούσωμεν τί διέξεισι, τὸ κατὰ Ματθαῖον Εὐαγγέλιον ἑρμηνεύων, ἐν τῇ δευτέρᾳ βίβλῳ Ex Cyrill. Com. in Matth. qui desiderantur. Vexatus hic maxime locus velut in specie favens hæreticis. τῆς ἐξομολογήσεως. «Διὰ τοῦτο φησὶν ἐνεργεῖν ἐν Πνεύματι Θεοῦ. Καὶ γάρ τοι κατὰ τὸ ἀληθὲς, οὐ σαρκὸς ἐνέργεια φυσικὴ καὶ ἀνθρωπότητος δύναμις, παραλύει τὸν Σατανᾶν, καὶ καταισχύνει τὸν Βελζεβούλ· ἐπεὶ διδασκέτω σέ τις παρελθὼν, εἰ βούλεται, τί το κωλύον τοῖς ἐθέλουσι προκεῖσθαι τὴν ἐξουσίαν τὴν κατὰ πνευμάτων ἀκαθάρτων.

One must not, then, by any means take the singular or kindred expressions of the saints, spoken on account of the union, to the overturning of the dual or other-genus expressions spoken by them on account of the difference; or again 105those against these. For whoever takes them so, and does not embrace both equally, fitly applying the ones to the union and the others to the natural difference, falls in all likelihood either into division altogether or into confusion; or rather, to speak properly, entangles himself, and thrusts himself out of the true communion and confession of the holy Fathers.

For even if the flesh became one with the Word according to hypostasis, and by the union was enriched with his operation, it in no way fell from being created according to essence, nor suffered any departure whatever from the natural and blameless passions. And if this is properly so, as indeed it is, it manifestly possessed both the nature itself and the natural operation as different from the Word. And the incarnate Word himself, having this as man (for it was his according to nature, since he shared in blood and flesh in like manner to us), Hebr. ii, 14. the same had two natural operations, just as also natures: the divine and the Father's, and the human that is ours; through which he was believed to be the same God and man. For of what he had the natural operations, of these clearly he had the natures too. For as God he was shown healing and giving life according to nature through both the voice and the touch of the holy flesh; so again the same, being also man, was known essentially through the same touch and hand, girding himself with a towel, and washing the feet of the disciples; Joan. xiii, 4. and breaking the loaves, and distributing them to them; and dipping the morsel into the dish; Marc. xiv, 20; Joan. xiii, 26. and again through the voice revealing the grief that was in him: «My soul is exceeding sorrowful, even unto death»; Matth. xxvi, 38. and «Now is my soul troubled»; Joan. xii, 27. and «So, could ye not watch with me one hour?»; Matth. xxvi, 40. and all such things; in no way setting aside what is by nature natural to each nature of those from which and in which he consists, but confirming the natures by natural operations.

And that it may be shown through Cyril the wise as well that the flesh did not have one operation by nature with the Word, just as it did not have one nature, even if it became kindred to him by being deified on account of the union, let us hear what he sets out, interpreting the Gospel according to Matthew, in the second book of the Confession: «Therefore he says that he operates in the Spirit of God. For indeed in truth it is not the natural operation of flesh and the power of manhood that undoes Satan and puts Beelzebul to shame; else let someone come forward, if he will, and teach thee what hinders authority over unclean spirits from lying before those who wish it.

Ἰδοὺ; εἰ τοῦτο ἐνεργεῖ σαρκὸς φύσει· πάντες γὰρ ἐσμὲν ἐν σαρκὶ, καὶ εἷς κατὰ πάντων ὁ τῆς ἀνθρωπότητος λόγος· ἀλλ᾿ οὔτε σαρκὸς ἔργον ἰδικῶς,

108οὔτε μὲν ἀνθρωπότητος, τὸ κατισχῦσαι πνευμάτων, ἐπεὶ μὴ πάντες ἰσχύουσιν· ἐνεργείας δὲ μᾶλλον ὁρᾶται τὸ κατόρθωμα διὰ τοῦ Πνεύματος.» Οὐκοῦν ἀληθὲς εἴρηκεν ὁ Πατὴρ, καθὸ νοεῖται σὰρξ καὶ ἄνθρωπος, ὅτι Πνεύματος ἔργον ἦν, καὶ οὐχὶ τῆς σαρκὸς, ἤγουν ἀνθρωπότητος, ἡ κατὰ τῶν δαιμὸ ων ἰσχύς. Ἅγιον μὲν γὰρ ὁμολογουμένως ἐστὶ τὸ σῶμα τοῦ Κυρίου, καὶ πᾶσαν ἔχον κατὰ πάσης νόσου τὴν δύναμιν· ἀλλ᾿ ἦν τε, καί ἐστιν ἅγιον, οὐκ ἐπείπερ ἁπλῶς νοεῖται σὰρξ, ἐν μόνοις οὖσα τοῖς ἰδίοις λόγοις, ἀλλ᾿ ᾗ ἐστι ναὸς τοῦ κατοικοῦντος ἐν αὐτῇ Θεοῦ Λόγου, ἁγιάζοντος τὴν ἰδίαν σάρκα διὰ τοῦ ἰδίου Πνεύματος. Ὥστε, ᾗ φησιν ὁ θεῖος οὗτος ἀνὴρ, κατὰ τὸ ἀληθὲς, οὐ σαρκὸς ἐνέργεια φυσικὴ, καὶ ἀνθρωπότητος δύναμις παραλύει τὸν Σατανᾶν· οὐδὲ τοῦτο ἐνεργεῖ σαρκὸς ἡ φύσις Fr. βουλόμενοι.. ἐπεὶ καὶ πάντες ἄνθρωποι ἐν σαρκὶ ὄντες, καὶ εἷς κατὰ τὸν τῆς ἀνθρωπότητος λόγον, ἑλόμενοι Aliquot male repetitæ lineæ in Venet. Blach. et Dufr. codd. mihi omissæ. ἂν ἐνεργεῖν, καὶ τὴν κατὰ πνευμάτων εἶχον ἰσχύν. Ταῦτα γοῦν Fr. ὑπολαμβάνομεν. διδάξας σαφῶς παρέστηκε, καὶ τῶν ἰδίων ἐρμηνευτὴς γέγονε λόγων, ὡς τῷ ἐνοικεῖν, ὅπερ ἐστὶν ἡνῶσθαι καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν μίαν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τῷ σὰρξ ὑπάρχειν, ἐν μόνοις οὖσα τοῖς ἰδίοις λόγοις, ἁγία ἐστὶ καὶ ζωοποιὸς, κατὰ πάσης νόσου τὴν δύναμιν ἔχουσα. Φυλάξωμεν τοιγαροῦν καὶ ἡμεῖς κατὰ τὴν τῶν ἁγίων ὑφήγησιν, τόν τε τε τῆς διαφορᾶς λόγον ἀλώβητον, τὰς φύσεις αὐτὰς, καὶ τὰ προσόντα κατ᾿ οὐσίαν αὐταῖς διασώζοντα καὶ μετὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν· καὶ τὸν τῆς οἰκονομίας τρόπον παντελῶς ἀδιάσπαστον, τὴν ἄκραν τῶν ἡνωμένων συμφυϊαν ἐνοειδῶς συντηροῦντα· καὶ τὴν πᾶσαν ἡμῶν ἐξελάσωμεν διαίρεσίν τε καὶ σύγχυσιν. Εἰ δὲ μὴ οὕτως τὰς μοναδικῶς εἰρημένας τοῖς ἁγίοις φωνὰς, τήν τε Θεανδρικὴν καὶ τὴν Μίαν τε καὶ συγγενῆ, κατὰ τὸν τῆς ἑνώσεως λόγον εὐσεβῶς ἐκδεξώμεθα καὶ νοήσωμεν, ἀλλ᾿ ἢ ταύτην ὡς φυσικὴν, ἢ πάλιν ὑποστατικὴν ὑπολάβωμεν, φύσιν μίαν ἐξ ἀνάγκης τὸν Χριστὸν δογματίσομεν· μηδετέρας τῶν ἐξ ὧν συνέστηκε τὸ παράπαν μετέχουσαν, ὅτι μηδεμία κατὰ φύσιν ὑπάρχει Χριστός. Φύσις γὰρ πρὸς φύσιν οὐκ ἂν κοινωνήσῃ κατὰ φύσιν ποτ᾿ ἄν· ἀλλ̀ἁμέτοχος, πάντη κατ᾿ οὐσίαν πρὸς πᾶσάν ἐστι διαφορος· τοῦτο μὲν ἡμῖν συναχθήσεται, φυσικὴν τὴν ἐνέργειαν λέγουσι· εἰ δὲ πάλιν ὑποστατικὴν, ἀλλοτριώσομεν αὐτὸν τοῦ Πατρὸς καὶ τοῦ Πνεύματος κατὰ τὴν ἐνέργειαν.

See whether he operates this by the nature of flesh; for we are all in the flesh, and one is the principle of manhood over all; but it is neither the work of flesh specifically, …

108nor indeed of manhood, to prevail over spirits, since not all have the strength; rather, the achievement is seen to be of an operation through the Spirit.» So the Father has spoken truly, insofar as flesh and man is understood, that the strength against the demons was the work of the Spirit, and not of the flesh, that is, of manhood. For the body of the Lord is admittedly holy, and has all power against every disease; but it was, and is, holy, not because it is understood simply as flesh, being in its own principles alone, but inasmuch as it is the temple of God the Word who dwells in it, sanctifying his own flesh through his own Spirit.

So that, as this divine man says, in truth it is not the natural operation of flesh and the power of manhood that undoes Satan; nor does the nature of flesh operate this; since all men too, being in the flesh, and one according to the principle of manhood, would, had they chosen to operate, have had the strength against spirits as well. Having taught these things, then, he has clearly established, and has become the interpreter of his own words, that it is by indwelling, which is to be united according to one hypostasis, and not by being flesh, being in its own principles alone, that it is holy and life-giving, having power against every disease. Let us too, then, following the guidance of the saints, keep both the principle of the difference unimpaired, preserving the natures themselves, and what belongs to them according to essence, even after the union; and the mode of the economy wholly indissoluble, maintaining in unitary fashion the utmost growing-together of the things united; and let us drive from ourselves all division and confusion.

But if we do not thus piously receive and understand, according to the principle of the union, the expressions spoken singularly by the saints, the theandric and the «one and kindred», but suppose this operation either as natural, or again as hypostatic, we shall of necessity teach as doctrine that Christ is one nature, partaking at all in neither of those of which he consists, since Christ is according to nature none of them. For a nature could never have communion with a nature according to nature; but, without participation, it is altogether different in essence from every other. This, then, will follow for us if we call the operation natural; but if again hypostatic, we shall alienate him from the Father and the Spirit according to the operation.

Οὐδενὶ γὰρ χαρακτηριστικῷ 109τῆς ἰδίας ὑποστάσεως, κοινωνήσοι Fr. κοινωνήσει. ποτ᾿ ἂν Πατρὶ καὶ Πνεύματι ὁ Υἱός. Μηδαμῶς τοιγαροῦν βουληθῶμεν φυσικὴν ἢ ὑποστατικὴν ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῦ τοῦ σαρκωθέντος Λόγου, καὶ τελείως ἐνανθρωπήσαντος ὁμολογεῖν τὴν ἐνέργειαν. Οὐδεὶς γὰρ τῶν ἀπὸ τῆς θείας αὐτοῦ πρὸς ἡμᾶς καταβάσεως μέχρι νῦν ἁγίων Πατέρων, φυσικὴν ἢ ὑποστατικὴν ὁμολογεῖν ἐνέργειαν ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῦ παραδέδωκεν· ἀλλ᾿ οὐδὲ μίαν ῥητῶς Dionysius enim καινήν dixit, novam, non μίαν, unam., ὡς ἐμὲ γινώσκειν, ἢ μόνον Κύριλλος ὁ θεσπέσιος· ἣν ὥσπερ ὁλοψύχως δεχόμεθα, καίπερ αὐτοῦ μόνου τυγχάνουσαν, τῆς ἑνώσεως χάριν· πῶς οὐχὶ καὶ τὰς τῶν ὅλων σχεδὸν ἁγίων Πατέρων, καὐτοῦ δὲ πάλιν δέχεσθαι περιχαρῶς προσήκει δυϊκὰς φωνὰς, τῆς φυσικῆς ἕνεκεν διαφορᾶς· ἵνα μὴ τῆς περὶ τὴν μίαν καὶ μόνον αὐτοῦ λέξιν προσκλήσει καὶ παραδοχῇ, τούτου τε καὶ ἄλλων ἅγίων τὴν ἐκβολὴν καὶ τὴν ἀκυρωσίαν τῶν λόγων ποιήσωμεν· ὡς μὴ ἄλλου τινὸς αὐτῶν, ἢ μόνου Κυρίλλου τὴν εὐσεβῆ τοῦ κηρύγματος πιστευθέντος ἀκρίβειαν· καὶ ταύτην οὐκ ἐν τοῖς ἄλλοις λόγοις αὐτοῦ, τοῖς τὴν διαφορὰν δηλοῦσιν, ἀλλ᾿ ἢ μόνον ἐν τῇ μίᾳ καὶ συγγενεῖ ἐνεργείᾳ. Ἄλλως τε, πῶς μίαν ἁπλῶς ἐπὶ Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ τινές φασι τὴν ἐνέργειαν; Ἀνάγκη γὰρ ἢ τῶν αὐτοῦ ταύτην ὑπάρχειν μερῶν, ἢ αὐτοῦ πάντως ὡς ὅλου. Ἀλλ᾿ εἰ μὲν τῶν μερῶν, μερισθήσεται πρὸς ἄμφω δηλαδὴ τεμνομένη· τοῦτο δὲ πάσχουσα, λυθήσεται πάντως, καὶ συνδιαλύσει τὰ κατ᾿ αὐτὴν ἡνωμένα. Εἰ δὲ τοῦ ὅλου, τῶν ἐναντίων ἔσται δεκτικὴ, θνητὴ καὶ ἀθάνατος οὖσα, κτιστὴ καὶ ἄκτιστος, περιγραπτὴ καὶ ἀπερίγραφος. Καὶ εἰ τοῦτο, καθ᾿ ἕξιν καὶ στέρησιν οὖσαν καὶ μὴ οὖσαν τὴν οἰκείαν ὁ Χριστὸς εἶχεν ἐνέργειαν· δι᾿ ἣν, ὡς κατ᾿ αὐτὴν προσφυῶς ἐνεργῶν, τῶν ἐναντίων κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ δεκτικός· ἀλλ᾿ οὐ κατ᾿ ἄλλο καὶ ἄλλο· οὗ τί δυσσεβέστερον; Το αὐτὸ δὲ καὶ περὶ θελήματος ἔστιν εἰπεῖν. Πῶς δὲ ἄρα συνεργάτιν τὴν οἰκείαν ὁ Λόγος παρελάμβανε σάρκα, κατὰ Κύριλλον τὸν σοφὸν, οὐκ ἔχουσαν τὴν κατὰ φύσιν ἐνέργειαν; Τὸ γὰρ μὴ ἔχον ἐνέργειαν μήτε κίνησιν φυσικὴν, νεκρὸν πάντως καὶ ἄψυχον· καὶ τίνι λόγῳ προσίετο τὸ νεκρὸν εἰς συνέργειαν; Τὸ γὰρ ἀνενέργητον, τῇ παντελεῖ ἀκινησίᾳ καταργεῖ. Πῶς δὲ ταύτην ἐδείκνυε ζωοποιεῖν δυναμένην, ὡς αὐτὸς πάλιν Κύριλλος ἐξεπαίδευσε, διὰ τῶν κατὰ φύσιν αὐτῆς κινημάτων· οἷον τῆς ἁφῆς, τῆς φωνῆς, τοῦ περιπάτου, τούτων οὐκ ὄντων αὐτῆς, ἢ ἐν αὐτῇ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν. Εἰ γὰρ αὐτῆς κατ᾿ οὐσίαν, φυσικὴν πάντως ἡ σὰρξ εἶχεν ἐνέργειαν· ἐνεργείας γὰρ αὐτῆς οὐσιώδους ἐστὶν, ἤ τε κίνησις τῶν χειρῶν καὶ τῶν ποδῶν, καὶ ἡ κατὰ τὴν προφορὰν φωνή.

For in nothing characteristic 109of his own hypostasis could the Son ever have communion with the Father and the Spirit.

Let us by no means, then, wish to confess the operation in the very Word incarnate, and perfectly become man, to be natural or hypostatic. For none of the holy Fathers, from his divine descent to us until now, has handed down that we confess a natural or hypostatic operation in him; nay, not even «one» expressly, so far as I know, except only the divine Cyril. And as we receive this wholeheartedly, although it belongs to him alone, for the sake of the union, how is it not fitting also to receive joyfully the dual expressions of almost all the holy Fathers, and again of himself, for the sake of the natural difference? Lest, by our appeal to and acceptance of his one and only word, we bring about the casting-out and the invalidation of the words of himself and of the other saints; as though none other of them but Cyril alone had been entrusted with the pious exactness of the proclamation; and he not in his other words, those which show the difference, but only in the «one and kindred operation».

Besides, how do some say that the operation in Christ God is simply one? For it must of necessity belong either to his parts, or to him altogether as a whole. But if to the parts, it will be partitioned, being cut, that is, toward both; and suffering this, it will be dissolved altogether, and will dissolve with itself the things united according to it. But if to the whole, it will be receptive of contraries, being mortal and immortal, created and uncreated, circumscribed and uncircumscribed. And if this, Christ had his own operation by habit and privation, being and not being; through which, as operating fittingly according to it, he is receptive of contraries in the same respect, and not in one respect and another: than which what is more impious?

The same may also be said concerning the will. And how then did the Word take his own flesh as a fellow-worker, according to Cyril the wise, if it did not have the operation according to nature? For what has neither operation nor natural movement is altogether dead and soulless; and for what reason would he admit the dead into co-operation? For what is without operation lies idle in total immobility. And how did he show it able to give life, as Cyril himself again taught, through its motions according to nature, such as touch, voice, walking, if these were not its own, or in it according to essence? For if they were its own according to essence, the flesh altogether had a natural operation; for the movement of the hands and of the feet, and the voice in utterance, belong to its essential operation.

Εἰ δὲ 112οὐκ αὐτῆς κατὰ φύσιν ταῦτα, ζωοποιεῖν δυναμένην ταύτην ὁ Σωτὴρ οὐκ ἀπέδειξε. Ταῦτα μικρὰ ἐκ πολλῶν, ὑμῶν τε καὶ τῶν καθ᾿ ὑμᾶς θεοσόφων ἁγίων παιδευθεὶς διδαγμάτων, διὰ τὴν ἱερωτάτην ὑμῶν κέλευσιν, ἐθάῤῥησα διὰ τοῦδε τοῦ μετρίου γράμματος χαράξαι, καὶ τῶν ὑπὲρ δύναμιν κατατολμῆσαι, μικρὸς ὢν καὶ εὐτελής· καὶ μήτε νοῦν εὔστοχον εἰς ἐπιβολὴν, μήτε λόγον εὔεικτον εἰς σαφήνειαν, μήτε ἦθος εὔκοσμον εἰς τὴν τῶν κρειττόνων ἐξάνεσιν ἔχων· ἀλλὰ μᾶλλον χάριτι, διὰ τῆς μεσιτείας ὑμῶν σκεπαζόμενος ὑπὸ τοῦ παναγάθου Θεοῦ, καὶ καλύπτοντος τὰ πλήθη τῶν ἐμῶν ἀμαρτημάτων. Δυσωπῶ δὲ μετὰ δακρύων, τοῖς τιμίοις ὑμῶν ὡς παρὼν προκαλινδούμενος ἴχνεσιν, ἐπειδὴ ὑμᾶς κατέστησε τὸ Πνεῦμα τὸ ἅγιον ἐπισκόπους ψυχῶν καὶ σωτῆρας, μὴ κατοκνῆσαι ἐν οἷς οὐκ ἠκριβωμένως γέγραφα, φιλάγαθον ποιήσασθαι τὴν διόρθωσιν· ἵν᾿ ἐκ τούτου μεσίτας Θεοῦ μᾶλλον, ἢ μιμητὰς ἑαυτοὺς ἀναδείξηται· τὸν ἐπὶ πάντας αὐτοῦ φωτισμὸν, καὶ τὸ ἔλεος· οὐ μόνον ἕξει λαχόντες, ἀλλὰ καὶ ἀξίᾳ παρέχοντες· ᾧ καὶ παραθέσθαι τὴν ἐμὴν τοῦ πένητος καὶ πτωχοῦ ἀσθένειαν καθικετεύω, πρὸς τὸ σκεπασθῆναι τῆς τὸ πᾶν νεμομένης διὰ τὰς ἐμὰς ἁμαρτίας, ὁρατῆς τε καὶ ἀοράτου, φλογός· καὶ εἰς τὴν δρόσον τῆς τε παναγίας αὐτοῦ καὶ ὀρθοδόξου πίστεως, καὶ τῆς ἱερᾶς ἀρετῆς διὰ παντὸς ἐπαναπαύεσθαι καὶ φρουρεῖσθαι. Ἀμήν.

ΣΧΟΛΙΟΝ. Εr. Ἴδε σοι τὸ ἄπορον λέλυται.

But if 112these are not its own according to nature, the Saviour did not show it able to give life.

These small things out of many, having been taught by the teachings of you and of the God-wise saints like you, I have made bold, at your most sacred bidding, to inscribe through this modest letter, and to venture on things beyond my power, being small and of no account; and having neither a mind apt in its aim for grasping, nor speech pliant for clarity, nor a well-ordered character for the accomplishing of the better things; but rather being sheltered by grace, through your mediation, by the all-good God, who also covers the multitudes of my sins. And I entreat you with tears, falling down, as though present, before your honoured footsteps, since the Holy Spirit has made you overseers and saviours of souls, not to shrink from making a kindly correction where I have written without exactness; so that from this you may show yourselves mediators of God rather than imitators, not only having obtained by habit his illumination upon all, and his mercy, but also furnishing it by dignity.
