Neque enim tale quid sacra regula eum vocari permittit, A cuncta enim cecidit. sanctitate, qui nimirum ab Ecclesia catholica sponte prosiliit. Non enim fas est illum ex quacungue laude cognominari, qui jam olim damnatus est et abjectus ab apostolica sede Romanæ urbis, ob externæ sensum opinionis, donec ab ea recipiatur conversus ad ipsam; imo ad Dominum Deum nostrum per piam confessionem et orthodoxam fidem, qua sanctificationem recipiat, sanctumque vocabulum.

Itaque si vult hereticus non esse, neque audire, non isti aut illi satisfaciat ; superfluum quippe hoc et irrationabile est : quia sicut uno contra eum scandalizato omnes scandalizati sunt; ita quoque uno satisfacto, omnes procul dubio satisfiunt. Festinet pro omnibus sedi Romanæ satisfacere. Hac enim satisfacta, communiter ubique omnes pium hunc. et orthodoxum predicabunt. Nam frustra solummodo loquitur, qui mihi similes suadendos putat, et non satisfacit el implorat sanctissimæ Romanorum Ecclesi beatissimam papam, id est, apostolicam sedem, qua ab ipso incarnato Dei Verbo, sed et omnibus sanctis synodis, secundum sacros canones et terminos, universarum, qua in toto terrarum orbe sunt, sanctarum Dei Ecclesiarum in omnihus et per omnia percepit et habet imperium, auctoritatem et potestatem ligandi et solvendi. Cum bac enim ligat et solvit eliam in coelo Verbum, quod cœlestibus virtutibus principatur. Si enim alios quidem satisfaciendos ducit, et beatissimum Romanum papam nequaquam implorat, simile, quiddam agit ei, qui forte homicidii, vel alterius cujusdam criminis redarguitur, et insontem se, non ei, quisecundum leges judicandi jura sortitus est, exhibere festinat : sed tantum inutiliter et sine lucro aliis et privatis hominibus munditiam monstrare sui satagit actus, qui nullam habeant se solvendi a crimine potestatem.

Quapropter, benedicte mi domine, adhuc. magis extende præceptionem, quæ bene vobis et secundum placitum Dci visa fuisse dignoscitur, quo nemini licentiam habeat iste quidquam loqui et eloqui dogmatis causa.

But they, despising the Lord's respite, and those who kindly exhorted them to conversion, and remaining torpid under the supplication made by these excellent men through so long a time, by no means bore to prefer the heavenly faith to their own.

After some things:

The divine and great Sophronius, then, coming at that time to Alexandria, at once upon the first reading (for Cyrus had given him too those nine chapters of impiety to review), crying out something mournful and great, poured forth fountains of tears, fervently beseeching him, supplicating, remonstrating, thrown down at his feet on the pavement, that he should proclaim none of these things from the ambo against the catholic Church of God; since these were plainly the doctrines of the impious Apollinarius.

And after a little:

In such a way, O head venerable to me before all, the men were tearing the divine things with insults hateful to God, and with, as it were, theatrical mockeries among themselves; and the most sacred Sophronius, who lamented so piteously, as one weeping, after the manner of a second Jeremiah, for the breaking of the catholic Church, and deploring very well and most patiently so great a fall of the divine doctrines, 144they in no way consoled …

And after some things:

For if the Roman see knows full well that Pyrrhus is not only reprobate but also wrong in mind and wrong in belief, it is surely plain that everyone who anathematizes those who have rejected Pyrrhus anathematizes the Roman see, that is, the catholic Church. For I omit to say that he anathematizes himself too, he who is such, if indeed he communicates with the Roman see and the catholic Church of God. I beseech thee, then, my blessed lord, to command all not to call Pyrrhus most holy or Almificus. For the sacred rule does not permit him to be called any such thing. For he has fallen from all holiness who has, of his own accord, leapt out of the catholic Church. For it is not right that he be surnamed with any praise whatever, who has long since been condemned and cast out by the apostolic see of the city of Rome on account of the sense of a foreign opinion, until he be received by it, converted to it, or rather to the Lord our God, through pious confession and orthodox faith, by which he may receive sanctification and the name of holy.

So if he wishes not to be a heretic, nor to be called one, let him not satisfy this man or that; for this is superfluous and unreasonable; since, just as when one was scandalized against him all were scandalized, so also when one is satisfied, all without doubt are satisfied. Let him hasten before all to satisfy the Roman see. For when it is satisfied, all everywhere in common will proclaim him pious and orthodox. For he speaks only in vain who thinks that men like me are to be persuaded, and does not satisfy and implore the most blessed pope of the most holy Church of the Romans, that is, the apostolic see; which from the incarnate Word of God himself, and also from all the holy synods, according to the sacred canons and definitions, has received and holds, over all the holy Churches of God that are in the whole world, in all things and through all things, the dominion, authority and power of binding and loosing. For with it the Word, who rules over the heavenly powers, binds and looses also in heaven. For if he thinks that others are to be satisfied, and by no means implores the most blessed Roman pope, he does something like one who, accused perhaps of homicide or of some other crime, hastens to show himself innocent not to him who has by the laws obtained the right of judging, but, only uselessly and without profit, busies himself to show the cleanness of his conduct to others and to private men, who have no power to loose him from the charge.

Wherefore, my blessed lord, extend still more the order which is known to have seemed good to thee and according to the good pleasure of God, so that this man may have licence to say or utter nothing to anyone for the sake of doctrine.

Sed liquido -discite voluntatem hujus abundantius investigantes, si velit. annuere 145penitus veritati : et si hoc agere studuerit, et ad id secundum rationem accelerat, hortamini eum suggestionem convenienter facere ad beatissimum papam Romanum : quatenus sic per illius divinam

But learn clearly his will, investigating it more abundantly, whether he is willing to assent 145wholly to the truth; and if he has been zealous to do this, and hastens to it according to reason, exhort him to make a petition fittingly to the most blessed Roman pope; so that thus, by his divine

146præceptionem, regulariter, quæ circa ipsum sunt, et decentissime moderetur ad gloriam Dei, et vestræ laudem sublimitatis.

146order, the things concerning him may be ordered according to the rule and most becomingly, to the glory of God and the praise of thy sublimity.

145ΤΟΥ ΑΓΙΟΥ ΜΑΞΙΜΟΥ Περὶ τῶν δύο τοῦ Χριστοῦ φύσεων.

αʹ. Ὁ Ἄρειος τὰς τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις ὁμολογεῖ· ἀλλὰ τὴν Μονάδα ἀρνεῖται· καὶ οὐ λέγει ὁμοούσιον τὴν ἁγίαν Τριάδα. Ὁ δὲ Σαβέλλιος, τὴν Μονάδα ὁμολογεῖ· ἀλλὰ τὴν Τριάδα ἀρνεῖται· τὸν γὰρ αὐτὸν λέγει Πατέρα καὶ Υἱὸν καὶ ἅγιον Πνεῦμα· ἡ δὲ Ἑκκλησία τοῦ Θεοῦ, καὶ τὴν Μονάδα Fr. καὶ Μονάδα καὶ Τριάδα. ὁμολογεῖ, καὶ τὴν Τριάδα κηρύττει. Ὁ Μακεδόνιος ὅμοια τῷ Ἀρείῳ πρεσβεύει· τὸ γὰρ ἅγιον Πνεῦμα, κτίσμα ὑποτίθησιν· ἡ δὲ Ἐκκλησία, ὁμοούσιον τῷ Πατρὶ καὶ τῷ Υἱῷ τὸ Πνεῦμα τὸ ἅγιον ἀνακηρύττει, καὶ Θεὸν πῶς διαβεβαιοῖ. Ὁμοίως καὶ ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἀγίας Τριάδος, Νεστόριος τὴν φυσικὴν διαφορὰν λέγει· ἀλλὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν οὐχ ὁμολογεῖ, οὐ γὰρ λέγει ταύτην καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν γεγονέναι. Ὁ δὲ Εὐτυχὴς, τὴν μὲν ἕνωσιν ὁμολογεῖ· τὴν δὲ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν διαφορὰν ἀρνεῖται, καὶ σύγχυσιν τῶν φύσεων εἰσάγει. Ἡ δὲ Ἐκκλησία, καὶ τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν διὰ τὸ ἀδιαίρετον, καὶ τὴν κατ᾿ οὐσίαν διαφορὰν διὰ τὸ ἀσύγχυτον πρεσβεύει.

βʹ. Πῶς ἡ ἄκρα ἕνωσις καὶ ταυτότητα ἔχει, καὶ ἑτερότητα; Ἢ ταυτότητα οὐσιῶν καὶ ἑτερότητα προσώπων, καὶ τὸ ἔμπαλιν. Οἷον ἐπὶ τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος, ταυτότης μέν ἐστιν οὐσίας· ἑτερότης δὲ προσώπων· μίαν γὰρ οὐσίαν ὁμολογοῦμεν· τρεῖς δὲ ὑποστάσεις. Ἐπὶ δὲ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου, ταυτότης μὲν ἐστι προσώπου· ἑτερότης δὲ οὐσιῶν· ἑνὸς γὰρ ὄντος ἀνθρώπου, ἄλλης οὐσίας ἐστὶν ἡ ψυχὴ, καὶ ἄλλης ἡ σάρξ Fr. τὸ σῶμα, ut et Turr..

145OPUSCULUM 13 — ON THE TWO NATURES OF CHRIST

Of the holy Maximus. On the two natures of Christ.

1. Arius confesses the three hypostases, but denies the Monad, and does not call the holy Trinity consubstantial. Sabellius confesses the Monad, but denies the Trinity; for he calls the same one Father and Son and Holy Spirit. But the Church of God both confesses the Monad and proclaims the Trinity. Macedonius holds things like Arius; for he supposes the Holy Spirit a creature; but the Church proclaims the Holy Spirit consubstantial with the Father and the Son, and affirms him [?] God.

Likewise also in the case of the one of the holy Trinity, Nestorius speaks of the natural difference, but does not confess the union; for he does not say that it has come about according to hypostasis. Eutyches confesses the union, but denies the difference according to essence, and introduces confusion of the natures. But the Church holds both the union according to hypostasis, on account of the undividedness, and the difference according to essence, on account of the unconfusedness.

2. How does the utmost union have both identity and otherness? Either identity of essences and otherness of persons, or the reverse. For example, in the case of the holy Trinity there is identity of essence, but otherness of persons; for we confess one essence, but three hypostases. In the case of man there is identity of person, but otherness of essences; for while man is one, the soul is of one essence and the flesh of another.

Ὁμοίως δὲ καὶ ἐπὶ τοῦ Δεσπόπου Χριστοῦ· ταυτότης μέν ἐστι, προσώπου· ἑτερότης δὲ, οὐσιῶν· ἑνὸς γὰρ ὄντος προσώπου, ἤτοι ὑποστάσεως, ἑτέρας οὐσίας 148ἐστὶν ἡ θεότης, καὶ ἑτέρας ἡ ἀνθρωπότης. Ὥσπερ γὰρ ἀδύνατον τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος ὁμολογεῖν μὲν τὴν ἕνωσιν, μὴ ἐκφωνεῖν δὲ τὴν διαφοράν· οὔτως ἀνάγκη πᾶσα ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος, καὶ τὴν ἕνωσιν καὶ τὴν διαφορὰν κηρύττειν.

γʹ. Ὥσπερ γὰρ οὐ διὰ τῶν αὐτῶν σημαίνεται λέξεων, ἤ τε διαφορὰ καὶ ἡ ἕνωσις ἐπὶ τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος· ἀλλὰ διὰ τοῦ μὲν λέγειν τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις, ἡ διαφορά· διὰ τοῦ ὁμολογεῖν δὲ μίαν οὐσίαν, ἡ ἕνωσις ὁμολογεῖται· οὕτω καὶ ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος· διὰ μὲν τοῦ γνωρίζειν τὰς δύο φύσεις, ἡ διαφορά· διὰ δὲ τοῦ κηρύττειν μίαν ὑπόστασιν σύνθετον, ἡ ἕνωσις ὁμολογεῖται. δʹ. Ὥσπερ γὰρ Ἄρειον ἀναθεματίζομεν, οὐχ ὡς κηρύττοντα Tilian. et Fr. οὐ κηρύττον: et sic in aliis; Turr. deest negans particula. ἐπὶ τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν διαφοράν· ἀλλ᾿ ὡς μὴ λέγοντα τὴν φυσικὴν ἕνωσιν· οὕτω καὶ Νεστόριον ἀναθεματίζομεν, οὐχ ὡς γνωρίζοντα τὴν φυσικὴν διαφορὰν ἐπὶ τοῦ Χριστοῦ· ἀλλ᾿ ὡς μὴ λέγοντα τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν.

εʹ. Ὥσπερ Σαβέλλιον ἀναθεματίζομεν, οὐχ ὡς κηρύττοντα ἐπὶ τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος τὴν φυσικὴν ἕνωσιν· ἀλλ᾿ ὡς μὴ λέγοντα τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν διαφοράν· οὕτως Εὐτυχέα ἀναθεματίζομεν, οὐχ ὡς μὴ λέγοντα τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν ἐπὶ τοῦ Χριστοῦ, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς μὴ γνωρίζοντα τὴν φυσικὴν διαφοράν.

ϛʹ. Τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν διαφορὰν ἐπὶ τῆς ἀγίας Τριάδος, καὶ τὴν φυσικὴν διαφορὰν ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἀγίας Τριάδος, οὐκ ἐν αἰσθήσει χρὴ λέγειν, ἀλλὰ νοῆσαι τοῖς τῆς διανοίας ὄμμασιν. Καὶ πῶς, ἐπὶ μὲν τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος ἐκφωνεῖτε τὰς τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις διὰ τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν διαφοράν· ἐπὶ δὲ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος οὐκ ἐκφωνεῖτε δύο φύσεις ἐν μιᾷ ὑποστάσει, διὰ τὴν φυσικὴν διαφοράν;

ζʹ. Ὥσπερ διὰ τὸ ὁμοούσιον τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος, μίαν οὐσίαν· καὶ διὰ τὸ ἑτεροὑπόστατον τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις λέγεις· οὕτω διὰ τὸ ἑτεροούσιον τοῦ Λόγου καὶ τῆς σαρκὸς, δύο οὐσίας· καὶ διὰ τὸ μὴ ἰδιοῦπόστατον, μίαν ὑπόστασιν λέγε.

ηʹ.

Likewise also in the case of the Master Christ: there is identity of person, but otherness of essences; for while the person, that is, the hypostasis, is one, 148the Godhead is of one essence and the manhood of another. For just as it is impossible in the case of the holy Trinity to confess the union but not to utter the difference, so there is every necessity, in the case of the one of the holy Trinity, to proclaim both the union and the difference.

3. For just as the difference and the union are not signified by the same words in the case of the holy Trinity, but by saying three hypostases the difference, and by confessing one essence the union, is confessed; so also in the case of the one of the holy Trinity: by acknowledging the two natures the difference, and by proclaiming one composite hypostasis the union, is confessed.

4. For just as we anathematize Arius, not as proclaiming in the holy Trinity the difference according to hypostasis, but as not saying the natural union; so also we anathematize Nestorius, not as acknowledging the natural difference in Christ, but as not saying the union according to hypostasis.

5. Just as we anathematize Sabellius, not as proclaiming in the holy Trinity the natural union, but as not saying the difference according to hypostasis; so we anathematize Eutyches, not as not saying the union according to hypostasis in Christ, but as not acknowledging the natural difference.

6. The difference according to hypostasis in the holy Trinity, and the natural difference in the one of the holy Trinity, one must not speak of as in sense, but understand with the eyes of the understanding. And how is it that in the case of the holy Trinity you utter the three hypostases on account of the difference according to hypostasis, but in the case of the one of the holy Trinity you do not utter two natures in one hypostasis, on account of the natural difference?

7. Just as on account of the consubstantiality of the holy Trinity thou sayest one essence, and on account of the being of other hypostasis three hypostases; so on account of the being of other essence of the Word and the flesh, say thou two essences, and on account of their not being each of its own hypostasis, one hypostasis.

8.

Ὥσπερ ἐπὶ τῆς ἀγίας Τριάδος τὴν μίαν οὐσίαν, οὐκ ἐπὶ συγχύσει τῶν τριῶν ὑποστάσεων λέγομεν· οὔτε τὰς τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις ἐπὶ ἀναιρέσει τῆς μιᾶς οὐσίας· οὕτως ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος, 149τὴν μίαν ὑπόστασιν, οὐκ ἐπὶ συγχύσει τῶν δύο φύσεων αὐτοῦ λέγομεν· οὔτε τὰς δύο φύσεις, ἐπὶ διαιρέσει τῆς μιᾶς ὑποστάσεως.

θʹ. Ὁ μὴ λέγων ἐπὶ Χριστοῦ διὰ τὴν τῶν φύσεων διαφορὰν τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν, Νεστοριανός ἐστι· καὶ ὁ μὴ λέγων ἐν τῇ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἑνώσει τὴν φυσικὴν διαφορὰν, Εὐτυχιανιστής ἐστι· ὁ δὲ καὶ τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν, καὶ τὴν φυσικὴν διαφορὰν κηρύττων ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος, τὴν βασιλικὴν καὶ ἀμώμητον πίστιν κρατεῖ.

ιʹ. Ὁ τοίνυν λέγων καὶ διαφορὰν καὶ ἕνωσιν ἐπὶ τοῦ Χριστοῦ, οὔτε τὴν διαφορὰν ἀναιρεῖ, οὔτε τὴν ἕνωσιν συγχέει. Καὶ γὰρ ὁ θεῖος Κύριλλος Tilian. ὁ μακάριος. Fr. et Turr. καὶ γὰρ Κύριλλος ἀναθεματίζει τοὺς διὰ τὴν διαφορὰν ἀναιροῦντας τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν· καὶ ἡ οἰκουμενικὴ σύνοδος ἀναθεματίζει τοὺς διὰ τὴν καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἕνωσιν ἀναιροῦντας τὴν φυσικὴν διαφορὰν ἐπὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς ἀγίας Τριάδος.

ΜΑΞΙΜΟΥ ΤΟΥ ΟΜΟΛΟΓΗΤΟΥ. Ὅροι διάφοροι. Οὐσία καὶ φύσις, ταυτόν· ἄμφω γὰρ κοινὸν καὶ καθόλου, ὡς κατὰ πολλῶν καὶ διαφερόντων τῷ ἀριθμῷ κατηγορούμενα, καὶ μήποτε καθοτιοῦν ἑνὶ προσώπῳ περιοριζόμενα. Ἐνυπόστατόν ἐστι, τὸ κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν κοινὸν, ἤγουν τὸ εἶδος, τὸ ἐν τοῖς ὑπ᾿ αὐτὸ ἀτόμοις πραγματικῶς ὑφιστάμενον, καὶ οὐκ ἐπινοίᾳ ψιλῇ θεωρούμενον. Ἄλλως, ἢ πάλιν ἐνυπόστατόν ἐστι, τὸ ἄλλῳ διαφόρῳ κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν εἰς ἑνὸς σύστασιν προσώπου καὶ μιᾶς γένεσιν ὑποστάσεως, συγκείμενόν τε καὶ συνυφιστάμενον, καὶ οὐδαμῶς καθ᾿ αὐτὸ γνωριζόμενον. Ὁμοούσιόν ἐστι τὸ τῆς αὐτῆς οὐσίας ἄλλῳ τυγχάνον, διάφορον δὲ πρὸς τὸ αὐτὸ κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν ὅν· ὡς ἐπί τε ἀνθρώπου καὶ τῶν ἄλλων ἔχει εἰδῶν. Εἴ τι οὖν ἄλλῳ ταυτὸν ὑπάρχει κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν, οὗ ταυτὸν ὑπάρχει τῷ αὐτῷ καὶ κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν· καὶ εἴ τι ἓν καὶ ἡνωμένον ἄλλῳ τυγχάνει κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν, οὐχ ἓν οὔτε ἡνωμένον τῷ αὐτῷ τυγχάνει καὶ κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν· καὶ εἴ τι διαφορὰν ἔχει πρὸς ἄλλο κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν, τοῦτο ἕνωσιν ἔχει πρὸς τὸ αὐτὸ κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν. Οὐσιώδης οὖν ἕνωσίς ἐστιν, ἡ τὰς διαφόρους κατ᾿ ἀριθμὸν καὶ πολλὰς ὑποστάσεις εἰς μίαν καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν οὐσίαν συνάγουσα.

Just as in the case of the holy Trinity we say the one essence not to the confusion of the three hypostases, nor the three hypostases to the destruction of the one essence; so in the case of the one of the holy Trinity 149we say the one hypostasis not to the confusion of his two natures, nor the two natures to the division of the one hypostasis.

9. He who, on account of the difference of the natures, does not say the union according to hypostasis in the case of Christ, is a Nestorian; and he who does not say the natural difference in the union according to hypostasis is a Eutychianist; but he who proclaims both the union according to hypostasis and the natural difference in the one of the holy Trinity holds the royal and blameless faith.

10. He, then, who says both difference and union in the case of Christ neither destroys the difference nor confuses the union. For indeed the divine Cyril anathematizes those who on account of the difference destroy the union according to hypostasis; and the ecumenical synod anathematizes those who on account of the union according to hypostasis destroy the natural difference in the one of the holy Trinity.

OPUSCULUM 14 — VARIOUS DEFINITIONS

Of Maximus the Confessor. Various definitions.

Essence and nature are the same; for both are common and universal, as being predicated of many things differing in number, and never circumscribed in any way whatever by one person.

The enhypostatic is what is common according to the essence, that is, the species, which really subsists in the individuals under it, and is not contemplated by bare conception.

Otherwise, or again: the enhypostatic is what is composed and co-subsists with another, different according to the essence, into the constitution of one person and the coming-to-be of one hypostasis, and is in no way known by itself.

The consubstantial is what is of the same essence as another, but is different from it according to the hypostasis; as is the case with man and with the other species. If then anything is the same as another according to the essence, it is not the same as it also according to the hypostasis; and if anything is one and united with another according to the essence, it is neither one nor united with it also according to the hypostasis; and if anything has difference toward another according to the hypostasis, this has union toward it according to the essence.

Essential union, then, is that which gathers the hypostases that are different in number and many into one and the same essence.

Οὐσιώδης δὲ διαφορὰ τυγχάνει, λόγος καθ᾿ ὃν ἡ οὐσία, ἤγουν φύσις, ἀμείωτός τε καὶ ἄτρεπτος, ἄφυρτος ὁμοῦ καὶ ἀσύγχυτος διαμένει, καὶ τὴν πρὸς ἄλλο κατ᾿ εἶδος ἕτερον εἰς μίαν ὑπόστασιν ποιουμένη σύνοδον, οὐδ᾿ ὅλως τῆς κατὰ φύσιν ἰδίας ἐξίσταται φύσεως. 152Ὑπόστασις καὶ πρόσωπον, ταυτόν· ἄμφω γὰρ μερικόν τε καὶ ἴδιον, ὡς ἐφ᾿ ἑαυτῶν τὴν περιγραφὴν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐκ ἐν πλείοσι τὴν κατηγορίαν φυσικῶς κεκτημένα. Ἐνούσιόν ἐστι τὸ μὴ μόνον ἐνθεωρούμενον ἔχον ἐφ᾿ ἑαυτοῦ τὸ τῶν ἰδιωμάτων ἄθροισμα, καθ᾿ ὃ ἄλλο ἀπ᾿ ἄλλου γνωρίζεται, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὸ κοινὸν τῆς οὐσίας πραγματικῶς κεκτημένον Sic anima et corpus, quæ partiales substantiæ, non ἐνούσια.. Ὁμοῧπόστατόν ἐστι, τὸ εἰς μίαν καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν ὑπόστασιν ἄλλῳ συντεθειμένον· διάφορον δὲ πρὸς τὸ αὐτὸ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν τυγχάνον· ὡς ἐπί τε ψυχῆς καὶ σώματος ἔχει, καὶ τῶν ἄλλων ὅσα φύσεως ἑτερότητι διαφέροντα, καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἥνωται. Εἴ τι οὖν ἄλλῳ καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν συντεθὲν, ταυτὸν ὑπάρχει κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν, τουτέστιν, ὑπόστασις μετ᾿ αὐτοῦ γέγονε μία, ἕτερον τῷ αὐτῷ κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν ἐστί. Καὶ εἴ τι ἓν καὶ ἡνωμένον ἄλλῳ τυγχάνει κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν, τοῦτο οὐχ ἓν οὔτε ὁμόγενές ἐστι τῷ αὐτῷ καὶ κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν. Καὶ εἴ τι συντεθειμένον ἄλλῳ διαφορὰν ἔχει κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν, τοῦτο ἕνωσιν ἔχει πρὸς τὸ αὐτὸ κατὰ τὴν ὑπόστασιν. Ὑποστατικὴ οὖν ἕνωσίς ἐστιν, ἡ τὰς διαφόρους οὐσίας ἤγουν φύσεις εἰς ἓν πρόσωπον, καὶ μίαν καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν ὑπόστασιν συνάγουσά τε καὶ συνδέουσα. Ὑποστατικὴ δὲ διαφορὰ, τυγχάνει λόγος, καθ᾿ ὃν ἡ κατὰ τὸ ἄθροισμα τῶν ἐνθεωρουμένων ἰδιωμάτων τῷ κοινῷ τῆς οὐσίας ἑτερότης, τέμνουσα κατ᾿ ἀριθμὸν ἄλλον ἀπ᾿ ἄλλου, τὴν τῶν ἀτόμων ποιεῖται πληθύν. Σχετικὴ ἕνωσίς ἐστιν, ἡ τὰς διαφόρους γνώμας εἰς ἓν συνάγουσα θέλημα. Σχετικὴ δὲ διαφορά ἐστιν, ἡ τὸ ἓν θέλημα γνώμης ἑτερότητι διατέμνουσα κίνησις. Σχετικὴ ἕνωσίς ἐστι, φιλικὴ συνδιάθεσις, ἢ θελημάτων ὁμονοητικὴ σύμπνοια καὶ ἐπίνευσις. Σχετικὴ διαφορά ἐστι, γνώμης ἑτερότης, ἢ θελημάτων ἀνομοιότης, ἢ διαίρεσις διαθέσεως. Συγχυτικὴ ἕνωσίς ἐστιν, ἡ ἐξ ὑποστάσεων εἰς ὅλου τινὸς κατὰ σύνοδον γινομένη σύνθεσις, ἥτις τὴν τῶν μερῶν ἑαυτῆς μετὰ τὴν ἕνωσιν ἐν εἴδει καὶ σχήματι, σωζομένην οὐκ ἔχει τὸ σύνολον ὕπαρξιν· ἀλλ᾿ ἠφανισμένην ὁμοῦ καὶ πεφυρμένην καὶ ἄγνωστον, καὶ τῶν κατ᾿ αὐτὴν συνδεδραμηκότων οὐδεμίαν οὐδαμῶς κέκτηται διαφορὰν, τὴν οὐσιώδη τούτων ἰδιότητα διασημαίνουσαν. Διαίρεσίς ἐστι, τομὴ διαμπὰξ ἀνὰ μέρος τιθεῖσά τε καὶ χωρίζουσα καὶ καθ᾿ ἑαυτὴν θεωρεῖσθαι παρασκευάζουσα, κατά τε οὐσίαν καὶ ὑπόστασιν, τὴν ἑκάστου πράγματος ὕπαρξιν. Ὑποστάσεως ἴδιόν ἐστι, τὸ καθ᾿ ἑαυτὴν ὁρᾶσθαι, καὶ τῶν ὁμοειδῶν κατ᾿ ἀριθμὸν διαστέλλεσθαι.

Essential difference is the principle according to which the essence, that is, the nature, remains undiminished and unchangeable, at once uncommixed and unconfused, and, making concourse into one hypostasis with another different in species, in no way departs from its own nature according to nature.

152Hypostasis and person are the same; for both are particular and proper, as having naturally their circumscription upon themselves, but not their predication in many.

The enousial is that which not only has upon itself, contemplated in it, the aggregate of the idioms by which one is known from another, but also really possesses the common [element] of the essence.

Of one hypostasis is that which is composed with another into one and the same hypostasis, but is different from it according to essence; as is the case with soul and body, and with the other things which, differing by otherness of nature, are united according to hypostasis. If then anything composed with another according to union is the same according to the hypostasis, that is, has become one hypostasis with it, it is other than it according to the essence. And if anything is one and united with another according to the hypostasis, this is neither one nor of the same genus with it also according to the essence. And if anything composed with another has difference according to the essence, this has union toward it according to the hypostasis.

Hypostatic union, then, is that which gathers and binds together the different essences, that is, natures, into one person and one and the same hypostasis. Hypostatic difference is the principle according to which the otherness, according to the aggregate of the idioms contemplated in the common [element] of the essence, cutting one from another in number, makes the multitude of the individuals.

Relational union is that which gathers the different inclinations into one will. Relational difference is the movement that cuts asunder the one will by otherness of inclination.

Relational union is a friendly co-disposition, or a concordant breathing-together and assent of wills. Relational difference is otherness of inclination, or unlikeness of wills, or division of disposition.

Confusing union is the composition that comes about by concourse out of hypostases into some whole, which does not at all have the existence of its own parts preserved, after the union, in form and figure, but effaced at once, commixed and unknown, and possesses in no way any difference of the things that have run together in it, signifying their essential property.

Division is a cut right through, which sets apart and separates, and causes to be contemplated by itself, both according to essence and hypostasis, the existence of each thing.

It is proper to hypostasis to be seen by itself, and to be set apart in number from the things of the same species.

Ἐνὑποστάτου ἴδιόν ἐστι, ἢ τὸ μετ᾿ ἄλλου διαφόρου κατὰ 153τὴν οὐσίαν ἐν ὑποστάσει γνωρίζεσθαι καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν ἄλυτον· ἢ τὸ ἐν ἀτόμοις φυσικῶς τυγχάνειν καθ᾿ ὕπαρξιν. Οὐσιας ἴδιον, τὸ ἐν πᾶσι καὶ πάντων φυσικῶς κατηγορεῖσθαί τε καὶ προφαίνεσθαι. Ἐνουσίου ἴδιον, τὸ πραγματικῶς ὑφίστασθαι, καὶ φέρειν ἁπάντων ἐφ᾿ ἑαυτοῦ τῶν προσωπικῶν ἰδιωμάτων τοὺς ἀληθεῖς χαρακτῆρας. Δύναμίς ἐστιν, ἔνυλος ἐνέργεια· ἐνέργεια, ἄϋλος Turr. ac si λόγος δὲ, ἄϋλος, ratio vero. δύναμις. Ἢ πάλιν, ἐνέργειά ἐστι δυνάμεως φυσικῆς ἀποτέλεσμα. Θέλημα φυσικόν ἐστιν, οὐσιώδης τῶν κατὰ φύσιν συστατικῶν ἔφεσις. Θέλημα γνωμικόν ἐστιν, ἡ ἐφ᾿ ἑκάτερα τοῦ λογισμοῦ αὐθαίρετος ὁρμή τε καὶ κίνησις. Διαφορά ἐστι λόγος, καθ᾿ ὃν ἡ πρὸς ἄλληλα τῶν σημαινομένων ἑτερότης σώζεσθαι πέφυκε, καὶ τοῦ πῶς εἶναι δηλωτικός. Ταυτότης ἐστὶν ἀπαραλλαξία, καθ᾿ ἣν ὁ τοῦ σημαινομένου λόγος τὸ πάντη κέκτηται μοναδικὸν, μηδενὶ τρόπῳ διαφορᾶς γνωριζόμενον.

It is proper to the enhypostatic either to be known 153with another different according to the essence in a hypostasis, by an indissoluble union; or to be naturally in individuals according to existence.

It is proper to essence to be predicated naturally in all and of all, and to shine forth. It is proper to the enousial to subsist really, and to bear upon itself the true characters of all the personal idioms.

Power is enmattered operation; operation is immaterial power. Or again, operation is the accomplishment of a natural power.

Natural will is the essential aspiration for the things constitutive according to nature. Will of inclination is the self-chosen impulse and movement of thought toward either side.

Difference is a principle according to which the otherness of the things signified toward one another is naturally preserved, and which is indicative of how [a thing] is.

Identity is invariance, according to which the principle of the thing signified possesses what is in every respect singular, known by no mode of difference.

OPUSCULUM 15 — SPIRITUAL AND DOGMATIC TOME AGAINST THE EKTHESIS

*Of the holy Maximus.

ΤΟΥ ΑΓΙΟΥ ΜΑΞΙΜΟΥ Πνευματικὸς τόμος καὶ δογματικὸς, ἀποδεικνὺς τὴν κατὰ καινοτομίαν γεγονυῖαν ἐπείσακτον ἔκθεσιν Ἡρακλείου τοῦ βασιλέως, ἐξ ὑποστολῆς Σεργίου τοῦ Κωνσταντινουπόλεως προέδρου, ἀπᾴδουσα μὲν τῶν ἱερῶν Λογίων τε καὶ Πατέρων· συνᾴδουσα δὲ τοῖς ἀνιέροις αἰρετικοῖς 156τοῖς τὴν τε σύγχυσιν ἅμα, καὶ τὴν διαίρεσιν τερατολογοῦσιν ἐπὶ τοῦ κατὰ Χριστὸν τὸν Θεὸν ἡμῶν μυστηρίου. Γραφεῖσα ἀπὸ Ῥώμης πρὸς Στέφανον τὸν ἁγιώτατον ἐπίσκοπον Δώρων, τελοῦντα ὑπὸ τὸν ἅγιον καὶ ἀποστολικὸν θρόνον τῆς ἁγίας Χριστοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ ἡμῶν πόλεως. Νυκτὶ μὲν τὸν παρόντα βίον, μυλῶνι δὲ τὴν τούτου κίνησιν, καὶ κλίνῃ τὴν ἀνάπαυσιν ἀπεικάσας ὁ Κύριος, τῶν ἐν ἑκατέροις ὄντων (μυλῶνί τε λέγω καὶ κλίνη) τὸν μὲν αἴρεσθαι, τὸν δὲ ἀφίεσθαι διωρίσατο. Καὶ αἴροντα [αἴρεσθαι] μὲν τυχὸν, τὸν ἐκείνης μὲν διὰ θεωρίας, ταύτης δὲ διὰ πράξεως ἀπολυθέντα· καὶ μήτε περιγραφῇ διαστημάτων, ἐν οἷς ὁρᾶται τῶν ὁρωμένων ἡ κίνησις, μήτε θρύψει παθῶν, ἐν οἷς ἡ ἀνάπαυσις, ἐναπομείναντα κατὰ διάθεσιν· ἀφίεσθαι δὲ, τὸν τούτοις αὐθαιρέτως ἐνισχημένον, διὰ τὸ τῆς ψυχῆς φιλήδονόν τε καὶ φιλομετέωρον. Τί δὲ κρατεῖσθαί με τὸν ἡμέτερον δοῦλον πείρᾳ πρότερον, καθάπερ ἀκοῇ τήμερον μεμαθηκότες, θεολαμπεῖς καὶ πανάριστοι, πῶς ἂν οἷς γεγράφατε συμπαθῶς εἵλεσθέ γε πρὸς ὕφεσιν οὕτως ἀχθῆναι τοῦ πτωχοῦ τε καὶ πένητος, οἱ τοσοῦτον ἀπὸ γῆς ἀρθέντες κατὰ Θεοῦ χάριν, καὶ ὑψωθέντες, ὥστε τὸν βίον τῷ λόγῳ, καὶ τὸν νοῦν τῷ Κυρίῳ τῶν τῇδε, καὶ ὑπὲρ τῶν τῇδε διᾶραι· τῷ ἀρχετύπῳ τὴν εἰκόνα προσοικειούμενοί τε καὶ ἑνίζοντες· ἢ μᾶλλον εἰπεῖν αληθέστερον, ἔνδειξιν ἐθέλοντες ὕψους ποιεῖσθαι τὴν αὐθαίρετον ὕφεσιν τῆς ὑπερτάτης ὑμῶν καλλονῆς, ὡς ἂν τῷ περιόντι καὶ ἡμᾶς ἀγάγοιτε πρὸς τὸ μέτρον τοῦ ὑπεραπείρου Θεοῦ Λόγου, κενώσει τῇ πρὸς ἡμᾶς τὸ τῆς οἰκείας δόξης ἀπεριγράφως ὕψος ἐπιδεικνύντος· ἐφ᾿ ᾧ τε μεταπλάσαι τοσοῦτον ἡμᾶς ἐπιποθέστερον, ὅσῳπερ ἂν σαρκὶ δι᾿ ἡμᾶς αὐτὸς ἑαυτὸν ἐπλαστούργησε πρὸς τὸ ταπεινότερον· φύσει γενόμενος ἄνθρωπος δίχα τροπῆς καὶ συγχύσεως, ὁ διαφερόντως φιλάνθρωπος· ἐλλείψας οὐδὲν εἰς πρόσληψιν τῶν ἡμετέρων, ἵνα μὴ καὶ ἑαυτῷ τὴν χάριν, καὶ ἡμῖν ἐλλείψῃ τὴν σωτηρίαν, οὐχ ὅλην τῇ φύσει διδοὺς, ὡς μὴ ὅλην τὴν φύσιν λαβών· ἀλλὰ κατ᾿ ἐκεῖνο μειώσας ἐκείνην, καθ᾿ ὅπερ ἂν οὐ προσείληφε ταύτην. «Τὸ γὰρ ἀπρόσληπτον, ἀθεράπευτον,» φησὶν ὁ μέγας Γρηγόριος Epist. ad Cledonium.. Πῶς γὰρ ὁ ὑπερούσιος δι᾿ ἡμᾶς σαρκωθεὶς Θεὸς Λόγος ἐκ Πνεύματος ἁγίου καὶ Μαρίας τῆς Ἀειπαρθένου καὶ Θεομήτορος προσλήψει σαρκὸς ψυχὴν ἐχούσης τὴν νοεράν τε καὶ λογικὴν, νέος τε χρηματίσας φύσει καὶ γενόμενος ἄνθρωπος, καινουργήσει τὸν πάλαι, μὴ δι᾿ ὅλου καὶ ὅλον λαβὼν, μόνης δίχα τῆς ἁμαρτίας, ἐξ ἧς ἡ παλαίωσις· καὶ δι᾿ ἣν ὁ θάνατος κατεκρίθη τῆς φύσεως, τροφὴν ἔχων τοσοῦτον ἡμᾶς, ὡς ἐκείνης γεγεύμεθα, τοῦ ξύλου πικρῶς ἐμφορηθέντες τῆς παραβάσεως;

A spiritual and dogmatic tome, showing that the intrusive Ekthesis of Heraclius the emperor, which came about by way of innovation at the suggestion of Sergius, president of Constantinople, is discordant with the sacred Oracles and the Fathers, but concordant with the unholy 156heretics who tell monstrous tales of confusion and division alike in the mystery of Christ our God. Written from Rome to Stephen, the most holy bishop of Dora, ranking under the holy and apostolic throne of the holy city of Christ our God.*

The Lord, having likened the present life to night, its movement to a mill, and its rest to a bed, determined that of those who are in each (I mean the mill and the bed) the one is taken and the other left. Matth. xxiv, 41; Luc. xvii, 34. And to be taken is perhaps the one who has been released from the former through contemplation and from the latter through practice, and who has remained, by disposition, neither in the circumscription of the intervals, in which the movement of the things that are seen is seen, nor in the softness of the passions, in which is the rest; but to be left is he who is held fast in these by self-choice, through the soul's love of pleasure and love of the lofty.

But what then? Having learned by trial before, as by hearing today, that I your servant am held fast, how could you, O God-illumined and all-excellent ones, have chosen, in what you have written, to be brought down so sympathetically to condescension toward the poor and needy man — you who have been so far lifted from the earth according to the grace of God, and exalted, as to raise up your life to reason, and your mind to the Lord of the things here, and above the things here; appropriating and making one the image to the archetype; or, to say it more truly, wishing to make the self-chosen condescension of your supreme beauty a demonstration of height, so that by its abundance you might lead us also to the measure of the more-than-infinite God the Word, who by his self-emptying toward us showed uncircumscribably the height of his own glory; to the end that he might refashion us the more longingly, by as much as he himself, for our sake, formed himself in flesh toward what is lowlier: become man by nature without change and confusion, he who is surpassingly the lover of man; leaving nothing lacking for the assumption of what is ours, lest he leave both the grace lacking to himself and the salvation lacking to us, not giving it whole to the nature, as not having taken the nature whole, but diminishing it in that respect in which he had not assumed it.

«For what is unassumed is unhealed,» says the great Gregory. For how would God the Word, the superessential, incarnate for us from the Holy Spirit and Mary the Ever-Virgin and Mother of God by the assumption of flesh having the intellectual and rational soul, having become new and come to be man by nature, renew the ancient [man], had he not taken him wholly and whole, apart from sin alone, from which comes the growing-old, and through which death was decreed against the nature, having us for its food so far as we tasted of that [sin], bitterly glutted with the tree of the transgression?

Πῶς δὲ κενωθεὶς ὁ αὐτὸς ἀναστήσει τὸν πεπτωκότα, μὴ δι᾿ ὅλου καὶ ὅλον ἐξ αὐτῆς ἄκρας συλλήμψεως ἑνώσας ἑαυτῷ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν. δίχα μόνου τοῦ ὀλισθήματος, ἐξ οὗ τὸ σύντριμμα, 157καὶ δι᾿ ὃ τὸ ἐπιτίμιον; Ταύτῃ τοι ψυχὴν νοερὰν καὶ λογικὴν μετὰ τοῦ συμφυοῦς αὐτῇ σώματος, τουτέστι τέλειον ἄνθρωπον, παντὸς μώμου χωρὶς προσλαβὼν, καὶ ἑνώσας ἑαυτῷ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν, αὐτὸς ὁ ὑπὲρ φύσιν Θεὸς, καὶ τὴν αὐτῆς φυσικὴν λαβὼν, εἶχε πάντως αὐτεξούσιον θέλησιν. Εἰ παραβάντες τὴν ἐντολὴν δίχα θελήσεως, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ θελήσεως δίχα, παρέβημεν, ἐδεόμεθα τῆς κατ᾿ αὐτὴν ἰατρείας· τῇ προσλήψει τοῦ ὁμοίου τὸ ὁμοῖον, αὐτοῦ δὴ τοῦ σαρκωθέντος Θεοῦ θεραπεύοντος· αὐτὴν δ᾿ ἂν καὶ τὴν ταύτης προσλαβὼν λογικήν τε καὶ ζωτικὴν εἶχεν ἐνέργειαν· ᾗ τὴν ἁμαρτίαν, ὡς εἴθε μὴ ποτ᾿ ἂν, λογισάμενοί τε καὶ ἐνεργήσαντες, ἐχρῄζομεν τῆς κατ᾿ αὐτὴν σωτηρίας, τῇ προσλήψει μολυσμοῦ παντὸς ταύτην ἀποκαθαίροντος, καὶ ὅλην τῇ σαρκώσει θεουργοῦντος τὴν ἡμετέραν φύσιν. Εἰ γὰρ οὐσιωδῶς ταῦτα ἔχουσαν ταύτην ἐδημιούργησε Θεὸς ὢν ὁ Λόγος, τήν τε θέλησίν φημι καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν, καὶ τὴν μὲν αὐτεξούσιον, τὴν δὲ ἐνεργοῦσαν παρέστηκε, δῆλον ὡς οὕτω τὴν φύσιν λαβὼν ἥνωσεν ἑαυτῷ τὸ καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν, ὡς ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς ταύτην ἐδημιούργησε, τουτέστι θελητικὴν φύσει καὶ ἐνεργητικήν· ἵνα μὴ τὴν φύσιν μόνον παθοῦσαν, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ καὶ τὰς αὐτῆς ἐμφύτους δυνάμεις προσείληφεν, οὐδ᾿ ἂν τὴν φύσιν προσείληφεν, ἀλλὰ τὴν οἰκονομίαν ἐφάντασεν. Ἀδύνατον γὰρ τὴν ἡμετέραν, ἢ τῶν ὄντων ἑτέρων εἶναι ποτ᾿ ἂν ἢ ὀνομάζεσθαι φύσιν, τῶν αὐτῆς ἐμφύτων δίχα δυνάμεων, αἷς οὐσιωδῶς πᾶς χαρακτηρίζεσθαι πέφυκεν. Ὅθεν οὐκ ἀνεθέλητος ἡ ἡμετέρα φύσις, ἐπεὶ μηδὲ ἀνθρώπου. Οὔτε μὴν ἀνενέργητος, ἐπεὶ μηδὲ ἄψυχος. Εἰ δὲ ἀνεθέλητος ἦν καὶ ἀνενέργητος, τὸ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς, ὥς τισι τῶν πάλαι καὶ νῦν ἔδοξεν αἱρετικῶν, οὐδὲ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς ὅλως γέγονεν ὁ ὑπὲρ ἡμᾶς, μὴ πιστούμενος τὸ ἡμέτερον τοῖς ἡμετέροις, καὶ οἷς αὐτὸς ἀρχῆθεν δημιουργήσας ἐχαρακτήρισε φυσικῶς, αὐτεξούσιον καὶ ἐνεργὸν ποιήσας κατὰ φύσιν, τὸν ἄνθρωπον. Καίτοι τούτων ἑκατέρων ἀναντιῤῥήτως προσειληφέναι πιστοῦται τὴν δύναμιν, οἷς ἄνθρωπον δι᾿ ἡμᾶς εἰσάγει τὸν ὑπερούσιον Λόγον θέλοντά τε καὶ ἐνεργοῦντα τῶν ἁγίων Εὐαγγελίων ἡ σύνταξις, πῆ μὲν βοῶσα· Καὶ περὶ τετάρτην φυλακὴν τῆς νυκτὸς ἔρχεται πρὸς αὐτοὺς περιπατῶν ἐπὶ τῆς θαλάσσης, καὶ ἤθελε παρελθεῖν αὐτούς· πῆ δέ· Καὶ ἀναστὰς ἐκεῖθεν εἱ̓ς τὰ ὅρια Τύρου καὶ Σιδῶνος, καὶ εἰσελθὼν εἰς οἰκίαν, οὐδένα ἤθελε γνῶναι, καὶ οὐκ ἠδυνήθη λαθεῖν· καὶ πάλιν· Ἐκεῖθεν ἐξελθόντες παρεπορεύοντο διὰ τῆς Γαλιλαίας, καὶ οὐκ ἤθελεν ἵνα τις γνῷ· καί· Τῇ ἐπαύριον ἤθελεν ἐλθεῖν εἰς τὴν Γαλιλαίαν.

And how would the same, emptied, raise up the fallen one, had he not united him to himself wholly and whole according to hypostasis from the very first point of conception, apart from the slip alone, 157from which comes the breaking, and on account of which the penalty?

Thus, then, having assumed the intellectual and rational soul together with the body grown together with it, that is, perfect man, without any blemish, and having united it to himself according to hypostasis, he who is God above nature, having taken also its natural [volition], had in every way a self-determining volition. If, transgressing the commandment, we transgressed by volition, and not apart from volition, we needed the healing that concerns it, God incarnate himself healing like by the assumption of like; and having assumed it, he would also have had its rational and vital operation, by which, having reasoned out and operated sin — would that we never had! — we stood in need of the salvation that concerns it, he cleansing it of every defilement by the assumption, and making divine our whole nature by the incarnation.

For if God the Word created this [nature] having these things essentially — I mean the volition and the operation — and presented the one as self-determining and the other as operating, it is clear that, having taken the nature, he united it to himself according to hypostasis just as he created it from the beginning, that is, volitive by nature and operative; so that it should not be that, had he assumed only the suffering nature but not also its innate powers, he would not even have assumed the nature, but made a phantasm of the economy. For it is impossible for ours, or for the nature of any other of the things that are, ever to be or to be named a nature apart from its innate powers, by which each is naturally characterized essentially. Hence our nature is not without volition, since then it would not even be a man's; nor indeed without operation, since it is not soulless. But if he was without volition and without operation as regards what is ours, as seemed to some of the heretics of old and of now, then he who is above us did not at all come to be as we are, not confirming what is ours by what is ours, and by the things by which he himself, having created man from the beginning, characterized him naturally, making him self-determining and operative according to nature.

And indeed the composition of the holy Gospels confirms incontrovertibly that he assumed the power of each of these, by those [passages] in which it presents the superessential Word, become man for us, as willing and operating; crying out in one place: And about the fourth watch of the night he comes to them walking upon the sea, and he willed to pass by them; Marc. vi, 48. in another: And arising from there into the borders of Tyre and Sidon, and entering into a house, he willed that no one should know, and he could not be hid; Marc. vii, 24. and again: Going out from there they passed through Galilee, and he did not will that anyone should know; Marc. ix, 29. and: On the morrow he willed to go forth into Galilee. Joan. i, 43.

Καὶ μετὰ ταῦτα πάλιν· Περιεπάτει ὁ Ἰησοῦς εἰς τὴν Γαλιλαίαν, οὐ γὰρ ἤθελε ἐν τῇ Ἰουδαίᾳ περιπατεῖν, ὅτι ἐζήτουν αὐτὸν οἱ Ἰουδαῖοι ἀποκτεῖναι· καί· Ποῦ θέλεις ἑτοιμάσωμέν σοι τὸ πάσχα φαγεῖν; καί· 160Ἔδωκαν αὐτῷ ὄξος μετὰ χολῆς μεμιγμένον, καὶ γευσάμενος οὐκ ἠθέλησε πιεῖν. Ταῦτα γὰρ, φημὶ δὴ τὸ Ἤθελε παρελθεῖν αὐτοὺς, καὶ, Ἤθελε ἐξελθεῖν, καὶ Οὐκ ἤθελε περιπατεῖν, καὶ Θέλειν φαγεῖν τὸ Πάσχα, καὶ Μὴ θέλειν τὸ ὄξος πιεῖν, τὴν καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς ἀνθρωπίνην αὐτοῦ πιστοῦται θέλησιν καὶ ἐνέργειαν. Οἷον τὸ μὲν θέλειν καὶ οὐ θέλειν παρελθεῖν, ἢ φαγεῖν, ἢ βαδίζειν, ἢ πιεῖν, προδήλως ἐστὶ θέλησις, δι᾿ ἧς φύσει θελητικὸς ὢν ἐγνωρίζετο. Τῷ δὲ γεύσασθαι καὶ φαγεῖν, καὶ ἐξελθεῖν, καὶ μεταβατικῶς πορεύεσθαι καὶ βαδίζειν, δηλαδὴ τὴν ἐνέργειαν ἐνεργητικὸς ὑπάρχων ἐδείκνυτο, καθ᾿ ὃ φύσει δι᾿ ἡμᾶς ὕστερον γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ καθ᾿ ὅπερ ὑπῆρχεν ἀνάρχως δι᾿ ἑαυτὸν φύσει Θεός. Ἐπεὶ μηδὲ πέφυκεν, ᾗ Θεὸς ἦν καὶ Θεοῦ κατὰ φύσιν Υἱὸς ὁ Λόγος, σωματικῶς ἐθέλειν τι καὶ ἐσθίειν, ἢ μεταβατικαῖς κινήσεσι τοπικῶς περιστέλλεσθαι, πανταχοῦ καὶ ὑπὲρ τὸ πᾶν ἀπειράκις ἀπείρως ὑπάρχων· οὕτω δ᾿ ἂν καὶ τὴν θείαν αὐτὸν καὶ πατρικὴν ὑπερουσίως ἔχοντα θέλησιν καὶ ἐνέργειαν παριστῶσα [ἡ Γραφὴ] διδάσκει σαφῶς· Ὥσπερ ὁ Πατὴρ ἐγείρει τοὺς νεκροὺς, καὶ ζῳοποιεῖ, οὕτω καὶ ὁ Υἱὸς οὓς θέλει ζῳοποιεῖ. Κἀνταῦθα τοίνυν, τὸ μὲν τοὺς νεκροὺς ἐθέλειν ζωοποιεῖν, τὴν τοῦ αὐτοῦ θεϊκὴν μαρτύρεται θέλησιν· αὐτὸ δὲ τὸ ζωοποιεῖν, τὴν παντουργὸν αὐτοῦ κυρίως ἐνέργειαν. Καὶ ἁπλῶς, εἴ τιπερ ἄλλο τοιοῦτο, τῆς ἐξῃρημένης τῶν ὄντων ἐξήρτηται φύσεως, εἰς τὴν ταύτης, ὡς οἷόν τε, δήλωσιν. Ἀλλὰ τούτοις μὲν αὐτὸς, ἑαυτὸν διὰ τῶν αὐτοῦ μαθητῶν κατ᾿ ἄμφω τὰς αὐτοῦ φύσεις, ἐξ ὧν καὶ ἐν αἷς συνέστηκε, θελητικὸν ὑπάρχοντα φύσει καὶ ἐνεργητικὸν παρίστησι τῆς ἡμῶν σωτηρίας ὁ Λόγος· οἱ δὲ μετ᾿ ἐκείνους ἐκείνων διάδοχοι, καὶ τῆς ἐν σαρκὶ τοῦ Λόγου θεοφανείας ἐκφάντορες, θεόκριτοί τῆς καθολικῆς Ἐκκλησίας Πατέρες, ἑκατέραν ὁμοίως τοῦ αὐτοῦ, τήν τε θείαν καὶ τὴν ἀνθρωπίνην φύσιν· οὐ μόνον δὲ, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὴν θέλησιν καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν κηρύττοντες, τάδε μεγαλοφώνως φασίν. Ὁ μὲν γὰρ τῆς ἀθανασίας ἐπώνυμος Athanas. De incarn. et Trinit., ἐν τῷ περὶ σαρκώσεως καὶ Τριάδος λόγῳ· «Καὶ ὅταν λέγῃ, Πάτερ, εἰ δυνατὸν, παρελθέτω ἀπ᾿ ἐμοῦ τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο· πλὴν μὴ τὸ ἐμὸν, ἀλλὰ τὸ σὸν γενέσθω θέλημα· καὶ πάλιν· Τὸ μὲν πνεῦμα πρόθυμον, ἡ δὲ σὰρξ ἀσθενὴς, δύο θελήματα ἐνταῦθα δείκνυσι· τὸ μὲν ἀνθρώπινον, διὰ τὴν ἀσθένειαν τῆς σαρκὸς, παραιτεῖται τὸ πάθος, τὸ δὲ Θεϊκὸν αὐτοῦ πρόθυμον.»

And after these, again: Jesus walked in Galilee, for he did not will to walk in Judaea, because the Jews sought to kill him; Joan. vii, 1. and: Where wilt thou that we prepare for thee to eat the passover? Matth. xxvi, 17. and: 160They gave him vinegar mingled with gall, and having tasted he did not will to drink. Matth. xxvii, 34.

For these — I mean He willed to pass by them, and He willed to go forth, and He did not will to walk, and to will to eat the passover, and not to will to drink the vinegar — confirm his human volition and operation, as ours is. For example, to will and not to will to pass by, or to eat, or to walk, or to drink, is plainly volition, by which he was known to be volitive by nature. But by tasting and eating, and going forth, and journeying from place to place and walking, he was plainly shown, as being operative, [to have] the operation according to which he later became man by nature for our sake, but not according to that which he was without beginning, through himself, by nature, God. For it is not natural to the Word, in that he was God and Son of God according to nature, to will anything bodily and to eat, or to be confined locally by movements from place to place, being everywhere and above the all, infinitely, infinite times over. So too [Scripture], presenting him as having superessentially the divine and paternal volition and operation, teaches clearly: As the Father raises the dead and gives life, so also the Son gives life to whom he wills. Joan. v, 21. Here, then, too, to will to give life to the dead bears witness to his divine volition; and to give life itself, to his properly all-working operation. And simply, whatever else is such depends from the nature exalted above the things that are, for the manifestation of it, so far as possible.

By these, then, the Word himself, through his own disciples, presents himself, according to both his natures, from which and in which he consists, as being volitive by nature and operative of our salvation; and those after them, their successors and the revealers of the theophany of the Word in the flesh, the God-chosen Fathers of the catholic Church, proclaiming alike each nature of the same, both the divine and the human, and not only that, but also the volition and the operation, say these things with a great voice. For the one named for immortality, in his discourse on the incarnation and the Trinity: Matth. xxvi, 39. «And when he says, Father, if it be possible, let this cup pass from me; nevertheless not mine, but thy will be done; and again, The spirit indeed is eager, but the flesh weak, he shows here two wills: the human, on account of the weakness of the flesh, refuses the passion, but his divine [will] is eager.»

Γρηγόριος δὲ ὁ τῆς Θεολογίας ἐπώνυμος ἐν τῷ περὶ 161Υἱοῦ δευτέρῳ λόγῳ· «Ἕβδομον λεγέσθω, τὸ καταβεβηκέναι ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ τὸν Υἱὸν, οὐχ ἵνα ποιῇ τὸ θέλημα τὸ ἑαυτοῦ, ἀλλὰ τοῦ πέμψαντος. Εἰ μὲν οὖν μὴ περὶ τοῦ κατεληλυθότος ταῦτα ἐλέγετο, εἴποιμεν ἂν ὡς περὶ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου τυποῦσθαι τὸν λόγον, οὐ τοῦ κατὰ τὸν Σωτῆρα νοουμένου. Τὸ γὰρ ἐκείνου θέλημα, οὐδὲ ὑπεναντίον Θεῷ θεωθὲν ὅλον, ἀλλὰ τοῦ καθ᾿ ἡμᾶς· ὡς τοῦ ἀνθρωπίνου θελήματος, οὐ πάντως ἑπομένου τῷ Θείῳ, ἀλλ᾿ ἀντιπίπτοντος ὡς τὰ πολλὰ καὶ ἀντιπαλαίοντος.» Ὁ δὲ τούτου ὁμώνυμος τῆς Νυσσαέων γενόμενος καθηγητὴς, μᾶλλον δὲ τῆς οἰκουμένης διδάσκαλος, ἐν τῷ εἰς τὸ Πάσχα λόγῳ· «Προσέρχεται ὁ λεπρὸς διεῤῥυηκὼς ἤδη καὶ ἠχρειωμένος τῷ σώματι. Πῶς γίνεται ἐπὶ τούτῳ παρὰ τοῦ Κυρίου ἡ ἴασις; Ἡ ψυχὴ θέλει, τὸ σῶμα ἅπτεται, δι᾿ ἀμφοτέρων φεύγει τὸ πάθος. Ἀπῆλθε γὰρ, φησὶν, ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ παραχρῆμα ἡ λέπρα.» Καὶ αὖθις ὁ αὐτὸς ἐν τῷ αὐτῷ λόγῳ· «Πάλιν τοὺς ἐν πολλοῖς χιλιάσι κατὰ τὴν ἔρημον αὐτῷ προσεδρεύοντας, ἀπολῦσαι μὲν νήστεις οὐ θέλει· ταῖς χερσὶ δὲ διακλᾷ τοὺς ἄρτους. Ὁρᾷς πῶς δι᾿ ἀμφοτέρων συμπαρομαρτοῦσα ἡ θεότης δημοσιεύεται, τῷ τε ἐνεργοῦντι σώματι, καὶ τῇ ὁρμῇ τοῦ ἐν τῇ ψυχῇ γινομένου θελήματος;» Ἐν δὲ τοῖς κατ᾿ Εὐνομίου τοῦ δυσσεβοῦς πονηθεῖσι δευτέρου λόγου· «Πῶς ὁ Κύριος τὸν κόσμον ἑαυτῷ καταλλάσσων, ἐπεμέριζε ψυχῇ τε καὶ σώματι τὴν παρ᾿ αὐτοῦ γενομένην τοῖς ἀνθρώποις εὐεργεσίαν, θέλων μὲν διὰ τῆς ψυχῆς, ἁπτόμενος δὲ διὰ τοῦ σώματος;» Καὶ αὖθις ὁ αὐτὸς ἐν τοῖς κατὰ Ἀπολιναρίου φησίν· «Ἐπειδὴ τοίνυν ἄλλο τὸ ἀνθρώπινον θέλημα, καὶ τὸ θεῖον ἄλλο, φθέγγεται μὲν ὡς ἐκ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου τὸ τῇ ἀσθενείᾳ τῆς φύσεως πρόσφορον, ὁ τὰ ἡμέτερα πάθη οἰκειωσάμενος· ἐπάγει δὲ τὴν δευτέραν φωνὴν, τὸ ὑψηλόν τε καὶ θεοπρεπὲς βούλημα κυρωθῆναι παρὰ τὸ ἀνθρώπινον ὑπὲρ τῆς τοῦ κόσμου σωτηρίας θέλων. Ὁ γὰρ εἰπών· Μὴ τὸ ἐμὸν, τὸ ἀνθρώπινον τῷ λόγῳ ἐσήμανε· προσθεὶς δὲ, Τὸ σὸν, ἔδειξε τὸ συναφὲς τῆς ἑαυτοῦ πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα Θεότητος , ἧς οὐδεμία θελήματός ἐστι διαφορὰ, διὰ τὴν κοινωνίαν τῆς φύσεως.»

And Gregory, named for Theology, in his second 161discourse on the Son: «Let the seventh be said: that the Son has come down from heaven, not that he might do his own will, but that of him who sent him. If, then, these things were not being said of him who came down, we should say that the word is shaped as about the man — not him who is understood in the Saviour, for his will is not even opposed to God, being wholly deified — but him who is as we are; since the human will does not wholly follow the divine, but for the most part resists and wrestles against it.»

And his namesake, who became the guide of the Nyssenes, or rather the teacher of the inhabited world, in his discourse on the Pascha: Matth. xv, 32 seqq. «The leper approaches, already wasted away and made useless in body. How does the healing come to him from the Lord? The soul wills, the body touches; through both the passion flees. For the leprosy, he says, departed from him at once.» And again the same in the same discourse: «Again, those who in many thousands waited on him in the desert he does not will to dismiss fasting; but with his hands he breaks the loaves. Seest thou how through both the Godhead, accompanying, is made public — by the body that operates, and by the impulse of the will that arises in the soul?» And in the second book of those he laboured against Eunomius the impious: «How did the Lord, reconciling the world to himself, distribute to soul and body the benefaction that came from him to men, willing through the soul, and touching through the body?» And again the same says, in his [books] against Apollinarius: Matth. xxvi, 39. «Since, then, the human will is one thing and the divine another, he who made our passions his own utters, as from the man, what is suited to the weakness of the nature; but he adds the second utterance, willing that the lofty purpose befitting God be ratified above the human for the salvation of the world. For he who said Not mine signified the human by the word; and adding But thine, he showed the conjoinedness of his Godhead with the Father, of which there is no difference of will, on account of the communion of the nature.»

Ὁ δὲ τὸν τῆς βασιλίδος καταφαιδρύνας θρόνον Ἰωάννης ὁ θεῖος, ἐν τῷ ἐπιγεγραμμένῳ λόγῳ, Πρὸς τοὺς ἀπολειφθέντας τῆς συνάξεως, καὶ τὸ ὁμοούσιον εἶναι τὸν Υἱὸν τῷ Πατρὶ 164ἀπόδειξις, οὗ ἀρχὴ, Πάλιν Ἱπποδρομίαι, καὶ πάλιν ὁ σύλλογος ἡμῖν ἐλάττων γέγονεν· «Ὁρᾷς πῶς καὶ τὴν προτέραν αὐτοῦ ἡλικίαν προανεφώνησεν; ἐρώτησον τοίνυν τὸν αἱρετικὸν, Θεὸς δειλιᾷ καὶ ἀναδύεται; καὶ ὀκνεῖ καὶ λυπεῖται; κἂν εἴπῃ, ὃ Ναὶ, ἀπόστηθι λοιπὸν, καὶ στῆσον αὐτὸν κάτω μετὰ τοῦ διαβόλου, μᾶλλον δὲ κἀκείνου κατωτέρω, οὐδὲ γὰρ ἐκεῖνος τολμήσει τοῦτο εἰπεῖν. Ἐὰν δὲ εἴπῃ. ὅτι Οὐδὲν τούτων ἄξιον Θεοῦ· εἰπὲ, ὅτι Οὐκοῦν οὐδὲ εὔχεται Θεός. Χωρὶς γὰρ τούτων, καὶ ἕτερον ἄτοπόν ἐστ᾿ ἂν τοῦ Θεοῦ τὰ ῥήματα ᾖ. Οὔτε γὰρ ἀγωνιᾷν μόνον ἐμφαίνει τὰ ῥήματα, ἀλλὰ δύο Θελήματα, ἓν μὲν Υἱοῦ, ἓν δὲ Πατρὸς, ἐναντία ἀλλήλοις. Τὸ γὰρ εἰπεῖν· Οὐχ ὡς ἐγὼ θέλω, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς σὺ, τοῦτο ἐμφαίνοντός ἐστι. Τοῦτο δὲ οὐδὲ ἐκεῖνοι ποτε συνεχώρησαν· ἀλλ᾿ ἡμῶν ἀεὶ λεγόντων τὸ Ἐγὼ καὶ ὁ Πατὴρ ἓν ἐσμὲν, ἐπὶ τῆς δυνάμεως, ἐκεῖνοι ἐπὶ τῆς θελήσεως εἰρῆσθαι τοῦτό φασι, λέγοντες, Πατρὸς καὶ Υἱοῦ μίαν εἶναι βούλησιν. Εἰ τοίνυν Πατρὸς καὶ Υἱοῦ μία βούλησίς ἐστι, πῶς φησιν ἐνταῦθα· Πλὴν οὐχ ὡς ἐγὼ θέλω, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς σύ; Ἂν μὲν γὰρ ἐπὶ τῆς θεότητος τὸ εἰρημένον ᾖ τοῦτο, ἐναντιολογία τις γίνεται, καὶ πολλὰ ἄτοπα ἐκ τούτου τίκτεται. Ἂν δὲ ἐπὶ τῆς σαρκὸς, ἔχει λόγον τὰ εἰρημένα, καὶ οὐδὲν γένοιτ᾿ ἂν ἔγκλημα. Οὐ γὰρ τὸ μὴ θέλειν ἀποθανεῖν τὴν σάρκα, κατάγνωσις. Φύσεως γάρ ἐστι τοῦτο. Αὐτὸς γὰρ τὰ τῆς φύσεως ἅπαντα χωρὶς ἁμαρτίας ἐπιδείκνυται, καὶ μετὰ πολλῆς τῆς περιουσίας, ὥστε τὰ τῶν αἱρετικῶν ἐμφράξαιστόματα. Ὅταν οὖν λέγῃ, Εἰ δυνατὸν, παρελθέτω ἀπ᾿ ἐμοῦ τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο, καὶ, Οὐχ ὡς ἐγὼ θέλω, ἀλλὰ ὡς σὺ, οὐδὲν ἕτερον δείκνυσιν, ἀλλ᾿ ἢ ὅτι σάρκα ἀληθῶς περιβέβληται φοβουμένην θάνατον. Τὸ γὰρ φοβεῖσθαι θάνατον, καὶ ἀναδύεσθαι καὶ ἀγωνιᾷν, ἐκείνης ἐστί. Νῦν μὲν οὖν αὐτὴν ἐρήμην ἀφίησι, καὶ γυμνὴν τῆς οἰκείας ἐνεργείας, ἵνα αὐτῆς δείξας τὴν ἀσθένειαν, πιστώσηται αὐτῆς καὶ τὴν φύσιν· νῦν δὲ αὐτὴν ἀποκρύπτει, ἵνα μάθῃς ὅτι οὐ ψιλὸς ἄνθρωπος ἦν. Ὥσπερ γὰρ εἰ διὰ παντὸς τὰ ἀνθρώπινα ἐδείκνυτο, τοῦτο ἂν ἐνομίσθη· οὕτως εἰ διὰ παντὸς τὰ τῆς Θεότητος ἐπετέλει, ἠπιστήθη ἂν ὁ τῆς οἰκονομίας λόγος. Διὰ τοῦτο καὶ ποικίλλει, καὶ ἀναμίγνυσι καὶ τὰ ῥήματα καὶ τὰ πράγματα, ἵνα μήτε τῇ Παύλου Σαμοσατέως, μήτε τῇ Μαρκίωνος καὶ Μανιχαίου νόσῳ καὶ μανίᾳ παράσχῃ πρόφασιν. Διὰ τοῦτο καὶ προλέγει τὸ ἐσόμενον ὡς Θεὸς, καὶ ἀναδύεται πάλιν ὡς ἄνθρωπος.»

And the divine John, who made illustrious the throne of the queen [city], in the discourse entitled Against those who stayed away from the synaxis, and a demonstration that the Son is consubstantial with the Father, 164whose beginning is Again horse-races, and again our assembly has become smaller: Joan. x, 30. «Seest thou how he also proclaimed beforehand his former age? Ask the heretic, then: Does God fear and shrink back? And does he hesitate and grieve? And if he says Yes, stand off from him henceforth, and set him down below with the devil, or rather lower even than him; for not even he will dare to say this. But if he says that none of these things is worthy of God, say: Then neither does God pray. For apart from these, another absurdity also follows, if the words are God's. For the words show not only agony, but two wills, one of the Son and one of the Father, contrary to each other. For to say Not as I will, but as thou is [the word] of one who shows this. But this not even those [heretics] ever conceded; but when we always say I and the Father are one of the power, they say this was said of the volition, saying that the wish of the Father and the Son is one. If, then, the wish of the Father and the Son is one, how does he say here: Nevertheless not as I will, but as thou? For if what is said is said of the Godhead, a contradiction arises, and many absurdities are begotten from it. But if of the flesh, what is said has reason, and there would be no charge. For it is no condemnation that the flesh does not will to die; for this is of nature. For he shows all the things of nature without sin, and with much abundance, so as to stop the mouths of the heretics. When, then, he says If it be possible, let this cup pass from me, and Not as I will, but as thou, he shows nothing else than that he truly put on flesh that fears death. For to fear death, and to shrink back, and to be in agony, is of that [flesh]. Now, then, he leaves it desolate and stripped of its own operation, so that, having shown its weakness, he might confirm its nature as well; and now he hides it, so that thou mayest learn that he was not a bare man. For just as, if he had shown human things throughout, he would have been thought this, so if he had accomplished the things of the Godhead throughout, the account of the economy would have been disbelieved. For this reason he varies and mixes both the words and the deeds, so that he may give no pretext to the disease and madness either of Paul of Samosata or of Marcion and Manichaeus. For this reason he both foretells what is to be as God, and shrinks back again as man.»

Τί δὲ καὶ ὁ τῆς Ἀλεξανδρέων καθηγητὴς Κύριλλος ἐν τῷ εἰκοστῷ τετάρτῳ τοῦ Θησαυροῦ κεφαλαίῳ παιδεύει, σαφῶς ἐπακούσωμεν· «Ὅταν οὖν φαινήται δειλιῶν τὸν θάνατον, καὶ λέγων· Εἰ δυνατὸν, παρελθέτω ἀπ᾿ ἐμοῦ τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο, ἐννόει πάλιν ὅτι δειλιῶσα τὸν θάνατον ἡ σὰρξ, ἐδιδάσκετο φορουμένη ὑπὸ τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγου μηκέτι τοῦτο πάσχειν· ἔλεγε γὰρ

165πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα· Οὐχ ὡς ἐγὼ θέλω, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς σύ. Οὐκ ἐφοβεῖτο μὲν γὰρ καθὸ Λόγος ἐστὶ καὶ Θεὸς τὸν θάνατον αὐτὸς, ἀλλ᾿ εἰς τέλος διεξάγειν οἰκονομίαν ἠπείγετο. Τοῦτο γάρ ἐστι τὸ θέλημα τοῦ Πατρός. Ἔχει δὲ καὶ τὸ μὴ θέλειν ἀποθανεῖν, διὰ τὸ παραιτεῖσθαι τὴν σάρκα τὸν θάνατον φυσικῶς.» Οὗτος δὲ τὸν μέγαν εὐαγγελιστὴν Ἰωάννην ὑπομνηματίζων φησὶν, «Ὁ αὐτὸς τὸν ἐπὶ τούτῳ λόγον ἐποιήσατο ὁ Χριστὸς, διδάσκων, ὅτι τὸ ὑπὲρ πάντων τεθνάναι θελητὸν μὲν ἔχει, διὰ τὸ βεβουλῆσθαι τὴν θείαν φύσιν· ἀνεθέλητον δὲ, διὰ τὰ ἐπὶ τῷ σταυρῷ πάθη. Καὶ τὸ ὅσον ἧκεν εἰς τὴν σάρκα παραιτουμένην τὸν θάνατον, τὰ τοιαῦτά φησιν.» Ὁ δὲ Γαβαλῶν ἐπισκοπήσας Σευηριανὸς ὁ θεσπέσιος, εἰς τὸ, Πάτερ, παρελθέτω τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο ἀπ᾿ ἐμοῦ· καὶ εἰς τὸ, Πάτερ, σῶσόν με ἀπὸ τῆς ὥρας ταύτης. «Προλαβὼν ὁ Κύριος ἐμφράττει τῶν αἱρετικῶν τὰ στόματα, ἵνα δείξῃ ὅτι τὸ πολυπαθὲς τοῦτο ἐνεδύσατο σῶμα, τὸ ἀγωνιῶν τὸν θάνατον· τὸ τρέμον τοῦ θανάτου τὴν βίαν· τὸ λυπούμενον καὶ ταραττόμενον πρὸς τὸ τοῦ βίου τέλος. Λέγει· Νῦν ἡ ψυχὴ μου τετάρακται. Περίλυπός ἐστι ἡ ψυχή μου ἕως θανάτου· οὐχ ἡ Θεότης μου· ἀπαθὲς γὰρ τὸ Θεῖον καὶ ἀτάρακτον καὶ ἀδειλίατον. Τὸ γὰρ πνεῦμα, φησὶν ὁ Κύριος, πρόθυμον· ἡ δὲ σὰρξ ἀσθενής. Ὥστε δύο θελήματα ἐμφαίνει· τὸ μὲν, θεῖον· τὸ δὲ, ανθρώπινον.» Ἀλλ᾿ οἶμαι ἱκανῶς δεδεῖχθαι τοῖς θεοφόροις τὸν περί τε δυάδος θελημάτων τοῦ αὐτοῦ λόγον, καὶ ἄλλου καὶ ἄλλου, καὶ θείου καὶ ἀνθρωπίνου θελήματος, καὶ τοῦ θελητὸν εἶναι καὶ ἀνεθέλητον τῷ Σωτῆρι τὸν θάνατον· τὸ μὲν, διὰ τὸ ὅπερ ἦν ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς· τὸ δὲ, διὰ τὸ ὅπερ ὕστερον γέγονε. Ἀρκτέον δῆτα καὶ τοῦ περὶ διαφορᾶς ἐνεργειῶν, καὶ δυάδος λόγου, κατὰ τάξιν ἡμῖν ταῖς τῶν θεηγόρων, καὶ τοῦτον ἐπισφραγίζουσι μαρτυρίαις Πατέρων. Αὐτίκα γοῦν ὁ μέγας ὁμολογητὴς καὶ διδάσκαλος Ἀμβρόσιος, ὁ Μεδιολάνων ἀρχιεπίσκοπος, ἐν τῷ πρὸς Γρατιανὸν δευτέρῳ λόγῳ, τάδε διέξεισιν· «Ἴσος οὖν ἐν τῇ τοῦ Θεοῦ μορφῇ, ἐλάττων δὲ ὁ αὐτὸς ἐν τῇ προσλήψει τῆς σαρκὸς, καὶ τῷ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου πάθει. Ποίῳ γὰρ τρόπῳ δύναται ἡ αὐτὴ ἐλάττων εἶναι καὶ φύσις* note key πῶς δὲ εἰ ἐλάττων ἐστὶν, ἡ αὐτὴ ὁμοίως ποιεῖ ἅπερ ὁ Πατὴρ ποιεῖ;

And let us hear clearly what Cyril, the guide of the Alexandrians, teaches in the twenty-fourth chapter of the Thesaurus: «When, then, he appears fearing death and saying, If it be possible, let this cup pass from me, consider again that the flesh, fearing death, was being taught, borne by God the Word, no longer to suffer this; for he was saying

165to the Father: Not as I will, but as thou. For he himself did not fear death inasmuch as he is Word and God, but he was pressing on to carry the economy through to its end; for this is the will of the Father. But he has also the not willing to die, because the flesh naturally refuses death.» And this same [Father], commenting on the great evangelist John, says: Joan. xii, 27. «Christ himself made the discourse on this, teaching that dying for all he has as willed, because the divine nature had wished it; but as unwilled, because of the passions on the cross. And so far as it came to the flesh refusing death, he says such things.» And the inspired Severian, who was bishop of Gabala, on Father, let this cup pass from me, and on Father, save me from this hour: Matth. xxvi, 38. «The Lord, anticipating, stops the mouths of the heretics, to show that he put on this much-suffering body, the body that is in agony at death, that trembles at the violence of death, that grieves and is troubled at the end of life. He says: Now is my soul troubled. My soul is exceeding sorrowful unto death: not my Godhead; for the Divine is impassible and untroubled and unafraid. For the spirit, says the Lord, is eager, but the flesh weak. So he shows two wills: the one divine, the other human.»

But I think that the account concerning the duality of the wills of the same [Christ] has been sufficiently shown by the God-bearing [Fathers]: both of one and another will, divine and human, and of death's being both willed and unwilled for the Saviour — the one on account of what he was from the beginning, the other on account of what he afterwards became. We must begin, then, also the account concerning the difference of the operations, and their duality, in order, with the testimonies of the God-speaking Fathers that set their seal on this one too.

At once, then, the great confessor and teacher Ambrose, the archbishop of Milan, in his second book to Gratian, goes through these things: «Equal, then, in the form of God, but the same less in the assumption of the flesh and in the passion of the man. For in what way can the same nature be less and equal? And how, if it is less, does the same do likewise the things which the Father does?

Ποίῳ γὰρ τρόπῳ ἡ αὐτὴ ἐνέργεια ἐκ διαφόρου ἐστὶν οὐσίας ; μὴ 168γὰρ οὕτω δύναται ἡ ἐλάττων, ὥσπερ ἡ μείζων ἐνεργεῖν; ἢ δύναται μία ἐνέργεια εἶναι, ὅπου διάφορός ἐστιν οὐσία;» Καὶ μὴν ὁ τῆς Ἱεροσολυμιτῶν ἱεράρχης Κύριλλος ὁ ἀοίδιμος, ἐν τῷ εἰς τὸ εὐαγγελικὸν ῥητὸν λόγῳ ἔνθα ὁ Κύριος τὸ ὕδωρ οἶνον ἐποίησεν Hæc de vini miraculo non exstant inter opera S. Cyrilli Hieresol., οὗ ἡ ἀρχή· «Θαῦμα καὶ τοῦτο θαῦμα ἐγενήθη. Ἐθαυματούργησεν· ἔδειξεν τὴν διπλῆν ἐνέργειαν, πάσχων μὲν ὡς ἄνθρωπος, ἐνεργῶν δὲ ὡς Θεὸς ὁ αὐτός· οὐ γὰρ ἄλλος καὶ ἄλλος, εἰ καὶ ἄλλως καὶ ἄλλως.» Οὕτω δὲ καὶ ὁ τῆς μεγάλης Ῥωμαίων ἔξαρχος Ἐκκλησίας Λέων ὁ παναλκὴς καὶ πανίερος, ἐν τῷ πρὸς τὸν ἐν ἁγίοις Φλαβιανὸν cap. 4. κατὰ Νεστορίου καὶ Εὐτυχοῦς τῶν δυσωνύμων δογματικῷ τόμῳ· «Ἐνεργεῖ γὰρ ἐν ἑκατέρᾳ μορφῇ μετὰ τῆς θατέρου κοινωνίας, ὅπερ ἴδιον ἔσχηκε· τοῦ μὲν Λόγου κατεργαζομένου τοῦθ᾿ ὅπερ ἐστὶ τοῦ Λόγου· τοῦ δὲ σώματος ἐκτελοῦντος ἅπερ ἐστὶ τοῦ σώματος· καὶ τὸ μὲν αὐτῶν, διαλάμπει τοῖς θαύμασι· τὸ δὲ, ταῖς ὕβρεσιν ὑποπέπτωκεν.» Ὁ δὲ τὸ στόμα χρυσοῦν, μᾶλλον δὲ Χριστοῦ κεκτημένος Ἰωάννης, ἐν τῷ λόγῳ εἰς τὴν Χήραν, τὴν τὰ δύο λεπτὰ προσενέγκασαν ἐν τῷ Γαζοφυλακίῳ, οὗ ἡ ἀρχή· «Ἁπλῆ μὲν ἡ τῆς νηστείας προσηγορία. Καὶ ἐν μὲν τοῖς ἄλλοις τῶν συναφθεισῶν φύσεων διάφορος ἡ ἐνέργεια τῆς ἀνθρωπότητος, καὶ τῆς θεότητος διάφορος ἡ ἰσχύς. Οἷόν τι λέγω· Κάτω κοπιᾷ, καὶ ἄνω συγκροτεῖ τὰ στοιχεῖα. Κάτω πεινᾷ, καὶ ἄνωθεν ὑετοὺς χορηγεῖ. Κάτω δειλιᾷ, καὶ ἄνω βροντᾷ. Κάτω δικαστηρίῳ παρέστηκε, καὶ ἄνωθεν ἑαυτὸν θεωρεῖ. Περὶ δὲ τὴν ἐλεημοσύνην, συντρέχει τὸ τῆς διπλῆς ἐνεργείας. Ὁ γὰρ ἄνωθεν ἐκ τῶν Χερουβὶμ βραβεύων τοῖς ἐλεήμοσιν, οὗτος πρὸ τοῦ Γαζοφυλακίου καθήμενος δοκιμάζει τοὺς φιλανθρώπους.» Ὁ δὲ αὐτὸς ἐν τῷ εἰς Θωμᾶν τὸν ἀπόστολον κατὰ Ἀρειανῶν λόγῳ· «Ταῦτα ἀκούσας, ἐκάθαρα τὴν ψυχὴν ἀπιστίας· ἀπεδυσάμην τὴν ἀμφίβολον γνώμην, ἀνέλαβον τὸ πεπεισμένον, ἡψάμην τοῦ σώματος χαίρων καὶ τρέμων. Ἐξήπλωσα μετὰ τῶν δακτύλων καὶ τῆς ψυχῆς ὄμμα, καὶ δύο λοιπὸν ἐνεργειῶν ᾐσθόμην.»

For in what way is the same operation from a different essence? 168For can the lesser operate as the greater does? Or can there be one operation where the essence is different?»

And indeed the celebrated Cyril, hierarch of the Jerusalemites, in his discourse on the Gospel text where the Lord made the water wine, whose beginning is: «A wonder, and this too came about as a wonder. He wrought a wonder; he showed the twofold operation, suffering as man, operating as God, the same; for not one and another, even if in one way and another.» So also Leo, the all-strong and all-holy exarch of the great Church of the Romans, in the dogmatic tome to Flavian among the saints, against Nestorius and Eutyches of evil name: «For he operates in each form, with the communion of the other, what is proper to it; the Word working what is of the Word, the body carrying out what is of the body; and the one of them shines with the wonders, the other has fallen under the insults.»

And John, who possessed the golden mouth, or rather Christ's, in his discourse on the Widow who brought the two mites into the Treasury, whose beginning is: «Simple is the name of fasting. And in other things the operation of the manhood of the conjoined natures is different, and the strength of the Godhead different. As I mean: below he toils, and above he holds the elements together. Below he hungers, and from above he supplies rains. Below he is afraid, and above he thunders. Below he stands at a tribunal, and from above he beholds himself. But in the matter of almsgiving, what belongs to the twofold operation runs together. For he who from above, from the Cherubim, awards prizes to the merciful, he, sitting before the Treasury, tests the lovers of man.» The same, in his discourse on Thomas the apostle, against the Arians: «Having heard these things, I cleansed my soul of unbelief; I stripped off the doubtful inclination, I took up conviction, I touched the body rejoicing and trembling. Together with my fingers I spread out the eye of my soul too, and at last I perceived two operations.»

Ἀλλὰ δὴ καὶ ὁ σοφώτατος Κύριλλος ὁ τῆς Ἀλεξανδρέων πρόεδρος, ἐν τῷ τριακοστῷ δευτέρῳ τοῦ Θησαυροῦ κεφαλαίῳ· «Οὐ γὰρ δή που μίαν εἶναι φυσικὴν τὴν ἐνέργειαν δώσομεν Θεοῦ καὶ ποιήματος· ἵνα μήτε τὸ ποιηθὲν, εἰς τὴν θείαν ἀνάγωμεν οὐσίαν· μήτε μὴν τῆς θείας φύσεως τὸ ἐξαίρετον, εἰς τὸν τοῖς γενητοῖς πρέποντα κατάγωμεν 169τόπον. Ὧν γὰρ ἡ ἐνέργεια, καὶ ἡ δύναμις ἀπαραλλάκτως μία, τούτοις ἀνάγκη καὶ τὴν τοῦ γένους Editi, τὴν τοῦ εἴδους. ἑνότητα σώζεσθαι.» Ταῦτα μὲν σίδε διαπρυσίως οἱ τῆς εὐσεβείας Πατέρες, οὓς ἐκ πολλῶν ἢ ἀπείρων, καὶ παντὸς οἷον, γραφικῶς εἰπεῖν, τοῦ τῶν μαρτύρων νέφους, κατ᾿ ἐπιδρομὴν, τὸν τοῦ γράμματος περιστελλόμενοι προκεκομίκαμεν ὄγκον, εἰς ἐναργεστάτην ἀπόδειξιν τῆς αὐτῶν περὶ τοῦ παντὸς Σωτῆρος ἀληθογνωσίας Χριστοῦ. Καθ᾿ ἣν ὁρμηθέντες, ταῖς ἁγίαις Ἐκκλησίαις πνευματοκινήτως παρέδωκαν δύο καθάπερ τοῦ αὐτοῦ καὶ ἑνὸς τὰς φύσεις, οὕτως ἰσαρίθμους ταῖς φύσεσι, τά τε τοῦ αὐτοῦ θελήματα καὶ τὰς ἐνεργείας πρεσβεύειν, καὶ ἄλλην καὶ ἄλλην, καὶ θείαν καὶ ἀνθρωπίνην θέλησιν καὶ ἐνέργειαν, εἰς δήλωσιν τῶν ἐξ ὧν, ἐν οἷς τε καὶ ἅπερ ἐστὶν ὁ αὐτὸς οὐσιώδους διαφορᾶς· τήν τε φαντασίαν ὁμοῦ καὶ τὴν σύγχυσιν, καὶ σὺν ταύταις παντελῶς τὴν διαίρεσιν ἀποσκευαζομένης, ὡς ἑνὶ καὶ τῷ αὐτῷ δι᾿ ἡμᾶς σαρκωθέντι Θεῷ Λόγῳ, ἐξ ὧν ἐστι πραγματικῶς καὶ ἀνεπιθολώτως γνωριζούσης [γνωριζομένης] καθ᾿ ἕνωσιν τὴν ἀδιάσπαστον Λιαφορᾶς γὰρ οὐσιώδους ἐπὶ τῆς καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν ἄκρας ἑνώσεως ἀσυγχύτως οὐ γνωριζομένης, αὐτῶν τε τῶν ἡνωμένων, καὶ τῶν προσόντων αὐτῷ φυσικῶς, οὐδὲ τῆς ἑνώσεως ἀσυγχύτως γνωρισθήσεται λόγος. Οἱ δὲ τῆς ἐναντίας καὶ ἀντιπάλου μοίρας, τὸ ἔμπαλιν. Ἀπολινάριος μὲν γὰρ ὁ δυσσεβὴς, οὕτως φησὶν ἐν τῷ ἐπιγεγραμμένῳ αὐτῷ λόγῳ· «Ἀπόδειξις περὶ τῆς θείας σαρκώσεως τῆς καθ᾿ ὁμοίωσιν ἀνθρώπου· καὶ οὐ μνημονεύουσιν ὅτι τὸ θέλημα τοῦτο ἴδιον εἴρηται, οὐκ ἀνθρώπου, τοῦ ἐκ γῆς, καθὼς αὐτοὶ νομίζουσιν, ἀλλὰ Θεοῦ τοῦ καταβάντος ἐξ οὐρανοῦ.» Ὁ δὲ αὐτὸς πάλιν ἀβέλτερος, «ἐν τῷ ἐπιγεγραμμένῳ· Αὐτὴν ἐπιφάνειαν τὴν ἔνσαρκον τοῦ Θεοῦ. Εἷς γὰρ ὁ Χριστὸς θεϊκῷ μόνῳ κινούμενος, καθὸ καὶ μίαν οἴδαμεν αὐτοῦ τὴν ἐνέργειαν, ἐν διαφόροις θαύμασι καὶ παθήμασι τῆς μιᾶς αὐτοῦ φύσεως προϊοῦσαν. Θεὸς γὰρ ἔνσαρκός ἐστι, καὶ πιστεύεται·» καὶ μετὰ βραχία· Καὶ τό· Πάτερ, εἰ δυνατὸν, ἀπελθέτω τὸ ποτήριον τοῦτο ἀπ᾿ ἐμοῦ· πλὴν μὴ τὸ ἐμὸν, ἀλλὰ τὸ σὸν θέλημα γενέσθω, οὐκ ἄλλου καὶ ἄλλου θελήματος ἔμφασιν ἔχει, μὴ συμβαινόντων ἀλλήλοις, ἀλλ᾿ ἑνὸς καὶ τοῦ αὐτοῦ θεϊκῶς μὲν ἐνεργουμένου, οἰκονομικῶς δὲ παραιτουμένου τὸν θάνατον· ἐπεὶ Θεὸς ἦν σαρκοφόρος ὁ τοῦτο λέγων· μηδεμίαν ἐν τῷ θέλειν ἔχων διαφοράν.»

But indeed also the most wise Cyril, president of the Alexandrians, in the thirty-second chapter of the Thesaurus: «For surely we shall not grant that the natural operation of God and of a thing made is one; lest we either bring up what was made into the divine essence, or bring down the pre-eminence of the divine nature into 169the place befitting originated things. For those whose operation and power are invariantly one must also have the oneness of the genus preserved.»

These things, then, these men proclaim piercingly, the Fathers of piety, whom, out of many, or rather countless, and out of the whole, so to speak in Scripture's words, cloud of witnesses, Hebr. xii, 1. we have brought forward cursorily, restraining the bulk of the letter, for the most evident demonstration of their true knowledge concerning Christ, the Saviour of the universe; impelled by which, they handed down to the holy Churches, moved by the Spirit, to hold that, just as the natures of the same and one are two, so equal in number to the natures are both his wills and his operations, and one and another volition and operation, divine and human, for the manifestation of the essential difference of the things from which, and in which, and which the same is; [a difference] which banishes the phantasm and the confusion together, and with them division altogether, as being made known in the one and the same God the Word, incarnate for us, [as the difference] of the things from which he really and unclouded is, according to the indissoluble union. For if the essential difference, both of the united things themselves and of what belongs to them naturally, is not made known without confusion in the utmost union according to hypostasis, neither will the principle of the union be made known without confusion.

But those of the contrary and opposing party [say] the reverse. For Apollinarius the impious speaks thus in the discourse entitled by him Demonstration concerning the divine incarnation in the likeness of man: Matth. xxvi, 39. «And they do not remember that this will is said to be proper, not to a man from the earth, as they themselves suppose, but to God who came down from heaven.» And the same, again, the fool, in [the book] entitled The incarnate manifestation itself of God: «For Christ is one, moved by the divine will alone, just as we also know his operation to be one, proceeding in different wonders and sufferings from his one nature. For he is God incarnate, and is believed so.» And after a little: «And the [words] Father, if it be possible, let this cup depart from me; nevertheless not mine, but thy will be done, do not carry the reflection of one and another will, not concurring with one another, but of one and the same, operating divinely, but by way of economy refusing death; since he who said this was God bearing flesh, having no difference in willing.»

Ὁ δὲ ἐκείνου τῆς ἀσεβείας μαθητὴς καὶ διάδοχος, καὶ φερωνύμως τῷ τῆς ἀληθείας λόγῳ πολέμιος, Πολέμων ὁ αἱρετικὸς, ἐν τῷ πρὸς τοὺς ἁγίους Πατέρας Ἀντιρρητικῷ, τάδε δυστρόφως φησίν· «Ὁ Λόγος γὰρ 172Θεὸς ἦν ὁ Χριστὸς, καὶ τελειότητι σαρκὸς ἑνωθεὶς καὶ γενόμενος ἄνθρωπος οὐ τὸν τρεπτὸν νοῦν ἑαυτῷ καταμίξας, θελήματι φυσικῷ ἐπὶ τἀναντία κινοῦμενον, ἑαυτῷ δὲ μᾶλλον γενόμενος νοῦς, ἀτρέπτῳ θελήματι θεϊκῷ πάντα πεποίηκεν. Ἀλλ᾿ οὐ τὸ μὲν θεῖον, τὸ δὲ, θεωθὲν, κατὰ τὸν Καππαδόκην Γρηγόριον Greg. Theol. orat. 2 de Filio., ἐπεὶ τρεπτόν. Τὸ γὰρ θεωθῆναι δυνηθὲν, καὶ γεωθῆναι πάντως ἠδύνατο, κατὰ τὸν πρῶτον Ἀδάμ.» Καὶ ἐν ἕκτῃ τῶν πρὸς τὸν Τιμόθεον αὐτῷ γεγραμμένων ἐπιστολῇ, τὸν αὐτῷ συνεργάτην, καὶ τῆς ἀσεβείας συλλήπτορα πονηρόν· «Ὁ δύο θελήματα λέγων Χριστοῦ, κατὰ τοὺς πάλαι καὶ νῦν φησαμένους, ἢ τὸν ἕνα δύο τινὰς εἰσάγει Χριστοὺς, ἀλλήλων οὐ φύσει μόνον, ἀλλὰ δὴ καὶ ἀπεχθείᾳ διῃρημένους· ἢ τὸν αὐτὸν ἑαυτῷ διδάσκει μαχόμενον. Ἔνθα γὰρ δυὰς, πάντως διαίρεσις.» Ἀλλὰ καὶ Θεμίστιος, ὁ τῆς μὲν Σευήρου συμμορίας ὑπασπιστὴς, τῆς δὲ τῶν Ἀγνοητῶν ἔξαρχος κακοφροσύνης, ἐν κεφαλαίῳ μεʹ τοῦ λόγου δευτέρου τῶν κατὰ τοῦ ἐπιδοθέντος τόμου Θεοδώρᾳ τῇ βασιλίδι παρὰ Θεοδοσίου τοῦ αἱρεσιάρχου, τῶν ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ κατονομασθέντων αἱρετικῶν, τάδε θεοστυγῶς· «Οὐ γὰρ ἐπεί τε καὶ ὁ ἱερὸς Ἀθανάσιος δύο θελήματα ἔφη, τὸν Χριστὸν δεικνύναι. κατὰ τὸν τοῦ πάθους καιρὸν, ἤδη καὶ δύο θελήσεις αὐτῷ περιθήσομεν· καὶ ταύτας μαχομένας ἀλλήλαις, κατὰ τοὺς σοὺς τούτους συλλογισμούς· ἀλλ᾿ εἰσόμεθα εὐσεβῶς, τὴν ὡς ἑνὸς μίαν θέλησιν τοῦ Ἐμμανουὴλ, πῆ μὲν, ἀνθρωπίνως κινεῖσθαι, πῆ δὲ θεοπρεπῶς.» Ὁ δ᾿ αὐτὸς πάλιν δυσάντητος αἱρετικὸς, ἐν κεφαλαίῳ βʹ, λόγῳ γʹ τῆς κατακολούθου τοῦ συμφυλέτου τῆς αὐτοῦ δυσσεβείας γεγονυίας αὐτῷ πραγματείας· «Μίαν φημὶ τοῦ Ἐμμανουὴλ τὴν γνῶσιν καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν, καθὰ καὶ τὴν θέλησιν. Σκόπει οὖν οὕτως· Ὁ ἄτρεπτος, ὡς Θεὸς, ὁ παντὸς πάθους ἀνώτερος, ἀνθρωποπρεπῶς κινηθεὶς κατὰ τὴν θέλησιν, παραιτεῖται τὸ πάθος· θεοπρεπῶς δὲ πάλιν πρὸς τὸ πάθος ἀναθαῤῥεῖ, καὶ διὰ σαρκὸς τούτων ἑκάτερον.» Καὶ ταῦτα μὲν οἶδε δυστρόπως. Οἱ δὲ τούτων ἐπίσης καὶ τῆς ἀληθείας ἀντίφρονες, τοὺς τῆς διαιρέσεως λέγω καθηγητὰς, πρὸς τὴν ἴσην ἐκείνοις καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν περί τε θελήματος ἑνὸς καὶ μιᾶς ἐνεργείας συνελαυνόμενοι κακοδοξίαν, οἳ καὶ τὴν ἐν σχέσει γνωμικὴν ἐπρέσβευον ἕνωσιν, τάδε περιβομβοῦσι.

And the disciple and successor of that man's impiety, and an enemy, true to his name, to the word of truth, Polemon the heretic, in his Antirrhetic against the holy Fathers, says these things perversely: «For the Word, the Christ, 172was God, and, united to the perfection of flesh and become man, not having mingled with himself the changeable mind, moved by natural will toward contraries, but rather having become mind to himself, he did all things by the unchangeable divine will. But not the one divine, the other deified, as Gregory the Cappadocian has it, since [that would be] changeable. For what could be deified could also in every way be made earthy, as the first Adam was.» And in the sixth of the letters written by him to Timothy, his fellow-worker and wicked accomplice in impiety: «He who says two wills of Christ, according to those who have said so of old and now, either brings in the one as two Christs, divided from one another not by nature only, but indeed by enmity also; or teaches that the same one is at war with himself. For where there is duality, there is in every way division.»

But Themistius too, the shield-bearer of the faction of Severus and the leader of the folly of the Agnoetae, in chapter 45 of the second book of those against the tome presented to the empress Theodora by Theodosius, the heresiarch of the heretics named after him, [says] these things, hateful to God: «For not because the sacred Athanasius too said that Christ showed two wills at the time of the passion, shall we therefore also attach two volitions to him, and these at war with one another, according to these syllogisms of thine; but we shall know piously that the one volition of Emmanuel, as of one, moves now in a human way, now in a way befitting God.» And the same ill-met heretic again, in chapter 52, book 3, of the treatise composed by him in conformity with the impiety of his fellow-tribesman: «I say that the knowledge and the operation of Emmanuel are one, as also the volition. Consider, then, thus: he who is unchangeable, as God, he who is higher than every passion, moved in a manner befitting man according to the volition, refuses the passion; but again, in a manner befitting God, takes courage toward the passion; and each of these through flesh.» And these things these men [say] perversely.

But those who are likewise opposed in mind to these and to the truth — I mean the leaders of division — being driven together into a wrong belief equal to theirs and the same, concerning one will and one operation, who also championed the union of inclination by relation, buzz these things about.

Θεόδωρος μὲν ὁ κακόφρων καὶ δύσερις, ὁ τὴν Μομψουεστινῶν ληϊσάμενος, ἐν τοῖς νΟΤΑΕ. 173ϛ τὰ θαύματα παρεξηγηθεῖσιν αὐτῷ λόγου δευτέρου· θέλω, καθαρίσθητι, πρὸς τὸν λεπρὸν εἰπὼν ὁ Σωτὴρ, ἔδειξε μίαν εἶναι τὴν θέλησιν, μίαν τὴν ἐνέργειαν, κατὰ μίαν καὶ τὴν αὐτὴν ἐξουσίαν προγομένην, οὐ λέγω φύσεως, ἀλλ᾿ εὐδοκίας, καθ᾿ ἣν ἡνώθη τῷ Θεῷ Λόγῳ ὁ κατὰ πρόγνωσιν, ἐκ σπέρματος Δαβὶδ ὕστερον γενόμενος ἄνθρωπος, ἐξ αὐτῆς μήτρας τὴν πρὸς αὐτὸν ἐνδιάθετον ἔχων οἰκείωσιν.» Ὁ δὲ τῆς ἐκείνου μανίας ὀπαδὸς καὶ διάδοχος, Νεστόριος ὁ ἀλητήριος, ἐν τῇ λεγομένῃ αὐτῷ Ἐμφανεῖ μιήσει λόγου δευτέρου τῆς κατ᾿ αὐτὴν πραγματείας τάδε κακούργως· «Ἀσυγχύτως φυλάττομεν τὰς φύσεις, οὐ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν, γνώμῃ δὲ συνημμένας, δι᾿ ὃ καὶ μίαν αὐτῶν τὴν θέλησιν, ἐνέργειάν τε καὶ δεσποτείαν ὁρῶμεν, ἀξίας ἰσότητι δεικνυμένας. Ὁ γὰρ Θεὸς Λόγος ἀναλαβὼν ὃν προώρισεν ἄνθρωπον, τῷ τῆς ἐξουσίας λόγῳ πρὸς αὐτοῦ προεκρίθη, διὰ τὴν προγνωσθεῖσαν διάθεσιν.» Ὁ δ᾿ αὐτὸς αὖθις παράφρων ἐν τῇ αὐτῇ λόγου τετάρτου· «Οὐκ ἄλλος ἦν ὁ Θεὸς Λόγος, καὶ ἄλλος ὁ ἐν ᾧ γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος. Ἓν γὰρ ἦν ἀμφοτέρων τὸ πρόσωπον ἀξίᾳ καὶ τιμῇ, προσκυνούμενον παρὰ πάσης τῆς κτίσεως, μηδενὶ τόπῳ ἢ χρόνῳ ἑτερότητι βουλῆς καὶ θελήματος διαιρούμενον. Ἀλλὰ μὴν καὶ Παῦλος ὁ Πέρσης ψευδοδιάκονος ὢν τῆς Νεστορίου μιαρωτάτης αἱρέσεως, ἐν τῷ Περὶ κρίσεως αὐτοῦ λόγῳ, τάδε σὺν ἐκείνοις φθέγγεται θεομαχῶν· «Ἐπειδὴ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ἡ τοῦ Θεοῦ λόγου πρὸς ὃν ἀνέλαβεν ἄνθρωπον οὐ γέγονεν ἕνωσις, μία φύσις οὐ γέγονεν. Εἰ δὲ μία φύσις οὐ γέγονεν, μία μονοπρόσωπος ὁ Χριστὸς οὐκ ἔστιν ὑπόστασις· οὐκοῦν κατ᾿ εὐδοκίαν ἡ ἕνωσις, βουλῆς αἱ γνώμης ταυτότητι κρατουμένη, ἵνα καὶ τὸ διάφορον τῶν φύσεων ἀσύγχυτον δείκνυται, καὶ τὸ τῆς εὐδοκίας μυστήριον μονάδι βουλήσεως διαδείκνυται» Οἷς, ὡς ὁρᾶτε, πανάριστοι, τά τε γράμματα, καὶ ψὸ τούτων διασκοποῦντες τὰ δόγματα, λίαν ἐν πᾶ-´ ιν ὁμόφωνός τε καὶ ὁμόλογος, ἡ, οὐκ οἶδ᾿ ὅπως, νῦν ιὰ τὰς ἁμαρτίας ἡμῶν ἐπεισφρήσασα κατὰ τῆς ημμωμήτου πίστεως καθέστηκεν ἕνωσις, τὸ ἓν ἐπὶ Ιστοῦ θέλημα κυροῦσα, καὶ τὴν μίαν ἐνέργειαν μούως τοῖς ἄφροσι. Τὸ μὲν ἓν θέλημα, διὰ τοῦ λέλέγειν· «Ὅθεν τοῖς ἁγίοις Πατράσιν ἐν ἅπασι, καὶ ν τούτῳ κατακολουθοῦντες, ἓν θέλημα τοῦ Κυρίου μῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ ὁμολογοῦμεν·» τὴν δὲ μίαν νέργειαν, διὰ τοῦ φάσκειν· «Ἀλλὰ γὰρ καὶ ἕπεθ́αι ταύτῃ, τουτέστι τηρήσει τῶν δύο ἐνεργειῶν, καὶ δύο πρεσβεύειν θελήματα.» Καὶ γὰρ εἰ ἕπεθα τῇ ἐνεργείᾳ τὰ θελήματα διορίζεται, πᾶσά πως νάγκη, τοῦ ἑπομένου πρὸς αὐτῆς κυρωθέντος, λέγω ἢ τοῦ ἑνὸς θελήματος, κυροῦσθαι καὶ τὸ ἡγούμενον, γαυν τὴν μίαν ἐνέργειαν. Ταύτην γὰρ ἐπειγομένη οφιστικῶς τῇ μιᾷ θελήσει συνεισαγαγεῖν, ἕπεσθαι ][ἐνεργείᾳ τὴν θέλησιν κατὰ δόξαν ἐθέσπισεν.

Theodore, the evil-minded and contentious, who plundered [the Church] of the Mopsuestians, in the second book of what he misexpounded 173on the wonders: «I will; be thou clean, the Saviour, saying this to the leper, showed that the volition is one, the operation one, brought forth according to one and the same authority — not, I say, of nature, but of good pleasure — according to which the man who, according to foreknowledge, came to be afterwards from the seed of David was united to God the Word, having from the womb itself the inward appropriation toward him.» Matth. viii, 3.

And the follower and successor of his madness, Nestorius the accursed, in what he calls the Clear Initiation, in the second book of the treatise on it, [says] these things maliciously: «We keep the natures without confusion, joined not according to essence but by inclination; on which account we see also their volition, operation and lordship as one, shown forth by equality of worth. For God the Word, having taken up the man whom he foreordained, was preferred by him in the principle of authority, on account of the foreknown disposition.» And the same madman again, in the same [treatise], book four: «God the Word was not one, and the man in whom he came to be another. For the person of both was one in worth and honour, worshipped by the whole creation, divided in no place or time by otherness of counsel and will.» Indeed Paul the Persian too, being a pseudo-deacon of the most foul heresy of Nestorius, in his discourse On Judgement, utters these things together with them, fighting against God: «Since the union of God the Word toward the man whom he took up did not come about according to essence, one nature did not come about. And if one nature did not come about, Christ is not one single-personed hypostasis. The union, then, is according to good pleasure, held fast by identity of counsel and inclination, so that both the difference of the natures may be shown unconfused, and the mystery of the good pleasure may be displayed by a monad of wish.»

With these, as you see, all-excellent ones — examining both the writings and the doctrines before them — the Ekthesis, which now, I know not how, has crept in on account of our sins against the blameless faith, has proved very much of one voice and one accord in all things, ratifying the one will in Christ, and the one operation, like the senseless. The one will, by saying: «Whence, following the holy Fathers in all things and in this too, we confess one will of our Lord Jesus Christ»; and the one operation, by asserting: «But indeed to hold two wills also follows on this, that is, on the maintaining of the two operations.» For indeed if it determines that the wills follow the operation, it is in every way somehow necessary, the consequent having been ratified by it — I mean the one will — that the antecedent also be ratified, namely the one operation. For, hastening sophistically to bring this in together with the one volition, it decreed, as it saw fit, that the volition follows the operation.

Διὸ

176καὶΠατέρας οἶδεν, οἷς ἐν τούτῳ καὶ πᾶσι κατηκλούθησεν, οὐ τοὺς ὄντως, ἀλλὰ τοὺς μηδ᾿ ὅλως ἁγίους. Οὐ γὰρ δήπου ποτ᾿ ἂν περὶ ἑνὸς θελήματος φήσειε τις Ἀθανάσιον τὸν ἀοίδιμον, διαῤῥήδην βοῶντα καθάπερ φθάσαντες παρέστημεν· «Δύο θελήματα ἐνταῦθα δείκνυσι· τὸ μὲν, ἀνθρώπινον, τὸ δὲ, θεϊκόν.» Οὐ Γρηγόριον τὸν Θεολόγον, περὶ τοῦ ἀνθρωπικοῦ τοῦ Σωτῆρος θελήματος λέγοντα· «Τὸ γὰρ ἐκείνου θέλειν, οὐδὲ ὑπεναντίον Θεῷ, θεωθὲν ὅλον.» Οὐ τὸν ὁμώνυμον αὐτῷ τῆς Νυσσαέων καθηγητήν· «Ἡ ψυχὴ θέλει, τὸ σῶμα ἅπτεται,» φάσκοντα, καί· «Τῷ ἐνεργοῦντι σώματι, καὶ τῇ ὁρμῇ τοῦ ἐν τῇ ψυχῇ γινομένου θελήματος. «Καί· «Θέλων μὲν διὰ τῆς ψυχῆς, ἁπτόμενος δὲ διὰ τοῦ σώματος,» καὶ, «Ἄλλο τὸ ἀνθρώπινον θέλημα, καὶ τὸ θεῖον ἄλλο, καὶ εἰπών· Μὴ τὸ ἐμὸν, τὸ ἀνθρώπινον τῷ λόγῳ ἐσήμανεν· προσθεὶς δὲ, Τὸ σὸν, ἔδειξε τὸ συναφὲς τῆς ἑαυτοῦ πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα θεότητος.» Οὐ τὸν Χρυσόστομον Ἰωάννην, παιδεύοντα· «Οὐ γὰρ ἀγωνιᾷν μόνον ἐμφαίνει τὰ ῥήματα, ἀλλὰ δύο θελήματα. Καὶ ἂν μὲν ἐπὶ τῆς θεότητος τὸ εἰρημένον ἦν τοῦτο, ἀντιλογία τις γίνεται· ἂν δὲ ἐπὶ τῆς σαρκὸς, ἔχει λόγον τὰ εἰρημένα. Οὐ γὰρ τὸ μὴ θέλειν ἀποθανεῖν τῆν σάρκα κατάγνωσις· φύσεως γάρ ἐστι τοῦτο. Αὐτὸς δὲ τὰ τῆς φύσεως ἅπαντα χωρὶς ἁμαρτίας ἐπιδείκνυται, καὶ μετὰ πολλῆς τῆς περιουσίας.» Οὐ Κύριλλον τὸν Ἀλεξανδρείας σοφῶς ἐκδιδάσκοντα· «Τοῦτο γὰρ ἦν τὸ θέλημα τοῦ Πατρὸς, εἰς τέλος διεξαγαγεῖν τὴν οἰκονομίαν. Ἔχει δὲ καὶ τὸ μὴ θέλειν ἀποθανεῖν, διὰ τὸ παραιτεῖσθαι τὴν σάρκα τὸν θάνατον φυσικῶς.» Καί· «Θελητὸν μὲν ἔχει τὸ ὑπὲρ πάντων τεθνάναι, διὰ τὸ βεβουλῆσθαι τὴν θείαν φύσιν· ἀνεθέλητον δὲ, διὰ τὴν σάρκα παραιτουμένην τὸν θάνατον φυσικῶς.» Οὐ Σευηριανὸν τὸν τῶν Γαβάλων πρόεδρον, μαρτυρούμενον, «Ὥστε δύο θελήματα ἐμφαίνει· τὸ μὲν θεῖον, τὸ δὲ ἀνθρώπινον.» Ἀλλ᾿ οὐδὲ περὶ μιᾶς ὁμολογίας Emend. ex sup., τῶν Μεδιολάνων ἀρχιεπίσκοπον· alioqui nullius sensus quod ita expressum. τὸν μέγαν ὁμολογητὴν Ἀμβρόσιον, ἐμφρόνως κηρύττοντα, «Ποίῳ γὰρ τρόπῳ ἡ αὐτὴ ἐνέργεια διαφόρου ἐστὶν οὐσίας Melius in VI synodo, δυνάμεως, potestatis, ut Latina Ambrosii.; Μὴ γὰρ δύναται μία ἐνέργεια εἶναι, ὅπου διάφορος οὐσία ἐστίν;» Οὐ Κύριλλον τὸν Ἱεροσολύμων, παρεγγυώμενον, «Ἔδειξε τὴν διπλὴν ἐνέργειαν, πάσχων μὲν, ὡς ἄνθρωπος, ἐνεργῶν δὲ, ὡς Θεὸς ὁ αὐτός.» Οὐ Λέοντα τὸν θεῖον, ἀποφωνοῦντά τε συνετῶς, «Ἐνεργεῖ γὰρ ἑκατέρα μορφὴ μετὰ τῆς θατέρου κοινωνίας, ὅπερ ἴδιον ἔσχηκεν.» Οὐ τὸν Χρυσόστομον αὖθις· «Καὶ ἐν μὲν τοῖς ἄλλοις, τῶν συναφθεισῶν φυσεων διάφορος ἡ ἐνέργεια, τῆς ἀνθρωπότητος καὶ τῆς θεότητος· περὶ δὲ τὴν ἐλεημοσύνην, συντρέχει τὸ τῆς διπλῆς ἐνεργείας· καὶ δύο λοιπὸν ἐνεργειῶν ᾐσθόμην.»

Therefore

176… and it knows as Fathers, whom it has followed in this and in all things, not those who are truly holy, but those who are not holy at all.
