And thus the common opinion of Christians, that it is created, is destroyed. For how is that a creature which creates from things that are not, and has the distinctive idiom of the divine nature?

6. If, because of the one will of Father and Son and Holy Spirit, their one nature too was shown by the God-possessed Fathers; and there is, according to them, O blessed ones, one will of the Word and of the flesh; then thus there will be one nature also of the Word and of the flesh. But if, in the case of the flesh and the Word, the one will does not infer the one nature, neither in the case of Father and Son and Holy Spirit will the one will infer the one nature; and our faith is empty, empty the preaching of the Fathers.

7. This one will is either elective or natural.

Ἀλλ᾿ εἰ μὲν φυσικὸν, καὶ μία φύσις ἔσται, ὡς καὶ ἤδη ἐδείχθη, τῆς σαρκὸς καὶ τοῦ Λόγου· 273εἰ δὲ προαιρετικὸν, ὥσπερ τῇ ὑποστάσει, οὕτω καὶ τῇ γνώμῃ χωρισθήσεται Πατρὸς καὶ Πνεύματος ὁ Χριστός.

ηʹ. Εἰ κατὰ τὸν μέγαν Κύριλλον, τὰ τῆς αὐτῆς ὄντα οὐσίας καὶ φύσεως, καὶ τοῦ αὐτοῦ τυγχάνουσι θελήματος, πῶς τὰ τῆς αὐτῆς μὴ ὄντα φύσεως, τοῦ αὐτοῦ εἶναι θελήματος δύνανται;

θʹ. Εἰ τὸ μὴ θέλειν ἀποθανεῖν, κατὰ τὸ χρυσοῦν στόμα Ἰωάννου, φυσικὸν ἔχει ἡ σάρξ· μετὰ πάντων δὲ τῶν φυσικῶς προσόντων αὐτῇ ἰδιωμάτων ὁ Λόγος ἥνωσεν αὐτὴν ἑαυτῷ ἀτρέπτως καθ᾿ ὑπόστασιν, πῶς τὰ φυσικῶς προσόντα αὐτῇ ἀρνούμενοι, οὐχὶ καὶ τὴν φύσιν τοῖς φυσικοῖς ἰδιώμασι συναρνοῦνται;

ιʹ. Εἰ τῷ θελήματι καὶ πρόσωπον συνεισάγεσθαι λέγουσι, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο τὸν οὐκ ὄντα φοβούμενοι φόβον, δύο θελήματα ἐπὶ Χριστοῦ λέγειν οὐκ ἀνέχονται, ἵνα μὴ καὶ δύο πρόσωπα ἐξ ἀνάγκης αὐτοῖς συνεισαχθῇ, βιασθήσονται, εἴπερ εὐσυνάρτητον τὸν τοῦ οἰκείου δόγματος λόγον δεῖξαι βούλονται, ἢ διὰ τὸ ἓν θέλημα τῆς Θεότητος, καὶ ἓν λέγειν πρόσωπον· ἐπειδὴ τῷ θελήματι κατ᾿ αὐτοὺς ἐξ ἀνάγκης καὶ πρόσωπον ἕπεται· ἢ διὰ τὰ τρία πρόσωπα, καὶ τρία θελήματα, Σαβελλίου τε τὴν συναίρεσιν καὶ Ἀρείου τὴν διαίρεσιν εἰσάγειν. Ἐπειδὴ δὲ, εὐλογημένοι, ἡ ὁμωνυμία αἴτιον πολλάκις πλάνης γίνεται, τοῦ ἀκροατοῦ πρὸς ἑτέραν σημασίαν μεταβαίνοντος, παρ᾿ ἣν ὑπέφηνεν ὁ λόγος, παρακαλῶ τὴν ὑμετέραν ἐν Χριστῷ σύνεσιν, διὰ τὴν[ εὐκρινῆ καὶ ἀσύγχυτον τῶν σημαινομένων πραγμά-] των δήλωσιν, σαφηνίσαι ὑμῖν πρὸ πάντων, εἰ ὅλως ἐστὶ θέλημα, καὶ ἐπὶ ποίων σημαινομένων ἤγουν πραγμάτων φέρεται τὸ τοιοῦτον ὄνομα· καὶ εἰ τὰ μὲν τῶν ὑπ᾿ αὐτὸ προσαγορευομένων πραγμάτων Ita in Basilio, Gregorioque Theologo de Spiritu Deo: in Athanasio περὶ προσώπων in theologia., σύνθετά εἰσι, τὰ δὲ ἁπλᾶ· καὶ ποῖα δὲ τὰ σύνθετα. Καὶ εἰ ἄρα τὰ σύνθετα, ὁμόχρονα ἔχουσι τὰ μέρη, ἢ οὐχ ὁμόχρονα· καὶ ποῖα ποίων προγενέστερα· ποῖα δὲ ποίων μεταγενέστερα. Καὶ τίς ὁ ἀποδεικτικὸς ἑκάστου μέρους καὶ ὁριστικὸς λόγος, ἵνα ἔχωμεν νοούμενον, τὸ ἓν θέλημα δέχεσθαι. Οὐ γὰρ περὶ φωνῶν ὁ λόγος τοῖς Πατράσιν, ἀλλὰ περὶ πραγμάτων. Ὅθεν φωνὰς μὲν παραχωροῦντας τούς. Πατέρας εὕρομεν· ἐννοίας δὲ, οὐδαμῶς. Πρὸς ἐπὶ] τούτοις δὲ, τί προαίρεσις, καὶ τί γνώμη· τί δὸ γνωμικὸν θέλημα, καὶ τί προαιρετικόν; Ταῦτα γὰρ πάντα συνδραμεῖν θέλουσι πρὸς τὴν τῶν θελημάτων παράστασιν.

But if natural, there will also be one nature, as has already been shown, of the flesh and the 273Word; and if elective, Christ will be separated from the Father and the Spirit, as in hypostasis, so also in inclination.

8. If, according to the great Cyril, the things which are of the same essence and nature are also of the same will, how can the things which are not of the same nature be of the same will?

9. If, according to John the golden mouth, the flesh has not willing to die as natural; and the Word united it to himself without change according to hypostasis with all the idioms belonging to it naturally; how do they, denying what belongs to it naturally, not deny together with the natural idioms the nature also?

10. If they say that a person too is introduced together with the will, and on this account, fearing a fear where there was none, they do not endure to say two wills in Christ, lest two persons also be necessarily introduced along with them, they will be forced, if indeed they wish to show the account of their own doctrine coherent, either, because of the one will of the Godhead, to say one person too, since according to them a person follows the will by necessity; or, because of the three persons, to say three wills too, and to introduce the coalescence of Sabellius and the division of Arius.

But since, O blessed ones, homonymy often becomes a cause of error, the hearer passing over to another signification than the one the word indicated, I beseech your understanding in Christ, for the sake of a well-distinguished and unconfused declaration of the things signified, to make clear to you before all things whether there is will at all, and of what significations, that is, things, such a name is predicated; and whether some of the things called by it are composite, others simple; and which are the composite. And whether, then, the composite have their parts contemporaneous or not contemporaneous; and which are prior in origin to which, and which posterior to which. And what is the demonstrative and definitive account of each part, so that we may, understanding it, accept the one will. For the account among the Fathers is not about sounds, but about things. Whence we have found the Fathers conceding sounds, but concepts not at all. And besides these, what is choice, and what inclination; what is a will of inclination, and what an elective will? For all these must concur toward the presentation of the wills.

Καὶ ὁ μὴ ταῦτα γινώσκων μετὰ ἀκριβείας, καὶ περὶ θελημάτων διηγούμενος, τυφλὸς μυωπάζων, μὴ γινώσκων μήτε τί λαλεῖ, μήτε περὶ τίνος διαβεβαιοῦται.

276ΤΟΥ ΑΥΤΟΥ Ἐκ τῶν ἐρωτηθέντων αὐτῷ παρὰ Θεοδώρου μοναχοῦ Videtur hic ipse presbyter Rhaithu, ad quem Περὶ οὐσίας καὶ φύσεως: nec forte alius ad quem Περ⟨…⟩ τῶν δύο θελημάτων. Φύσις ἐστὶ, κατὰ μὲν φιλοσόφους, ἀρχὴ κινήσεως καὶ ἠρεμίας· κατὰ δὲ τοὺς Πατέρας, εἶδος κατὰ πολλῶν καὶ διαφερόντων τῷ ἀριθμῷ, ἐν τῷ ὁποῖόν τί ἐστι, κατηγορούμενον. Οὐσία ἐστὶ, κατὰ μὲν φιλοσόφους, αὐθυπόστατον πρᾶγμα μὴ δεόμενον ἑτέρου πρὸς σύστασιν· κατὰ δὲ τοὺς Πατέρας, ἡ κατὰ πολλῶν καὶ διαφερόντων ταῖς ὑποστάσεσιν ὀντότης καῖ φυσική. Ἄτομόν ἐστιν, κατὰ μὲν φιλοσόφους ἰδιωμάτων συναγωγὴ, ὧν τὸ ἄθροισμα ἐπ᾿ ἄλλου θεωρεῖσθαι οὐ δύναται· κατὰ δὲ τοὺς Πατέρας, οἷον Πέτρος ἢ Παῦλος, ἤ τις ἕτερος τῶν καθ᾿ αὐτὰ ἰδίοις προσωπικοῖς ἰδιώμασι τῶν ἄλλων ἀνθρώπων ἀφοριζόμενος. Ὑπόστασις δέ ἐστιν, κατὰ μὲν φιλοσόφους, οὐσία μετὰ ἰδιωμάτων· κατὰ δὲ τοὺς Πατέρας, ὁ καθ᾿ ἕκαστον ἄνθρωπος, προσωπικῶς τῶν ἄλλων ἀνθρώπων ἀφοριζόμενος.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Εἰρηναίου ἐπισκόπου Λουγδούνων, μαθητοῦ τοῦ ἁγίου Ἰωάννου τοῦ ἀποστόλου καὶ εὐαγγελιστοῦ Potius Polycarpi et aliorum virorum apostolicorum., ἐκ τῶν πρὸς Δημήτριον διάκονον Βιαίνης περὶ πίστεως λόγων· οὗ ἀρχὴ, «Ζητῶν τὸν Θεὸν, ἄκουε τοῦ Δαυὶδ λέγοντος.» Θέλησις καὶ ἐνέργεια Θεοῦ ἐστιν, ἡ παντὸς χρόνου καὶ τόπου καὶ αἰῶνος, καὶ πάσης φύσεως ποιοτική τε καὶ προνοητικὴ αἰτία. Θέλησίς ἐστι τῆς νοερᾶς ψυχῆς, ὁ ἐφ᾿ ἡμῖν λόγος, ὡς αὐτεξούσιος αὐτῆς ὑπάρχουσα δύναμις. Θέλησίς ἐστι νοῦς ὀρεκτικὸς καὶ διανοητικὴ ὄρεξις πρὸς τὸ θεληθὲν ἐπινεύουσα. Κλήμεντος τοῦ Στρωματέως, ἐκ τοῦ Περὶ Προνοίας λόγου. Θέλησίς ἐστι φυσικὴ δύναμις, τοῦ κατὰ φύσιν ὄντος ὀρεκτική. Θέλησίς ἐστι φυσικὴ ὄρεξις, τῇ τοῦ λογικοῦ φύσει κατάλληλος. Θέλησίς ἐστι φυσικὴ, αὐτοκράτορος νοῦ αὐτεξούσιος κίνησις· ἢ νοῦς περὶ τι αὐθαιρέτως κινούμενος.

And he who does not know these with precision, and holds forth about wills, is blind, short-sighted, knowing neither what he says nor about what he makes assertion.

276OPUSCULUM 26 — FROM THE QUESTIONS PUT TO HIM BY THEODORE THE MONK

Of the same. From the questions put to him by Theodore the monk.

Nature is, according to the philosophers, a principle of movement and quiescence; but according to the Fathers, a species predicated of many things differing in number, in answer to «of what sort is it?» Essence is, according to the philosophers, a self-subsistent thing not needing another for its constitution; but according to the Fathers, the true being, and that natural, of many things differing in hypostases. An individual is, according to the philosophers, a gathering of idioms whose sum cannot be considered in another; but according to the Fathers, such as Peter or Paul, or any other of those who are by themselves, marked off from other men by his own personal idioms. And a hypostasis is, according to the philosophers, essence with idioms; but according to the Fathers, each man individually, marked off personally from the other men.

Of the holy Irenaeus, bishop of Lyons, disciple of the holy John the apostle and evangelist, from the discourses on faith to Demetrius, deacon of Vienne, whose beginning is: «Seeking God, hear David saying.»

«The volition and operation of God is the making and providing cause of every time and place and age, and of every nature. Volition of the intellectual soul is the principle in our power, as being its self-determining power. Volition is appetitive mind, and appetite of understanding inclining toward what is willed.»

Of Clement the Stromatist, from the discourse On Providence.

«Volition is a natural power, appetitive of what is according to nature. Volition is a natural appetite, appropriate to the nature of the rational. Natural volition is the self-determining movement of a sovereign mind; or mind moved by self-choice about something.

Αὐτεξουσιότης ἐστὶ νοῦς κατὰ φύσιν κινούμενος· ἢ νοερὰ τῆς ψυχῆς κίνησις αὐτοκρατής.

277Τοῦ ἁγίου Ἀλεξάνδρου πάπα Ἀλεξανδρείας, ἐκ τῆς πρὸς Αἴγλωνα ἐπίσκοπον Κυνοπόλεως Meminerunt Strabo et Ptolemæus. Ægypti olim urbs. κατὰ Ἀρειανῶν Ἐπιστολῆς. Θέλησίς ἐστι φυσικὴ, παντὸς νοεροῦ αὐθαίρετος δύναμις, ὡς μηδὲν κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν ἀκούσιον ἔχοντος.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Εὐσταθίου Πατριάρχου Ἀντιοχείας, ἐκ τοῦ Περὶ ψυχῆς λόγου. Θέλησίς ἐστι φυσικὴ λόγου τοῦ ἐν ἡμῖν κίνησις. Θέλησίς ἐστιν ὄρεξις λογική τε καὶ ζωτική.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Ἀθανασίου πάπα Ἀλεξανδρείας, ἐκ τοῦ Περὶ πίστεως μείζονος λόγου. Θέλησίς ἐστι ψυχῆς νοερᾶς αὐτεξούσιος κίνησις. Πρῶτον ἰδίωμα τῆς νοερᾶς ψυχῆς, καὶ τῆς οὐσίας αὐτῆς δηλωτικόν ἐστιν ἡ θέλησις. Νοῦς Κυρίου οὔπω Κύριος, ἀλλ᾿ ἢ θέλησις, ἢ βούλησις ἢ ἐνέργεια.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Γρηγορίου ἐπισκόπου Νύσσης, ἐκ τοῦ Κατὰ Ἀπολιναρίου λόγου. Θέλησίς ἐστι νοῦς τις, καὶ περί τι διάθεσις. Αὐτεξουσιότης ἐστὶν, αὐτοκρατὴς τοῦ θελήματος κίνησις. Θέλησίς ἐστι τῆς νοερᾶς ψυχῆς, τὸ κατ᾿ ἐξουσίαν αὐτὴν αἱρεῖσθαι τῶν κατὰ γνώμην, διὰ τῆς προαιρετικῆς θελήσεως. Θέλησίς ἐστι πάσης λογικῆς φύσεως, ἡ κατὰ φύσιν αὐτῆς ζωή. Ἡ στέρησις τῆς οὕτω ζωῆς, οὐσίας ἐστὶν ἀναίρεσις.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Διαδόχου ἐπισκόπου Φωτίκης, ἐκ τοῦ ἕκτου κεφαλαίου τῶν Ἀσκητικῶν. Αὐτεξουσιότης ἐστὶ, ψυχῆς λογικῆς θέλησις ἀκωλύτως γινομένη πρὸς ὅπερ ἂν βούληται.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Ἀναστασίου πατριάρχου Ἀντιοχείας. Θέλημά ἐστι, λόγος ἐν καρδίᾳ στρεφόμενος, δι᾿ ἐνεργείας καὶ μόνον φανερωθῆναι δυνάμενος. Θέλημά ἐστι βούλησις ψυχῆς [εἰ μὴ] οἴκοθεν ἀνακοπῆναι μὴ δυναμένη.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Νεμεσίου ἐπισκόπου Ἐμέσης. Θέλησίς ἐστιν ὄρεξις βουλευτικὴ τῶν ἐφ᾿ ἡμῖν καὶ δι᾿ ἡμῶν γίνεσθαι δυναμένων, καὶ ἄδηλον ἐχόντων τὸ τέλος.

Τοῦ αὐτοῦ· Κατὰ τί λογικὸς ὁ ἄνθρωπος, καὶ τί ἐνδιάθετος λόγος, καὶ τί προφορικός. Ἐνδιάθετος λόγος ἐστὶ τὸ κίνημα τῆς ψυχῆς, τὸ ἐν αὐτῷ τῷ λογιστικῷ γινόμενον, ἄνευ τινὸς ἐκφωνήσεως· ὅθεν πολλάκις σιωπῶντες, λόγον ὅλον παρ᾿ ἑαυτοῖς διεξερχόμεθα, καὶ ἐν τοῖς ὀνείροις διαλεγόμεθα. Κατὰ τοῦτο δὲ μάλιστα λογικοὶ πάντες ἐσμέν· ἀλλ᾿ οὐχ ὡς κατὰ τὸν προφορικὸν λόγον. Καὶ γὰρ οἱ ἐκ γενετῆς κωφοὶ, καὶ οἱ διὰ πάθος, ἢ καὶ νόσημα τὴν φωνὴν ἀποβαλόντες, οὐδὲν ἧττον λογικοί εἰσιν.

Self-determination is mind moved according to nature; or a sovereign intellectual movement of the soul.»

277Of the holy Alexander, pope of Alexandria, from the letter against the Arians to Aeglon, bishop of Cynopolis.

«Natural volition is the self-chosen power of every intellectual being, as having nothing involuntary in respect of essence.»

Of the holy Eustathius, patriarch of Antioch, from the discourse On the Soul.

«Volition is a natural movement of the reason within us. Volition is rational and vital appetite.»

Of the holy Athanasius, pope of Alexandria, from the greater discourse On Faith.

«Volition is a self-determining movement of an intellectual soul. The first idiom of the intellectual soul, and indicative of its essence, is volition. The mind of the Lord is not yet the Lord, but either volition, or wish, or operation.»

Of the holy Gregory, bishop of Nyssa, from the discourse Against Apollinarius.

«Volition is a certain mind, and a disposition toward something. Self-determination is a sovereign movement of the will. Volition of the intellectual soul is its choosing with authority among the things of inclination, through elective volition. Volition of every rational nature is its life according to nature. The privation of life so understood is the destruction of essence.»

Of the holy Diadochus, bishop of Photice, from the sixth chapter of the Ascetica.

«Self-determination is the volition of a rational soul coming about without hindrance toward whatever it wishes.»

Of the holy Anastasius, patriarch of Antioch.

«Will is a word turning in the heart, able to be made manifest through operation alone. Will is a wish of the soul unable to be checked [except] from within.»

Of the holy Nemesius, bishop of Emesa.

«Volition is a deliberative appetite of things in our power and able to come about through us, and having an uncertain end.»

Of the same: In what respect man is rational, and what is the inward word, and what the uttered.

«The inward word is the motion of the soul which comes about in the rational power itself, without any utterance; whence often, being silent, we go through a whole discourse within ourselves, and converse in dreams. And in respect of this above all we are all rational; but not as in respect of the uttered word. For those deaf from birth, and those who have lost their voice through some affliction or also disease, are no less rational.

Ὁ δὲ προφορικὸς λόγος ἐν τῇ φωνῇ καὶ ἐν ταῖς διαλέκτοις τὴν ἐνέργειαν ἔχει. 280Τοῦ ἁγίου Μαξίμου, ἐκ τῶν περὶ θελημάτων αὐτοῦ συλλογισμῶν· τί φυσικὸν θέλημα, καὶ τί γνωμικόν; Θέλημά ἐστι φυσικὸν, ἡ ἁπλῶς καὶ ὡσαύτως ἔχουσα τῶν κατὰ φύσιν συστατικῶν ὁρμή τε καὶ ἔφεσις. Θέλημα δὲ γνωμικὸν, ἡ τῶν καθ᾿ ἡδονὴν συναγερτικῶν ποιὰ καὶ διάφορος κίνησίς τε καὶ ὄρεξις.

Τοῦ αὐτοῦ, ἐκ τῆς πρὸς Μαρῖνον πρεσβύτερον ἐπιστολῆς, Τί θέλησις, καὶ τί προαίρεσις; Θέλημά φασιν εἶναι φυσικὸν, ἤγουν θέλησιν. Ζήτει ὄπιθεν ὁμοίως εἰς τὴν αὐτὴν ἐπιστολὴν, περὶ βουλῆς καὶ γνώμης καὶ φρονήσεως καὶ δόξης καὶ ἐξουσίας.

ΟΡΟΙ ΔΙΑΦΟΡΟΙ Τῶν ἁγίων καὶ θεοφόρων Πατέρων ἡμῶν, περὶ τῶν δύο ἐνεργειῶν τοῦ Κυρίου καὶ Θεοῦ καὶ Σωτῆρος ἡμῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Ἰουστίνου φιλοσόφου, καὶ τέλος μάρτυρος, ἐκ τοῦ πρὸς Εὐφράσιον σοφιστὴν, περὶ Προνοίας καὶ Πίστεως λόγου, οὗ ἡ ἀρχή· «Ἄχραντος ὁ Λόγος. » Εἰ σύνδρομον ἔχει Θεὸς τῇ φύσει τὴν θέλησιν, ὥσπερ καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν· ἀεὶ γὰρ φύσει θελητικὸς καὶ ἐνεργητικὸς ὑπάρχει ὁ Θεός· οὐδὲν τῶν θελητικῶν τῆς αὐτῆς αὐτῷ φυσικῆς ἔσται θελήσεως καὶ ἐνεργείας· ἐπεὶ καὶ φύσεως ἔσται πάντως τῆς αὐτῆς. Κατ᾿ ἐνέργειαν δὲ καὶ φύσιν οὐκ οἶδεν ὁ Θεὸς περιγραφήν.

Τοῦ αὐτοῦ, ἐκ τοῦ αὐτοῦ λόγου. Ἐνέργεια πάσης οὐσίας ἐστὶν, ἡ προσφυὴς αὐτῇ ποιότης. Ἐνέργεια φυσική τε καὶ συστατική ἐστιν, ἡ ὁριστικὴ διαφορὰ τῆς τοῦ δηλουμένου πράγματος φύσεως· ἧς χωρὶς, κατ᾿ οὐδὲν οὐδὲν παντελῶς ἔχει, τὴν τοῦ πῶς εἶναι διάγνωσιν. Ἐνέργεια φυσική ἐστιν, ἡ οὐσιώδης καὶ συστατικὴ πάσης οὐσίας ποιότης· ἧς τὸ ἐστερημένον, καὶ πάσης οὐσίας ἐστέρηταιἘνέργεια φυσική ἐστι, δύναμις οὐσιώδης μὴ δὲ μίαν ἑτέρου πρὸς ἕτερον ἐπιδέχεσθαι συγχωροῦσα κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν ἀλλοίωσιν. Ἐνέργεια φυσική ἐστιν, ἡ ἀμιγῆ τὴν οὐσιώδη πάντων πρὸς ἅπαντα διάκρισιν σώζουσα δύναμις.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Ἀλεξάνδρου, ἐκ τῆς πρὸς Αἴγλωνα ἐπίσκοπον Κυνοπόλεως κατὰ Ἀρειανῶν ἐπιστολῆς. Ἐνέργεια φυσική ἐστιν, ἡ πάσης οὐσίας ἔμφυτος κίνησις. Ἐνέργειά ἐστι φυσικὴ, ὁ πάσης φύσεως οὐσιώδης καὶ γνωστικὸς λόγος. Ἐνέργειά ἐστι φυσικὴ, ἡ δηλωτικὴ πάσης οὐσίας δύναμις. Γρηγορίου Νύσσης.

But the uttered word has its operation in the voice and in the languages.»

280Of the holy Maximus, from his syllogisms on wills: what is a natural will, and what a will of inclination?

«Natural will is the impulse and aspiration, holding simply and in the same way, of the things constitutive according to nature. But will of inclination is the qualified and differing movement and appetite of the things that rouse toward pleasure.»

Of the same, from the letter to Marinus the presbyter: What is volition, and what choice?

«They say that will is natural, that is, volition.» Look back likewise in the same letter, concerning counsel and inclination and prudence and opinion and authority.

OPUSCULUM 27 — VARIOUS DEFINITIONS OF THE HOLY FATHERS ON THE TWO OPERATIONS

Various definitions of our holy and God-bearing Fathers, on the two operations of our Lord and God and Saviour Jesus Christ.

Of the holy Justin, philosopher and at the last martyr, from the discourse to Euphrasius the sophist, On Providence and Faith, whose beginning is: «Undefiled is the Word.»

«If God has volition concurrent with his nature, as he has operation also (for God is always by nature volitive and operative), none of the volitive beings will be of the same natural volition and operation with him; since it would then wholly be of the same nature too. And in respect of operation and nature God knows no circumscription.»

Of the same, from the same discourse.

«The operation of every essence is the quality inherent in it. Natural and constitutive operation is the defining difference of the nature of the thing indicated; without which nothing whatever, in any respect, has the discernment of its how-being.»

«Natural operation is the essential and constitutive quality of every essence; that which is deprived of it is deprived also of all essence.»

«Natural operation is an essential power which allows no alteration whatever of one thing into another in respect of essence.»

«Natural operation is the power which preserves the unmixed essential distinction of all things from all.»

Of the holy Alexander, from the letter against the Arians to Aeglon, bishop of Cynopolis.

«Natural operation is the innate movement of every essence. Natural operation is the essential principle of every nature, and the principle of its knowledge.

Ἐνέργεια λογικῆς ψυχῆς, ἐστὶ νοῦ κίνησις καὶ 281διάνοια. Ἐνέργειά ἐστι κίνησις οὐσιώδης χαρακτηριστικὴ τῆς φύσεως, ἧς ἰδία καθέστηκε, δι᾿ ἧς γινώσκεται τῶν ἄλλων οὐσιωδῶς διαφέρουσα. Ἐνἐργειά ἐστι κίνησις δραστική. Δραστικὸν δὲ λέγεται, τὸ ἐξ ἑαυτοῦ κινούμενον. Ἐνέργειαν γὰρ ἡμεῖς εἶναί φαμεν, τὴν φυσικὴν ἑκάστης οὐσίας δύναμίν τε καὶ κίνησιν, ἧς χωρὶς οὔτε ἔστιν, οὔτε γινώσκεται φύσις. Νοερῶν γάρ ἐστι, νόησις· αἰσθητικῶν, αἴσθησις, καθ᾿ ἣν, αὗταί τε καὶ τῶν ἐκτὸς ἐφάπτονται φυσικῶς, καὶ τοῖς ἐκτὸς ὑποπίπτουσι. Πτηνῶν, πτῆσις· νηκτῶν, νῆξις· ἑρπηστικῶν, ἕρψις· βαδιστικῶν, βάδισις· βλαστῶν, βλάστησις. Καὶ περιληπτικῶς εἰπεῖν, τὸ σημαντικὸν ἁκάστης ἰδίωμα φύσεως, ἐνέργειαν λέγομεν φυσικήν. Ἧς μόνον ἐστέρηται, τὸ μὴ ὄν. Τὸ γὰρ ὃ́ν, οὐσίας τινὸς μετέχον, καὶ τῆς δηλούσης αὐτὴν φυσικῶς μεθέξει πάντως δυνάμεως. Ὅρους γὰρ τῶν οὐσιῶν, τὰς φυσικὰς αὐτῶν ἐνεργείας ὁ ἀληθὴς ἐπίσταται λόγος. Ἐνέργειά ἐστι ψυχῆς λογικῆς, οἰκεία τοῦ νοῦ διάνοιά τε καὶ κίνησις. Ἐνέργειά ἐστι ψυχῆς λογικῆς, ἡ κατὰ φύσιν αὐτῆς ζωή.

Τοῦ Χρυσοστόμου. Οὐσιώδης ἐνέργειά ἐστι Θεοῦ, τὸ μηδαμοῦ εἶναι· οὐ τῷ μὴ εἶναι, ἀλλὰ τῷ ὑπερεῖναι τόπου καὶ χρόνου καὶ φύσεως· οἷς φυσικῶς ἀρχὴ συνεπινοεῖται καὶ πέρας καὶ γένεσις· ὅς ἐστι συνοχεὺς καὶ ποιητὴς μόνῳ τῷ βούλεσθαι. Ἄνευ γὰρ φυσικῆς ἐνεργείας, οὐδενὸς φύσις ἐπίδηλος. Οἷον γὰρ ἑκάστη φύσει χαρακτῆρα γνωριστικὸν ὁ Δημιουργὸς, τὴν διακρίνουσαν αὐτὴν τῶν ἄλλων, οὐσιωδῶς ἐναπέθετο κίνησιν.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Κυρίλλου. Ἐνέργεια θεοπρεπὴς καὶ δύναμίς ἐστι, τὸ εἶναι πανταχῇ, καὶ πληροῦν μὲν ἀῤῥήτως τὸν οὐρανόν· πληροῦν δὲ αὖ πάλιν τὴν γῆν, καὶ χωρεῖν μὲν ἐν πᾶσι, χωρεῖσθαι δὲ ἐπ᾿ οὐδενός. Οὐ γὰρ περιληπτὸς, ἢ διαστήματι περιωρισμένος· ἀλλ᾿ οὐδὲ περιγραφῆς τινος εἴσω. Παθεῖν γάρ τι τοιοῦτον ὁ ἄποσός τε καὶ ἀμεγέθης καὶ ἀσώματος οὐκ ἀνέχεται.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Βασιλείου Ἐπείπερ πάσης οὐσίας ὅρος, ἡ κατὰ φύσιν αὐτῆς ἐνέργεια καθέστηκε· πίστωσις ἀνάγουσα τὸν νοῦν ἐπὶ τὴν φύσιν, ἐξ ἧς ἔχει τὴν πρόοδον, ὧν μία καὶ ἡ αὐτὴ ἐνέργεια, τούτων καὶ ἡ οὐσία μία.

Τοῦ αὐτοῦ, ἐκ τῶν κατ᾿ Εὐνομίου. Πῶς ἑτερότης οὐσίας, ἐν ᾗ ταυτότης ἐνεργείας γνωρίζεται; Παρηλλαγμένων γὰρ τῶν οὐσιῶν, παρηλλαγμένας εἶναι δεῖ καὶ τὰς ἐνεργείας. Πῶς οὐ τῆς αὐτῆς οὐσίας τὸ Πνεῦμα τῷ Πατρὶ καὶ τῷ Υἱῷ, τὸ καὶ τῆς αὐτῆς ἐνεργείας. Πῶς ἑτερότης οὐσίας εν τῇ Τριάδι, ἐν ᾗ ταυτότης ἐνεργείας εὑρίσκεται;

Natural operation is the power indicative of every essence.»

Of Gregory of Nyssa.

«The operation of a rational soul is a movement of mind and 281understanding. Operation is an essential movement characteristic of the nature whose own it is, through which that nature is known as differing essentially from the others. Operation is an effective movement; and that is called effective which is moved from itself. For we say that operation is the natural power and movement of each essence, without which a nature neither is nor is known. For of intellectual beings it is intellection; of sensitive beings, sense, according to which they both naturally touch the things outside and fall under the things outside. Of the winged, flight; of swimming things, swimming; of creeping things, creeping; of walking things, walking; of shoots, sprouting. And, to speak comprehensively, the idiom signifying each nature we call natural operation. Of this only what is not is deprived. For what is, partaking of some essence, will wholly partake also of the power that naturally manifests it. For true reason knows the natural operations of essences as their definitions. The operation of a rational soul is the proper understanding and movement of the mind. The operation of a rational soul is its life according to nature.»

Of Chrysostom.

«The essential operation of God is to be nowhere: not by not being, but by being above place and time and nature, in which beginning and end and coming-to-be are naturally co-conceived; he who is sustainer and maker by wishing alone. For without natural operation the nature of nothing is manifest. For as a characteristic mark that makes it known, the Creator essentially deposited in each nature the movement which distinguishes it from the others.»

Of the holy Cyril.

«Operation and power befitting God is to be everywhere, and to fill the heaven ineffably, and again to fill the earth, and to pass through all things, but to be contained by none. For he is not comprehensible, or bounded by extension; nor is he within any circumscription. For he who is without quantity and without magnitude and incorporeal does not admit of suffering any such thing.»

Of the holy Basil.

«Since the definition of every essence is the operation according to its nature, a confirmation leading the mind up to the nature from which it has its procession, of those whose operation is one and the same, of these the essence too is one.»

Of the same, from the books against Eunomius.

«How is there otherness of essence where identity of operation is known? For when the essences are varied, the operations too must be varied. How is the Spirit not of the same essence with the Father and the Son, being also of the same operation? How is there otherness of essence in the Trinity, in which identity of operation is found?

Εἰ γὰρ κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν ἀπαραλλάκτως, ἕξει καὶ 284κατὰ τὴν δύναμιν. Ὧν δὲ ἡ δύναμις ἴση, ἴση που πάντως καὶ ἡ ἐνέργεια· ὧν δὲ ἡ φύσις μία, τούτων ἐνέργειαι αἱ αὐταί.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Γρηγορίου τοῦ Νύσσης. Οὐκοῦν ἡ τῆς ἐνεργείας ταυτότης ἐπὶ Πατρὸς καὶ Υἱοῦ καὶ ἁγίου Πνεύματος δείκνυσι σαφῶς τὸ τῆς φύσεως ἀπαράλλακτον. Τοῦ δὲ ἐνεργοῦντος τὴν φύσιν, διὰ τῆς τῶν ἐνεργειῶν κατανοήσεως ἐπιγνῶναι δυνάμεθα. Τὸ δὲ κοινὸν τῆς φύσεως ἐνεργῶς ἀποδείκνυται, διὰ τῆς τῶν ἐνεργειῶν ταυτότητος συνιστάμενον. Ἀλλὰ Θεὸς μὲν ὁ Πατὴρ, Θεὸς δὲ ὁ Υἱὸς, Θεὸς δὲ τὸ Πνεῦμα τὸ ἅγιον· εἷς δὲ ἐν τῷ αὐτῷ κηρύγματι Θεὸς, διὰ τὸ μήτε φύσεως, μήτε ἐνεργείας θεωρεῖσθαί τινα διαφορὰν ἐν Θεότητι. Εἰ οὖν καὶ ἐνέργεια καὶ δύναμις μία, πῶς ἐστιν ἑτερότητα φύσεως ὑπονοῆσαι, ἐν οἷς οὐδεμίαν κατὰ τὴν δύναμίν τε καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν διαφορὰν ἐξευρίσκομεν Πᾶσα ἀνάγκη κατάλληλον εἶναι καὶ οἰκείαν ὁμολογεῖν τῇ φύσει καὶ τὴν προαίρεσιν· καὶ εἰ ἀνομοίως ἔχοιεν κατὰ τὴν φύσιν, ἀνόμοια ἀεὶ εἶναι καὶ τὰ θελήματα. Τῆς δὲ δυνάμεως ἐφ᾿ ἑκατέρων ἱκανῶς ἐχούσης, οὐδέτερον ἀτονήσει πρὸς τὴν ἐκπλήρωσιν τοῦ ἰδίου θελήματος. Οὐκοῦν ἐὰν ἴδωμεν διαφερούσας ἀλλήλων τὰς ἐνεργείας τὰς παρὰ τοῦ Πατρός τε καὶ τοῦ ἁγίου Πνεύματος ἐνεργουμένας, διαφόρους εἶναι καὶ τὰς ἐνεργούσας φύσεις ἐκ τῆς ἑτερότητος τῶν ἐνεργειῶν στοχαζόμεθα. Οὐ γὰρ ἐνδέχεται τὰ διεστῶτα κατὰ τὸν τῆς φύσεως λόγον, πρὸς τὸ τῶν ἐνεργειῶν εἶδος συνενεχθῆναι, οὔτε ψύχει τὸ πῦρ, οὔτε θερμαίνει ὁ κρύσταλλος. Ἀλλὰ τῇ τῶν φύσεων ἑτερότητι, συνδιαχωρίζονται ἀπ᾿ ἀλλήλων καὶ αἱ παρὰ τούτων ἐνέργειαι. Κυρίλλου. Ἐνεργήσει γὰρ ὁμοίως, τὰ τὴν αὐτὴν ἀλλήλοις λαχόντα φύσιν. Οἷς δὲ ὁ τοῦ πῶς εἶναι λόγος ἐξηλλαγμένος, τούτοις ἂν εἴη, καὶ ὁ τῆς ἐφ᾿ ἅπασιν ἐνεργείας λόγος, οὐχ ὁ αὐτός. Τὰ τὴν αὐτὴν ἐνέργειαν ἔχοντα, καὶ ταῖς αὐταῖς δυνάμεσι φυσικαῖς ἀποκεχρημένα, τῆς αὐτῆς εἶναι πάντως οὐσίας ἀνάγκη. Οὐδὲν γὰρ τῶν ὄντων πρὸς τὸ ἑτερογενὲς καὶ ἑτεροούσιον, τὰς αὐτὰς ἀπαραλλάκτως φορέσει δυνάμεις τε καὶ ἐνεργείας. Τὰ γὰρ τῆς αὐτῆς ἐνεργείας ὄντα, καὶ τῆς αὐτῆς οὐσίας ὁμολόγηται. Τὰ ἑτεροφυῆ καὶ ἑτερογενῆ τὴν αὐτὴν ἐνέργειαν ἔχειν, οὐκ ἄν τις δῴη σωφρονῶν. Οὐ γὰρ ἂν ὕδατι πῦρ ἕν τι καὶ ταυτὸν ἐνεργήσειεν· ἀλλ᾿ ὥσπερ ἀπεσχοινισμένον ἔχουσι τῆς οὐσίας καὶ τῆς ποιότητος τὸν λόγον, οὕτω καὶ διάφορον συναποδώσουσι τὴν ἐνέργειαν. Οἷς γὰρ ἡ ἐνέργεια καὶ ἡ δύναμις ἀπαραλλάκτως μία, τούτοις ἀνάγκη καὶ τὴν τοῦ εἴδους κοινότητα σώζεσθαι. Φιλεῖ γὰρ τὰ τὴν αὐτὴν ἐνέργειαν ἔχοντα, τῆς αὐτῆς ὑπάρχειν καὶ οὐσίας, καὶ τὸ ἑτεροφυὲς ἐν τούτοις ἀπίθανον.

For if it holds invariantly in respect of essence, it will hold so in respect of 284power too. And of things whose power is equal, the operation too is surely wholly equal; and of things whose nature is one, the operations are the same.»

Of the holy Gregory of Nyssa.

«Therefore the identity of operation in Father and Son and Holy Spirit shows clearly the invariance of the nature. And we can come to know the nature of the one operating through the observation of the operations. And the commonness of the nature is shown in act, being established through the identity of the operations. But God is the Father, God the Son, God the Holy Spirit; yet one God in the same preaching, because no difference either of nature or of operation is considered in the Godhead. If then both operation and power are one, how is it possible to suspect otherness of nature in those in whom we find no difference in respect of power and operation?»

«It is wholly necessary to confess that choice too is appropriate and proper to the nature; and that if they were unlike in nature, the wills too would always be unlike. But the power being sufficient in each of the two, neither will fail toward the fulfilment of its own will. Therefore, if we should see the operations performed by the Father and the Holy Spirit differing from one another, we conjecture from the otherness of the operations that the operating natures too are different. For it is not possible that things which stand apart in the principle of nature should be brought together toward the form of the operations: neither does fire chill, nor ice warm; but by the otherness of the natures the operations from them too are separated together from one another.»

Of Cyril.

«For things which have obtained the same nature with one another will operate alike. And for those whose principle of how-being is altered, for these the principle of their operation in all things would not be the same either. Things which have the same operation and make use of the same natural powers must in every case be of the same essence. For none of the things that are will bear, toward what is of other genus and of other essence, the same powers and operations invariantly. For things which are of the same operation are confessed to be also of the same essence. That things of other nature and of other genus have the same operation, no one of sound mind would grant. For fire would not operate one and the same thing as water; but just as they have the principle of essence and of quality severed, so too they will render, along with it, a different operation. For of those whose operation and power is invariantly one, of these the commonness of the species must also be preserved. For things having the same operation are wont to be also of the same essence, and otherness of nature in them is implausible.

Οὐ γὰρ δήπου μίαν εἶναι φυσικὴν ἐνέργειαν δώσομεν Θεοῦ καὶ ποιήματος, ἵνα μήτε τὸ ποιηθὲν εἰς τὴν θείαν ἀναγάγωμεν οὐσίαν, μήτε μὴν τῆς θείας φύσεως τὸ ἐξαίρετον, εἰς τὸν τοῖς γενητοῖς 285πρέποντα καταγάγωμεν τρόπον. Τὰ γὰρ μίαν ἔχοντα τὴν ἐνέργειαν, ἕνα καὶ τὸν αὐτὸν τοῦ πῶς εἶναι φορέσει λόγον.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Ἀμβροσίου ἐπισκόπου Μεδιολάνων, ἐκ τοῦ πρὸς Γρατιανὸν τὸν βασιλέα δευτέρου λόγου. Πῶς γὰρ ἡ αὐτὴ ἐνέργεια, ἐκ διαφόρου οὐσίας ἐστί; Μὴ γὰρ οὕτως ἡ ἥττων ἐνεργεῖ, ὥσπερ ἡ μείζων ἐνεργεῖ. Ἔνθα γὰρ διάφορος οὐσία ἐστὶν, ἀδύνατον μίαν ἐνέργειαν ὑπάρχειν.

Τοῦ ἁγίου Βασιλείου, ἐκ τῶν κατὰ Εὐνομίου συλλογισμῶν. Ἕτερόν ἐστιν ὁ ἐνεργήσας, καὶ ἕτερον ἡ ἐνέργεια, καὶ ἕτερον τὸ ἐνέργημα, καὶ ταῦτα πλεῖστον ἀλλήλων διέστηκεν. Ὁ ἐνεργῶν γάρ ἐστιν ὁ πρὸς τὸ ἐνεργῆσαί τι κινούμενος. Ἐνέργεια δὲ, οἱονεὶ κίνησις ἡ ἐνεργητικὴ, καὶ αὐτὴ ἡ ὁρμὴ, ὡς ἄν τις εἴποι τῆς προαιρέσεως, ἡ πρὸς τὸ ἐνεργῆσαί τι συντεινομένη καὶ νεύουσα, καὶ ἅμα τῷ ἑλέσθαι πεπαυμένη καὶ λήγουσα, καὶ θᾶττον κινηθεῖσα καὶ παραδραμοῦσα. Ἐνεργήματα δὲ, τὰ ἐκ ταύτης ἀποτελούμενά τε καὶ ὑφιστάμενα.

For we shall surely not grant one natural operation of God and of a thing made, lest we either bring up the thing made into the divine essence, or bring down the distinctive prerogative of the divine nature to the 285mode befitting things originated. For things which have the one operation will bear one and the same principle of how-being.»

Of the holy Ambrose, bishop of Milan, from the second book to Gratian the emperor.

«For how is the same operation from a different essence? For the lesser does not operate so, as the greater operates. For where there is a different essence, it is impossible that there be one operation.»

Of the holy Basil, from the syllogisms against Eunomius.

«One thing is he who has operated, another the operation, and another the effect of operation; and these stand very far apart from one another. For the one operating is he who is moved to operate something. And operation is, as it were, the operative movement, and the very impulse, as one might say, of choice, straining and inclining toward operating something, and ceasing and ending at the same time as it has chosen, both moved more swiftly and running past. And effects of operation are the things accomplished and subsisting from it.»

On this text

DRAFT (Wilson to read). The theological and polemical opuscula of Maximus the Confessor, in the edition Combefis made in 1675, as Migne prints it (PG 91.9–286): twenty-seven pieces, numbered in the modern order. They are the working documents of the Monothelite controversy: letters and dogmatic tomes to Marinus the presbyter and to Marinus the deacon, to George the abbot, to Nicander, to the monks and abbots of Sicily, and to Theodore of Mazarium; the long tome against the Ekthesis of Heraclius (Opusculum 15); the discussions of the two wills and the two operations of the one Christ (Opuscula 16 to 20); the definitions of nature, essence, hypostasis and person, with their scholia; and the florilegia of the Fathers that close the collection (Opuscula 23, 26, 27). On the text. The Greek is our own OCR of Migne's plates; every doubtful reading was checked against the page image, and three over-inked pages were transcribed by eye. Two pages of our scan are clipped at the gutter, so a few line-initial letters there are read from the sense. Opusculum 12 survives only in Anastasius's Latin, which Migne prints without a Greek column, and it is translated from that Latin. Where Migne's own type is wrong the English follows the sense and the printed form is listed with the cruces; Combefis's foot-of-page notes and variant readings are recovered for every page: those keyed to a Greek word stand in the Greek, his Scripture references stand in the English where a translator's note gives the same reference, and the rest, with his longer notes and his monitum in a working translation, are listed with the cruces. English. A complete English translation exists: Kevin M. Clarke, The Opuscula and the Dispute with Pyrrhus (Fathers of the Church, Catholic University of America Press, 2026), and earlier partial ones (Louth, Maximus the Confessor, 1996, which has Opuscula 3 and 7); readers who have them should keep them, and this is a new translation, not the first.
